# Broken State — State Map Prototype

A browser prototype of the design doc, built to answer one question: *is running a hub, sending teams across a dangerous state and dealing with other groups fun?* Scavenging and hub building are abstracted; fights play out as XCOM-style tactical battles.

## Run it

```bash
node server.cjs
```

Then open http://localhost:5173. No install step and no dependencies; only Node.js is needed.

Headless tools (no browser):

```bash
node tools/simtest.js 120 42 8
```

Runs 120 in-game days on seed 42 with 8 AI groups and prints a summary (for balance checks). Add `size=small`, `size=large` or `size=huge` for another world size (Normal by default); `autotest.js`, `timelapsetest.js` and `maptest.js` take the same argument, `balancesim.js` takes `--size huge`.

```bash
node tools/cardcount.js
```

Counts which event cards fire across five 90-day runs, then lists the hub events (`src/sim/hubevents.js`) by id, recruit cards by source and warnings before desertion.

```bash
node tools/pacing.js 365 all
node tools/pacing.js log 120 balanced 3
```

Pacing and decision quality (findings in `review/pacing.md`; or `node tools/pacing.js [days] [cautious|balanced|aggressive|all] [seeds...]`, seeds 1–14 by default, about 8 minutes for 42 full years): plays autopilot campaigns and catches every card as it is added, whatever module adds it (the module, the hour, its choices and the autopilot's answer). Reports cards per in-game day (mean, p90, max, per 30-day bucket; all cards and decisions only), cards and clock stops per real minute at 1× and 3× (an hour is 3 s at 1×; every card pauses the clock), other pausing screens (search, trade, battle), bursts (most cards in 6 and 24 hours), quiet stretches, crises stacking (raid, horde at the walls, illness, a team in trouble within 3 days), team cards against base cards, the low-stakes share and the rate without it, big moments from the log next to the cards that warn of them (raids, hordes at the walls, groups turning hostile, hives, short rations), the mix by module, the top 25 cards (repeats within a week, the autopilot's answers) and the decisions with one answer 90%+ of the time. Fails on a crash or a card with no choice that can be taken; flags duplicate cards and cards answered after their subject was gone. `log` prints one run's full log day by day (the log panel keeps only 200 lines).

```bash
node tools/traytest.js 60 1 2 3
```

Pause rules, the card tray and 5× (`src/sim/cardrules.js`, `src/ui/pacing.js`; or `node tools/traytest.js [days=60] [seeds…=1 2 3]`, about 2–3 minutes): the switches and their defaults, keys 1–5; each known card in its kind and with its default answer (never greyed out, never one that needs the map, never a fight; Orders → Hold here, Storm → Shelter, a perk → Decide later), auto-answers with their log line, a choice that throws or a stale card dropped harmlessly; a team holding while its card waits; a 6-day browser-like loop with every switch off (cards wait, answer themselves within 8 hours, nothing stays stuck); 3× and 5× dropping to 1×, deaths, first contacts, groups turning hostile and hordes near a base stopping the clock; every card of the autopilot runs with a kind and a safe default, and the clock stops per day before (every card) and after (the default rules); the time one in-game hour takes on a Huge map.

```bash
node tools/combattest.js 300 5
node tools/combattest.js 300 5 weapons
```

Runs thousands of tactical battles with the AI playing both sides and reports win rates per matchup. The last six matchups put a hive's special dead among 12 of the dead (4 runners, 2 brutes, 3 spitters, a screamer, 3 crawlers, one of each elite: `zombietypes.js`). With `weapons`, the weapon balance instead: teams of three (Shooting 5–11) each armed with one weapon (knife, baton, bat, crowbar, machete, nail bat, fire axe, sledgehammer, scrap crossbow, 9mm, .357 revolver, compound bow, hunting crossbow, shotgun, hunting rifle, lever-action rifle, police carbine, assault rifle) against 4 raiders (power 5) and 12 of the dead (power 2), and pistols with two Molotovs / pipe bombs / flares / smoke grenades each against 16 of the dead.

```bash
node tools/sizetest.js 10 3
```

Battle map sizes by context (`src/sim/battlesize.js`; or `node tools/sizetest.js [battles per matchup=10] [seed=3] [--quick]`, about 10 s): the size table (Small 24 × 18, Medium 32 × 24, Large 40 × 30, Extra-large 48 × 34) and what maps to each (sites by location type, fights in the open by the people and the dead on the map and the terrain, a hive, a group's base by its people, your hub and outposts by their buildings); every context built twice the same, valid (everyone on a free walkable cell your people can reach, nobody out of reach but one of the dead shut in a locked room), your people in the first 5 columns and the enemy 14–21 tiles off (an assaulted base's defenders behind their wall), cover within 3 tiles of most of the open ground, the first shot or blow by turn 3 (the AI on both sides); a raid with more rooms, containers and dead on a bigger site; the anti-slog ends (two far-off unaware stragglers: the player is told and can call it, the AI ends it; a raider who runs out of sight gets away). Then battles by size class played by the AI on both sides: turns, the share past the 60-turn limit (fails over 5%), ends called "clear enough", the enemy turn's time (fails unless 99% are under 40 ms).

```bash
node tools/loottest.js
```

Loot audit (`src/sim/loot.js`; or `node tools/loottest.js --quiet` for the checks only): every item has at least one source (site gear lists, small finds per site and per raid container, books, enemy bodies and their pockets, the dead's bags, group and merchant stock, travellers, recruits, crafting, road cards, blueprints); everything a site type (`SITE_TAGS`) or raid area (`AREA_TAGS`) can hold shares a theme tag with it (`ITEM_TAGS`: no military radio in a university lab, a hunting lodge has rifles, bows, camo and game calls, a police station batons, riot gear, cuffs and a carbine…); every item has a trade category, a weight, a hidden value and a description; 28+ valuables in every valuable family, light for their worth (6+ value a kg) and good for nothing but trade; every weapon has battle numbers, bows are silent and shoot arrows (never ammo), every throwable is in the throw table; the packs (sling bag 3 slots, school backpack 6, assault pack 8, hiking pack 10, frame rucksack 14); the small-finds rolls only give what their table holds, about one in ten search hours at full loot and fewer at a picked-over site; about 8% of the dead wear a bag with 1–3 things inside; a raider's pockets hold something one time in five. Then a table of every item: category, worth tier, kg and where it comes from.

```bash
node tools/retreattest.js 40 7 all
```

Sounding the retreat (`combat.js` `soundRetreat`; or `node tools/retreattest.js [battles per matchup] [seed] [cautious|balanced|aggressive|all]`, 0 battles for the checks only): offered on the player's turn of a battle with an evac strip, refused in the enemy turn, with no way out and in a raid, the strip read through `inEvac` / `evacAt`; escape odds by distance, each enemy next to them (−25), a shooter in sight (−6), wounds, armor, Scouting, night and carrying, and the chance a caught unit goes down; the plan has the one next to a downed teammate carry them and can leave them; sounding it (the one on the strip always gets out, a stabilized teammate carried out lives, a dropped one is lost, a wounded unit next to the dead is often caught and sometimes dragged down, the outcome reaches the handler as "retreat"); a downed teammate on the strip goes with the last one out by the per-unit Retreat; the AI retreats against six elite soldiers, not against four of the dead, Cautious before Aggressive with one down and one hurt, Aggressive's numbers for a base defence, never where there is no way out; the same battle plays out the same; the panel's confirm (the odds, Leave / Carry, Esc, Shift+R, Space does nothing, Enter gives the order, the result's summary). Then the measurement: nine matchups (even raiders, an enemy ambush, 3 vs 4 raiders at power 5, soldiers at 7, 3 vs 6 raiders at 6, 12 and 16 of the dead, mixed, a hive with its Broodmother) on maps with an evac strip, each battle played twice from the same start (same team, map and dice), once with the AI never retreating and once in the given style; per matchup and style the wins, deaths and team wipes both ways, how often and on which turn the AI retreated, and how many of its retreats were from a fight the other run won. Fails if a battle it did not retreat from plays differently, if the retreat costs lives or wipes, if over a quarter of its retreats are from fights it would have won, or if it gives up more than 5% / 3% / 2% (Cautious / Balanced / Aggressive) of the wins. Seed 7, 40 a matchup: deaths 411 → 104 (Cautious), 414 → 182 (Balanced), 403 → 251 (Aggressive); team wipes 79 → 2, 75 → 3, 76 → 10; wins 218 → 205, 215 → 209, 218 → 217. In autopilot campaigns the only battles it plays on the map with a way out are hive assaults: over 16 three-year runs (Balanced and Aggressive, seeds 1–8; `state.autoRetreat = false` switches the AI's retreat off for a comparison) it stormed 36 hives, won every one and never retreated, and the runs ended identically with and without it.

The same tool checks the retreat by the numbers (`src/sim/breakoff.js`): the four policies; the numbers' escape check is the tactical formula (the same odds and chance to go down as `escapeOdds` in 40 placed battles), with the car (+10), bikes (+5), a stealthy stance (+10), night, carrying and an enemy on them; the policies' rules (`wantsOut`); 3000 model fights per policy: Never never breaks off, a fight not broken off plays out exactly as with Never and a broken-off one took the same wounds up to its round, the more cautious the policy the more it pulls out, If losing pulls out of a fight it would have won at most a quarter of the time, no policy costs more deaths than fighting it out (also against 16–40 of the dead), and they give up wins; 260 real fights by a team (`autoResolveCombat`): no loot on a retreat, the enemy loses part of its people, everyone is home, dead or a prisoner, the rounds not fought keep their ammo, the downed carried out come home barely alive, the left behind are taken or killed by a group's people and lost to the dead, and the carried pull through at 50% + 2% a point of Medicine; the conflict card (the fight choice carries its fight, the four policies with Never marked for a player's team, the forecast without touching the game's dice, choosing If losing, At first blood breaking off a real "Fight back" with nothing looted, a won fight looted as before, the group's losses) and the road's horde fight (broken off between wounds, the horde thinned less, the policy on its card); the autopilot by style (Cautious and Balanced If losing, Aggressive When most are down), a card's policy over the style, a crew by its role (style or its own Retreat setting, also the AI style of a hive assault it plays headless), base defences, raids and fights with no way out never broken off, `breakOff` (the helper for abandoning a base by the numbers); the same fights play out the same, and a team's policy survives a save and load with the fights after it unchanged. The measurement then also resolves every battle by the numbers (fought out, and with the style's policy) on the same team and enemy and compares the two modes: deaths, wipes and wins by the numbers, each runner's escape odds per enemy kind, runners who got out of every break-off (also one nobody got out of) and the team home alive after a retreat, and deaths a battle fought out; fails if the policy costs lives or wipes, if over a quarter of its retreats are from fights it would have won, if a runner's odds per enemy kind differ by more than 15 points between Cautious on the map and If losing by the numbers (both pull out early), if runners getting out differ by more than 15 points, if a retreat by the numbers brings fewer home than the map's (−10 points), or if Cautious retreats less than Aggressive. Seed 7, 10 a matchup: by the numbers, deaths 12 → 2 (If losing, Cautious), 7 → 1 (Balanced), 11 → 8 (When most are down, Aggressive); escape odds (Cautious on the map vs If losing) against raiders 87% vs 80%, soldiers 86% vs 85%, the dead 73% vs 70%; runners out 97% vs 99%. By the numbers is a much gentler fight than the map before any retreat (about 0.1 deaths a battle fought out against 1.2 on the map), so a retreat by the numbers brings nearly everyone home (99%, the map's 60–88%).

```bash
node tools/conflicttest.js 120
node tools/conflicttest_cover.js 30
```

Conflict balance (six 120-day campaigns with a random-choice auto-player: conflict types, outcomes, conflicts per trip, captives) and coverage (forces every conflict type and option, rescue missions, and a 60-day captive soak). Every team goes out with a "Retreat if it goes badly" policy (`breakoff.js`, the four in turn); it counts the fight choices (each must carry a usable fight for the card's forecast) and the fights broken off by policy, and fails if a team set to Never broke one off or no team ever did.

```bash
node tools/fighttest.js 7
```

Fight and living-world rules: other groups field humans only; a hit that leaves a sleeper alive wakes its group; no horde below 5 stays on the map and wandering hordes that meet merge up to 600 (the rest stays a horde of its own); surrender takes what its text says; gear wear (only the units that fought, only what they fielded, bandage kit per treatment, surgical kit needs the skill); ammo found on the way counts in fights by the numbers; a queued battle whose team is gone is dropped; no double loot after a fight on the map and bonus gear needs carry space; failed payments, the trade-visit queue and the hourly check that no team stays halted; a collapsed group's land and parties; pacts torn up by hostile groups and the raid threshold; tribute and help-call amounts worked out when chosen; tribute paid to a raid already at the walls ends it (no break-in after); a war raid beaten in person scores for your side; raids on you (sized 0.6–1.0× your prepared defence and never above the whole group, escalation +25% a raid beaten up to 2× and reset by a break-in, the siege shortened by a strong defence, damage growing with the hours and the outcome and hitting the defences first, a damaged wall worth 2, a wrecked bunkhouse no beds, no building on alert, the result card with what was taken and wrecked and its "Start the repairs", tribute goodwill fading 0.5 a day, a provoked peaceful group raiding only you, a collapse handing bases and people to the winner, a new gang when fewer than two hostile aggressive groups are left); unknown groups not named; the downed carried out of raids; defending in person (attackers match the raid's strength, no second raid after a loss); trade-route terms and exchanges at barter prices; a route with no escort pausing itself.

```bash
node tools/supplytest.js 70
```

Supply runs: founds test outposts, runs repeat / one-off / hold / disband / recreate jobs and blocked cases for 70 days on three seeds (the groups do not raid in it: raids sized to a base's defence would take the test outposts while their crews are on the road), and checks stock moves and schedules.

```bash
node tools/medtest.js 30 42
```

Medical: hospital tiers through the build queue (one step at a time, upgrades replace the old tier), medic staffing, passive healing and bed caps per tier, treating at a base (meds used, heal per tier), field treatment with a team's carried meds, infected wounds, and a short campaign with a staffed clinic; treatment limits: one wound treatment per patient per day at a base and in the field (20 clicks a day for 5 days = 5 treatments), medic capacity (3 + Medicine/4 a day, 2 untrained, surgery takes 3 from one medic, a plain treatment when no medic is free for it, illnesses use the same time, resets at midnight), automatic care worst off first (3 meds for 69/69/69/12 go to the 12 first; pneumonia first; the two worst when medic time runs short), the meds reserve kept by every automatic dose over 5 nights at reserves 0/3/6 (also for pneumonia; a hand treatment may use it; automatic care off uses nothing); the admit line at 30/50/70/90/100 (beds for the right people, `s.inBed` matching `patients()`, everyone out of bed fit to work and building), worst first when beds run out; an understaffed upgrade (a Clinic with 1 medic runs as a Field hospital and logs a warning; a Trauma hospital with 2/1/0 medics runs as Clinic/Field hospital/none); the Medical tab's rate against the real change over a day (12 cases, looked at midnight and 10:00); and 200 days with a clinic, an admit line and a reserve with no NaN or negative meds.

```bash
node tools/medstest.js            # sources by site type, bodies, trade, meds spent only on need
node tools/medstest.js sample 20  # the autopilot over a year: meds stock, meds spent, deaths
```

Meds: where they come from by site type (an hour of searching and a raid by the bot at each kind of site; pharmacies, clinics, hospitals and medevac wrecks many times the rest, ordinary places 1–2 at a time), bodies, the trade price, a base where everyone is well spending none, a hurt survivor treated from the base stock and a team in the field treating a bad wound and pneumonia from its own meds.

```bash
node tools/sicktest.js            # illness: causes, contagion, courses of meds, untreated, team doses, alerts, saves, the upset stomach's one-dose cure
node tools/sickcensus.js 112 20   # the autopilot over a year: illnesses per season, meds on illness, deaths, meds held
```

Illness: each cause raises a person's risk (no heating fuel in the cold, hunger and thirst below 25, building, a long march overloaded, dirty water, spoiled food, a fireless camp, crowding, a sick neighbour) and each protection lowers it (a filter, full gauges, a staffed clinic, a lit camp fire, a treated case barely spreading); flu spreads at a crowded base and on a team; a course costs 1, 2 or 3 meds by severity (chest cold 25, dysentery 55, pneumonia 95) and then mends; untreated fevers turn into pneumonia and cost health and mood; a team doses from its own meds by its best at Medicine, once a day; no meds at a base or on a team is logged and alerted; falling ill and spreading are alerted; the course, the news, the stats and the week's meds survive a save, and an old save's illnesses still run.

```bash
node tools/plotstest.js
```

Construction plots, water barrels and storage crates (`src/sim/buildings.js` PLOTS, WATER_BARREL; about 2 s): the plots by base size and that they grow with it, every building's plot kind, a full set of plots refusing the next building (walls and the other kind still going up, a hospital upgrade on its old plot), queued builds holding plots, demolishing (half back, the watchtower and radio flags), a shelter's two utility plots, barrels (4 at most, tiers 25 / 50 / 100, one upgrade at a time, upgrades taking no plot and not counting as a new barrel, a cancelled upgrade refunded), purified and un-purified barrels (the well fills only the un-purified room, the rest stands outside), crates' food room, and old saves over the plots.


Base and survivor rules: game over when the last person dies, deserts or changes sides; the leader card (best six free people, re-checked when you pick); deaths at a base (gear to the armory, no death site); a lost outpost (vehicles left on the tile, staff to the hub, supply runs paused); a cut-off outpost; hunger (escalating, no healing, armor does not help); the stock floor and the stranger card; trade-route escorts living at the hub; construction refunds; the clinic's meds bonus needing medics; farm slots, Heat, med kits, armor condition, recipe and trait texts; crew carried over on the new-run screen, starting roles by skill, unique names; and a hands-off 45-day run on three seeds.

Crafting: hand-craft gates (stock, skill, workshop), the craft queue (materials paid up front, crafters' work-hours, blocked jobs skipped, cancel refunds, queue cap), equipping makeshift gear, durability (fights, treatments, days) and breaking, water filters and crowbars at a base, field crafting from a team's supplies with the bandage kit in field treatment, 25 tactical battles with makeshift weapons, and a short campaign with a crafter.

Crafting benches (`src/sim/buildings.js` BENCH: a crafting bench 8 scrap, 10 h; a workbench 16 scrap + 1 part, 18 h, Mechanics 2; a master workbench 28 scrap + 3 parts, 30 h, Mechanics 5; one utility plot, each tier upgrading the one below in place; about 3 s): a bench at a shelter, an outpost and the hub on a utility plot (none without a free one), hand crafting at a base needing one for all but the Field recipes (station recipes and re-packed filters aside; a workshop does for one in old saves), crafters 25% / 40% / 60% faster and +5% / +8% / +12% condition (at most 100%; field crafting gets nothing), the workbench and up counting as a workshop and being what an ammo bench needs, the new hub starting with a crafting bench and no ammo bench, and the governor building a bench where someone crafts, then the workbench and the ammo bench at the hub.

```bash
node tools/invtest.js 60 11
```

```bash
node tools/rosterqoltest.js 42
```

Roster bulk actions, the Watching panel and map notes (about 20 s): Equip best (only upgrades, items conserved, nobody unticked touched, the weight rule, packs keep their contents, people away skipped), Send to the hospital and Move to base (the convoy leaves and arrives after the road's hours, no road food means nobody leaves and no job is left behind), the roster's buttons and report, every kind of watched row and its status, gone rows, a horde out of sight shown where it was last seen, notes added, edited, deleted, hidden, drawn and in the tooltip, both saved and loaded (and older saves without them).

```bash
node tools/raidloottest.js
node tools/searchtest.js [seed] [v]
```

Raid loot by hand (`src/sim/raidloot.js`): each survivor carries their own share and finds up to their max load, the first look in a container costs the action and a second is free, supplies move as far as they fit (the rest stays inside; any part of a stack, 1 to all), gear goes in hands, pockets and the pack (a pack picked up raises the max load, a gun picked up is fought with at once), found ammo counts at once, a car's trunk and the bikes' racks are opened at the vehicle (a car also has to take home what everyone carries), the extracted bring what they carry into the team's cargo and the lost lose it, a raid saved mid-search loads, and hauling time (8 kg a turn free, then 12 kg an action; one heavy lift; putting back free; Take all stops when time runs out), handing things to a teammate next to you (free, their max load holds, a downed one too, a gun into their hands is fought with at once), the pile someone who bleeds out drops (searchable, not counted twice, lost if left), tools that count at once (flashlight, night vision, med kit), and headless raids (bots carry, walk back to the truck to unload when full, extract) never overload anyone.

Searching (`src/sim/search.js`, `tools/searchtest.js`): splitting a stack moves exactly n and leaves the rest (to a teammate too), never more than fits (and `supplyFit` says so beforehand), hauling time paid on what moved; a full search costs 1 action by day and in the dark and hides nothing; a quick rummage costs no action, shows 30–50% of the things (each one and over 160 containers), adds 5 Heat, once a turn, not on an opened or locked container; it pulls an unaware one of the dead and a guard within 7 tiles (not one beyond, not the dead already after you; 9 tiles fumbling in the dark without a light), and indoors not one behind a wall that puts it past the radius; the full search after it shows the rest; a locked container is 1 action a pick attempt at 55% + 4% a Stealth point − 15% a step, then 1 action to search; a raid saved mid-search keeps what is hidden; the search window's number to move (1, −, typed, +, half, all, Shift+click, Ctrl/Shift-drop) and its full-search button; the raid bot rummages at Heat 45 and searches in full at Heat 5; a rummage cuts a hand 6% of the time by day with no skill, 4.5% at Scavenging 10, 3% at Stealth 20 (the better of the two counts, up to 20), 9% in the dark without a light (6% with a flashlight), never 10%, and over 4000 tries each the cuts come at those rates; a cut costs 2–5 health, never down, and puts a cut (severity 12–25) on the survivor with a line in the battle log; the raid bot cut on every rummage still rummages once a turn and gets on with its turn.

Inventory: migration of old gear (backpack out of the tool slot, arm guards to the arms, old football pads + helmet split), equip / swap / unequip rules, pockets and packs (a pack carries its contents along), armor summed across worn pieces, carry limits by weight (40 kg each with nothing on; a pack adds its kg and weighs its own and what is inside; moving a thing between a person and the cargo weighs nothing extra, taking a pack off when the team is full is refused, a bicycle only goes in storage), looting bodies by hand and headless, leftovers at death sites, captives stripped, battles with and without extra armor, and a render smoke test of the inventory and loot screens.

```bash
node tools/etatest.js 12
```

Travel-time check: launches teams to varied tiles (on foot / by truck, each stance, full and low fuel, with what they pack: the load sets the pace, `src/sim/load.js`), redirects them, runs queued orders (move → camp → treat → move), and boat crossings (on foot, truck on a raft, truck left on the shore, then a move on the far side); compares the estimated hours with the real ones. Trips without road-event delays must match to the hour (a card that changes what the team carries or who is in it counts as an event), and the map's estimated position must reach the destination on the arrival hour.

```bash
node tools/loadtest.js 42 60
```

Weight and load (`src/sim/load.js`, `vehicles.js`, `parties.js`; or `node tools/loadtest.js [seed] [autopilot days]`): every item and supply has a weight (pistol 1, assault rifle 4, plate 8, vest 4 kg; food 0.5, water 1, fuel 1, meds 0.1, ammo 0.02 a unit; a pack weighs what is inside); the band edges (Light ≤ 18, Medium ≤ 28, Heavy ≤ 38, Overloaded above) and their pace and rations (×1 / 0.9 / 0.75 / 0.55, ×1 / 1.15 / 1.35 / 1.6); Scouting 10 and 20 taking a quarter and half of it off; Bad Back (max 28 kg, loads feel 20% heavier), the assault pack (+10 kg, 12% lighter), Packrat (+8 kg); the pool shared so everyone is equally burdened, the team at its slowest member's pace, capacity = max loads less what is worn, `poolAtBand`; on foot a Heavy team is slower and hungrier and the trip takes exactly the estimate, water drunk on the way is the estimate's rate, resting is one ration a day; in summer the trip needs 1.3× the rations in water and drinks exactly that, and a trip into a heat wave drinks each hour at its day's thirst (at least 1.6 on a hot day), as the estimate counts it; on the move a lighter load does not speed the team up but a heavier one slows it at once; cars: full pace and normal rations for the riders, fuel ×1 to 60% full, ×1.3 at 100%, ×1.6 at 125%, overloaded 15% slower and breaking down more, and a light, a full and an overloaded truck each take exactly the estimated hours and burn exactly the estimated fuel; bikes: one stack per base, racks of 15 kg, 55% of the effect taken away, faster on roads, a bike each (refused without), the trip matches its estimate, no fuel, left and picked up again without fuel, a boat takes them for 1 scrap, home again they merge, a bicycle item joins them, they break down less, saved and loaded mid-trip they play on the same; plan rations by load; the same bike trip twice; and the autopilot over [days] in each style (how teams went: car, bikes, on foot by band; none leaves Overloaded on foot unless it must).

```bash
node tools/teamtest.js
```

Team rules on the State Map (scripted cases): the carry limit (by weight: a walker with nothing on carries 40 kg, an assault pack 48 more), crew and mission checks at launch; a team with nobody left always ends as a wiped team (cargo at a death site, vehicle abandoned, both marked) and is swept every hour; cards never outlive their team or the person they are about, and at most one event card per team per hour; an interrupted trip goes back to the front of the queue and queued jobs keep their tile; team names and "Done" titles; gathering only on a shore and running out; boats planned again when the vehicle changes; a vehicle restarted from the fuel in its tank and the cargo left inside it; carry space for recovered items and markers cleared when nothing is there; no camp healing without food; old cards that cannot take stock below zero; one search at a time; supply-run edits and thirsty held convoys; town and group names.

```bash
node tools/moveouttest.js
```

Moving people between bases (`src/sim/moveout.js`; or `node tools/moveouttest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): the Overcrowded card (none while everyone has a bed, within the hour of one person too many, not again the next day, again at once when 2 more are over the beds, again after 5 days, never two open for one base, none while the autopilot plays); a team moved out (from that base's people and stores, the hub with a bed to spare after, waiting outside with no card in the way of the map pick, named *Moving out team*; sent to a free tile it gets the orders card and founds an outpost with the scrap it carried; a crowded outpost sends one too, only its own people, from its own stock); the Enter card (a team sent to an outpost is asked *Enter …?* instead of the orders card: yes, its people live there and its supplies and cargo are in the outpost's stores, "moved into" in the log; a team from the outpost asked at the hub, no: still a team waiting at the hub's tile with its orders card; a team at its own base is not asked); the autopilot (the Overcrowded card: *Leave it for now*; the Enter card: no at a full outpost, yes once a bunkhouse gives them beds, yes for a team sent there to move in).

```bash
node tools/tripstest.js
```

Order templates, Repeat trip and undo (`src/sim/trips.js`, `src/ui/trips.js`, `src/ui/undo.js`): templates saved, updated by name, renamed, deleted and capped; a setup applied to another target (fit people kept, stand-ins by job for the hurt, the dead and the away, the vehicle by kind, Move steps of the old target dropped, supplies capped at the stock and flagged, a mission that cannot go replaced); a planner team remembers its setup and its retreat policy, lands in the recent trips when it gets home (other launches do not) and raises the home alert for a day; save and load keep it all, an older world loads with none; undo in the planner (steps, crew, a template applied, at most 20 steps), the build queue (queue, cancel, reorder; refused once started, finished or the refund used) and the Work tab (a cell, a deleted role), Ctrl+Z only on those screens and never in a battle; clean HTML with a handler for every button.

```bash
node tools/fieldtest.js
```

Field rules (scripted cases): zombie speed in battle (3 tiles an action, 4 at power 5+, survivors 5); Heat for teams that stand still (gain by activity, place, hive land, team size, night and stance; 8 an hour off on the move, none at a base; the panel text), trouble by Heat and danger (Monte Carlo on two seeds: a quiet night under 5%, three days in a city nearly always and more than once; the planner's look-ahead agrees; trouble is a conflict card and the team goes back to its camp afterwards); plan presets run in order and come home, nearby sites richest first; ETA and rations for whole plans (12 plans on foot and by vehicle: home to the hour when no road card delayed them, the team panel's queue estimate equal to the planner's); in summer, plans that run into a heat wave carry more water than food and the team drinks exactly the planned water, hour by hour at each day's thirst; Fetch water (a plan to a river or lake shore counts its 2 hours, fills the containers and brings the water home; away from water the order fails with a reason); "if … then come home" conditions (hurt, food, cargo; the report card can resume the plan; no rule, no return); the radio rule (no orders out of contact, orders while its card is open or in the hour you answered it, any time in radio range; a held team checks in again after 12 hours, an open-ended camp once a day); cards on finds (horde, rich site with *Search it next*, supplies short of home); the battle's end-turn confirmation.

```bash
node tools/autotest.js 120 all 1 2 3 4 5
```

Autopilot: plays 120 days on seeds 1–5 with the AI governor in charge (`src/sim/autopilot.js`; style `cautious`, `balanced`, `aggressive` or `all`), next to simtest's simple auto-player on the same seeds. Per run: days survived, population (now and peak), deaths, desertions, captives, buildings, outposts, trips launched, cards resolved, raids held off and hub stock, plus what the governor did (builds, crafts, gear handed out, treatments, search hours, supply runs, outposts, trades, role changes). Then a takeover check on the browser code paths: switch on while a raid battle is open, play 10 days the way the game loop does, switch off (auto-combat restored, teams untouched). Then a relay check: with the hub's station on the air, an outpost 9–11 tiles out and a known group past it (beyond the station's 12 + 6), the governor raises a radio relay at the outpost and the group comes into reach; never one beside the hub's station, nor at an outpost the station does not reach (it would not link). Flags crashes, cards left open after a tick and teams stuck with nothing to resolve.

```bash
node tools/balancesim.js 730
```

Balance census: 100 autopilot campaigns (seeds 1–34 × the three styles, trimmed to 100; or `node tools/balancesim.js [days] [--runs N] [--seeds 1-34|1,2,5] [--styles all|cautious,balanced] [--workers N] [--json path] [--quiet]`), each up to 730 days or until the holdout falls or wins, spread over worker threads (about 3 minutes on 12). It only watches the world between ticks (new log lines, cards as they are added, the memorial wall, survivor flags) and changes no rule, so every run plays out as it would without it. Prints Markdown tables per style: outcomes (fell / hive victory / alliance victory / survived) with the day spread and a survival curve; deaths by cause, desertions, what took the last people and the last 3 days before a fall; population over time; pressure per 30-day phase (group and outsider raids, horde attacks and warnings, deaths, desertions, serious illness, cards and decisions a day, overlap days, hungry or thirsty days); first death / raid / horde / break-in / overrun / desertion; days between big threats; cards a day (mean, p90, busiest day, distribution, most common titles); longest stretches with no card and with no threat; "overlap" (days with two or more of raid, horde attack, sickness, death, hunger or thirst within 3 days); hives, hordes and the dead on the map over time, horde attacks by result and hive assaults; raids by result and attacker; wars, collapses and the coalition; stocks over time and days out of food or water. Flags crashes, NaN, cards left open and stuck teams. `--json` writes every run's raw data (daily counters, samples every 30 days, each raid and horde attack). Findings: `review/balance-census.md`.

```bash
node tools/maptest.js 60
```

Map generation over 60 seeds for every world size (or `node tools/maptest.js [seeds] [firstSeed] size=huge` for one): the map has the size's dimensions; every map has its National Guard bases (off-road, away from the hub; two on a Huge map), every town and city has its own police station and auto shop (cities at least two police stations), the size's number of cities and about its share of towns, site names are unique (no numbered towns), every town and city is on the road network, the hub is a small town at least 6 tiles from any hive, hive density stays near a Normal map's (about 2 per 1,000 tiles), the most AI groups the size allows all get placed on their own sites with their own color and name, every site type has raid buildings, valid gear and a `known` rule (hidden sites, `discovery.js`: every site classified by its rule and the road, no town, city or landmark hidden, no hidden site known on day 1 outside the hub's view), and the same seed and size rebuild the same map (a Normal map is the same as one made without a size, as before sizes existed). Prints sites per zone and per type, hidden sites (by type, near the hub, in view on day 1), cities, towns, hives, group spacing and the generation time per size, then a table of the sizes.

```bash
node tools/customtest.js
```

The custom setup (`src/sim/worldsize.js`, new-run screen): the custom formula gives the presets' counts at the presets' sizes, sizes, groups and hives are clamped to their ranges, a custom 64 × 42 map is the Normal map, a set hive count of 0–10 is exact on Small, Normal and Huge maps, and every corner (32 × 24 to 200 × 140, 0 to 50 groups, 0 to 10 hives, Normal and Hardcore) builds, shows on the new-run screen, plays 20 days with the autopilot, saves and loads as the same size. Prints the build time and the time a day takes for each.

```bash
node tools/timelapsetest.js 240 42 8
```

Timelapse recording: plays 240 days on seed 42 with 8 groups (simtest auto-play), then kills one survivor a day until the holdout falls. Checks a frame for every day, memory (estimate and measured heap, scaled to 365 days), captions, decoding (sequential vs random access, and exact match with the live world sampled weekly), that the same seed without recording ends the same, renders every day's frame state with the real map renderer on a stub canvas (and, zoomed in, every 10th day with the terrain detail: decals drawn, none with *Map detail: Flat*, the same decals as the game's map), and builds the viewer's overlay HTML; ruins of razed bases match the live world in every sampled frame, and a ruin made, built over and made again shows in the replay (and is drawn) only on the days it stood. The memory budget is 2 MB on a Normal map and grows with the tile count on Large and Huge ones (`node tools/timelapsetest.js 200 42 15 size=huge`: about 0.8 MB for a year).

```bash
node tools/historytest.js 40 42
```

The Ledger's history and the run summary (`src/sim/history.js`, `src/ui/ledger.js`; or `node tools/historytest.js [days] [seed]`, about a minute and a half): a Balanced autopilot run with a test outpost writes one colony row a day and one per base; each base's stock change every day is its in − out + moved, the colony's stocks are the bases' sum and its moved column nets out; deaths, arrivals and population match the world; the outposts' daily surplus run shows as moved. The same seed with the history thrown away every hour plays out the same (dice, log, stocks), and reading it (rows, the Ledger, the summary) leaves the dice alone; the recorder's cost a day. Compaction (daily → weekly past 120 days, weekly → 28 days past 104 weeks: spans, averages, counters), a save made mid-day loading and playing on with the same history, an older save without one starting its history the day it is loaded; causes of death, the worst day, notable events; the Ledger for every base and range, the summary tab, the game-over and victory screens with the summary, the top bar's arrows and sparklines, the E key; no undefined / NaN / null, balanced tags, every data-action handled; the Help topic and this README.

```bash
node tools/wintest.js 1095 30 all
node tools/wintest.js 1095 12 balanced --tune HIVE.SITE_DAYS=9999:9999 --keep --log 40
node tools/wintest.js --force hive
```

Win conditions: plays long autopilot campaigns (days, then seeds as a count, a range `1-30` or a list `1,2,5`, then styles `all` or a list) in parallel worker threads and counts how each ends: hive victory, coalition victory (its own path: over half the land with every group still standing in the coalition), fallen or still going. Per victory: the day, a timeline every 90 days (population, hives standing and being built, hives destroyed, coalition members and land) and the days hives fell and groups joined; per style: deaths by cause and, for runs still going, what keeps each group out of the coalition. `--keep` plays on after a hive victory (as the *Keep playing* button) to see whether the coalition wins too; `--log N` prints the key events and last N log lines of one run per outcome; `--tune` tries balance numbers for that run only (`HIVE`, `DEFENCE`, `ALLIANCE`, `HORDE_SIZES`, e.g. `HIVE.BOSS_DAY=240`; nothing in the game changes). Per style it also counts the conquest (assaults, bases taken and taken back, groups broken) and what the groups took from you (bases, the hub, retreats, times with no base left), and the world: groups collapsed, new gangs, raider bands that settled, bases razed (yours, between groups). `--force hive|alliance|conquest|keep|loss` is a rigged scenario on seed 1 (every hive but one removed and a strong squad sent at the last; for alliance every group put in the coalition and outposts placed until it holds over half the land, with the hives still standing; for conquest every group but two broken by taking their bases, the two left in the coalition, outposts to over half) that checks each ending end to end: the game state, the log line, the timelapse caption and the end screen.

```bash
node tools/eratest.js
node tools/calibrate.js
node tools/balancesim.js 1666 --runs 10 --workers 8
```

Eras (`src/sim/eras.js`, `outbreak.js`; or `node tools/eratest.js [days]`, about two minutes): the calendar (112-day years, matching the season display), nothing growing in years 1–2 and every lever growing from year 3 to its cap (a new roaming horde bigger in year 6, a guard or a split part not; an aggressive group's strength, a peaceful one's unchanged; a new gang with the year's arms), the coalition's conditions met on day 30 but no victory (the Accord's clock, its decay, no victory before year 8, the coalition's age), the hives all destroyed in year 1 and a mass of the dead coming over the border to build a new one with no hive victory, the hive victory only after the burn-out year and a season clear; then one autopilot campaign per style to the end of year 6 (day 658): no victory before year 8, hives and aggressive groups still in the state at the start of year 5, no crash, NaN or stuck team. `calibrate.js [fights=24] [seed=5] [--raw path]` (about 15 seconds): 27 matchups of field fights (people against raiders, soldiers, the dead, a mix, a hive's brood; ambushes both ways) each played on the battle map by the AI on both sides, fought out, and resolved by the numbers from the same start; prints both modes' wins and deaths a fight by matchup and fails when, over the grid, the numbers are more than 8 points off the map's wins or 25% off its deaths. `balancesim.js` runs over 730 days report by game year (`--tune ERAS.ACCORD.DAYS=56` and the like for what-ifs, as wintest.js).

```bash
node tools/conquertest.js 42
```

Conquest and the coalition victory (`src/sim/conquest.js`, `alliance.js`; or `node tools/conquertest.js [seed]`, about a minute): taking a group's base by hand (defenders, walls, your outpost with its land and stores, the stronghold radius) and with the Assault their base mission end to end (the card with its odds, the battle by the numbers, who stays to hold it, a hopeless assault that fails); a group's last base breaking it (its parties gone, no longer counted); goodwill with its pact partners, friends and enemies, infamy (what peaceful groups want before joining, their drift, the fade), attacking a coalition member (out, no pact); the coalition victory with only members left and the hives standing (and after Keep playing the hive victory still comes once; with nobody left you alone count); late gangs (a raider band without the goods to start a base does not settle, one with people and loot digs in and becomes a hostile gang, the log says so; gangs from the border keep coming with no cap; a gang that settles first has to be beaten, then the coalition wins at that moment); a group breaking at 3 people (also when war raids grind it down); razing (never by peaceful types, more with a grudge and far from home, a razed outpost of yours: gone, a ruin with a little loot, 5 scrap to rebuild, the tile panel, save/load; the razed hub; razing between groups; the warning card); raids that keep bases (between groups by keepChance, your overrun outpost kept, its people leaving as a team, the garrison, the card and the Goals line); retreat before a raid (the card's choice, the walk to the hub, the raiders taking it); losing the hub with another base and with none (the exiles: 20 days with every screen drawn, Establish base instead of Build outpost, the exiles' staging camp with its needs and work becoming a shelter with the stores as stock and then built up into the hub, the autopilot with no base for 30 days); a lost base's walls weak at first and patched up 60 days later, the tile panel, save/load of a base they hold and of a captured or retaken one, taking it back (same base, buildings as they stand), ruins coming back when the holders are gone; base battles' way out on the side away from the attack, and pulling out of a raid fight (the base is theirs) and of a horde fight (the base stays yours); an Aggressive autopilot year that storms and takes bases.

```bash
node tools/hivebirthtest.js 42
node tools/festivaltest.js 42
```

Hive birth you can see (`src/sim/hivebirth.js`; or `node tools/hivebirthtest.js [seed]`): the rumour near your land and none far from it, the massing card once the gathering is known (the planner aimed at it, Mark it the default, ignore), the marker's estimate from a fresh and an old look, the alert, the fight smaller than a hive's brood, breaking up a gathering before the mound (given up, the next site pushed back) and under way (set back, unfed: no hive sends to it, no wandering horde drawn in, fed again after the pause), a Cull horde team breaking one up end to end (the card's Break up choice, the battle played headless), the card when a hive you knew of rises, the autopilot's answer and its trip (strong, weak, out of reach, one at a time), save/load, and a year of Aggressive autopilot without an error. The harvest festival (`src/sim/festival.js`; or `node tools/festivaltest.js [seed]`, about a second): the morning of autumn day 22 once a year, holding it (food, bonfire, drink, the day off but for the guard, the roles back at midnight, mood, the afterglow, bonds, the map's fires), the small one and skipping it, put off when under attack or starving and dropped for the year, no hub, the autopilot by its food, stories over 40 festivals, save/load.

```bash
node tools/hivetest.js 24
```

Hives and hordes (`src/sim/hives.js`, `hordes.js`, `defences.js`; numbers in `hiveconst.js`): resource income by cluster size, horde costs and sizes and the horde score, 5–6 hives (over 8 seeds) with no hordes and 5 resources each at the start, hordes moving at most 2 tiles a day, merging and splitting (parts of at least 10), a build site every 120–180 days (hives and hordes alone, 570 days), the 30-score / 1,000-dead rule for a new hive with at most 40 of the dead going in a day (25+ days to grow), the Wallbreaker for 40 and the next one from that hive only 90 days later, hordes from the border, hold limits by wall tier and with defences, a horde held at the walls falling back 4 tiles and leaving that base be for 10 days, a break-in played out as a battle, toughness on a hive's land, infection fading after a hive dies, the win condition, determinism, and two 365-day autopilot runs without NaN. The hive tech tree (`hivetech.js`, `zombietypes.js`): growths placed within 2 tiles of their hive (never on water, a site or a base, one a tile), their costs and build times, income from pits and feeding grounds, the brood chamber's guard, the special dead a grown spawner unlocks (and the guard it buys carrying them), Wallbreakers only from a den, tiers by day and age (a late hive starts at 0), the hive's choices (young: a pit; attacked: a brood chamber; day 700: its spawners; day 1600: screamers, crawlers or the den; its guard first; saving for what it cannot afford), 300 days of growths, burning (one fire leaves 40 of 100, regrowth, the second fire, Molotovs, fighting its guard with a strong team), decay after the hive dies, the horde mix (merging, splitting, battle counts, the walls' weight, crawlers slipping past and the watchtower and floodlights stopping them, border hordes by era), determinism and save/load with growths; the special dead in battle (stats, an unseen crawler, acid through full cover, a brute smashing a barricade, a screamer calling the dead, one battle twice the same). Then the battles calibrated by the AI playing both sides (the argument is battles per row): storming a hive with a well-armed, a medium and a poorly armed team, and break-ins at a tier-2 hub by horde size, with and without a Wallbreaker.

```bash
node tools/hordecensus.js 365 all 1-20
```

Horde census (the pacing of `hiveconst.js`): autopilot campaigns (days, then cautious / balanced / aggressive / all, then seeds as a list or a range; default 365 days, all styles, seeds 1–20; the runs are spread over worker threads). Per style and for all runs together: the hives at the start and at the end, build sites picked and hives built / destroyed, hordes bought by hives and from the border, Wallbreakers, groups left; then per 30 days (only runs still going): hives standing, hordes bought, border hordes, the dead on the map, horde attacks on your bases (sent by a hive / wandered in, held at the walls, break-in battles won, overrun), people killed in them, the mean horde size against the mean hold limit, attacks with a Wallbreaker, sites and hives built. Then the first horde at a base and at the hub (median and quartiles), the nearest hive to the hub at the start, deaths by cause, repeat hits (the same horde again within 10 days), days between attacks on the hub, how many build sites you saw before they were done, and the build-site timings (picked → building → hive standing: the window to strike). Tuned for: first horde at the gates around day 25–35 (medium, held by the starting walls), one about every three weeks until day 120, two a month by day 150, about weekly by day 330, a new hive every ~150 days if the hives are left alone (one build site at a time; about four weeks from the first gathering to the hive standing), Wallbreakers from day 200. The slower spread (was 50–80 days, two sites, Wallbreakers from day 120) came from `tools/wintest.js`: with it the autopilot wins the hive war in some runs (1 of 20 Balanced, 4 of 20 Aggressive in three years) instead of none.

```bash
node tools/stealthtest.js 42
```

Gun attachments (`src/sim/gunmods.js`, the table of numbers in its header; or `node tools/gunmodtest.js`, about 12 s): sights (red dot +4 aim, holographic +5 and +3 up close, a 4× scope +3 tiles of range and less fall-off but −8 up close, a laser +6 up close), muzzle devices (a 9mm or rifle suppressor: shots 35% / 40% as loud at ×0.95 damage and a tile of range; the oil-filter suppressor, now a muzzle attachment: half, 12 shots of wear; a muzzle brake +3 aim and a pistol compensator +2, both louder; a shotgun choke: +2 range, −4 up close), magazines (an extended 9mm magazine 24 rounds, a drum ×2 for the carbine and the assault rifle at −2 aim, a shotgun tube +2) and under the barrel a laser, a foregrip or a bipod; one per mount, only on the guns each fits (no suppressor on a revolver or a shotgun; bows and the pipe shotgun take none). Fitted in the inventory (select a gun: Fit / Remove; select a part: Fit to the gun in hand), they count in battle (`shotOdds`: aim, up close, range, fall-off, damage), in a shot's noise (earshot, alerts, raid Heat, the map's gunfire Heat) and on a hunt (its Heat, the base's hunting noise, the chance the dead turn toward the shots), add their weight and worth to the gun and are sold and looted with it. Found at gun stores, police stations, the Guard, prisons, hunting lodges, cabins and ranches, army wrecks, malls (searches and raid containers, their own dice), on bodies, on traders' and merchants' tables; enemies' guns carry them by kit (soldiers 65%: holo and red dot sights, scopes, suppressors, a drum now and then; guards lasers; militia and fighters scopes and bipods; raiders and survivors cheap or makeshift parts) with the same effects, shown in their weapon's name and looted with the gun; starting guns come with 0–2 cheap parts (by the run's seed); the autopilot fits loose parts from the armory. A carried oil filter (older saves) still halves a 9mm or rifle shot in battle.

Stealth kit, prisoners and the deploy step (`src/sim/stealth.js`, `prisoners.js`, `deploy.js`): takedown odds (30 + 2.5 × Stealth, a club +20, from behind +15, from cover +10, soldiers −15, an aware but staggering target −15; a non-lethal blow on the aware), Subdue knocking out where Melee kills, the knocked out waking in 4–6 turns unless tied up; a won fight's prisoners walking home with the team (10% slower each on foot) and into the cells, a fight at a base sending them straight there, *Leave them* (goodwill +2); cell costs (1 food and 1 water a day) and escapes by the day (guarded cell, unguarded, shed: Monte Carlo against 0.4% / 3% / 8%), starving; questioning (their bases onto the map, the group known, people and stock, once every 3 days, souring them), ransom (paid or refused over 60 asks, stock and goodwill), trade back and release (goodwill, their head count), execute (mood −6, Brave −2, goodwill with them and every known group); recruiting (the rate, months to a recruit card, taken in; zealots far slower); rescue raids (the daily chance and its modifiers, a Monte Carlo against the expected count, the warning card's three choices, beaten off at the walls or freeing them); throwables (carried counts, 1 action, a bottle pulling the near guard but not the far one, the guard walking over to look, too far refused, firecrackers pulling the dead); the suppressor (half the noise, a far guard not woken, wear per shot, gone when worn out, no suppressor on a shotgun); night raids (enemy sight 7 and 5, your sight 7 / 9 with a flashlight / 13 with night vision, detection by day, at night and with a lit flashlight, half range from behind, searching in the dark); a body seen raising the alarm, the same body behind a wall not found, Drag (3 tiles for 1 action, the body behind the dragger); the deploy step (the attack side from the State Map in all four directions, the turned map, valid default posts, the dead on that edge, the gate holder and the towers' shooters, no placing outside the walls, *Hold the gate* swapping places, *Default positions*, floodlights aimed at a third of the approach, headless battles skipping the step, the open yard without walls); the new recipes; the autopilot's rules (no prisoners without a cell, Aggressive subduing for intel up to its free cells, dealing with them by style); determinism of a night raid with the kit and a deployed break-in; and 120 days with prisoners on each autopilot style without NaN or negative stock.

```bash
node tools/defencetest.js 42
```

Base defence in three tiers on two maps, verticality and the battle fog of war (`src/sim/deploy.js` the wall map, `src/sim/interior.js` the yard and buildings map, `combat.js` heights, sight and battering, `defences.js` the tiers by the numbers; or `node tools/defencetest.js [seed]`, about 10 s): the yard map generated from a base's real buildings (the same base lays out the same map; its main house, bunkhouses, infirmary, workshop, storehouse and water tank are on it; upper floors with stairs, flat roofs with ladders, barred doors and windows with hit points; barricades, sandbags and buildings added show up; every door and the wall reachable from the back gate); the wall map (wall sections and the gate with hit points and battered by a horde that broke through, the walkway at height 1 with ladders, towers at height 2, shooters posted on them; a section battered down is a breach; the dead cannot pass the barred gate, your people can); the breach (4 of the dead inside the wall at the start of your turn: on to the yard map, the same people with their health, the downed still down where they lay, the base's ammo, the attackers inside brought along and the rest waiting outside to come in through the openings, a redeploy away from them, people in the buildings, fought to the end); falling back by order (the attackers move next; nowhere further to fall back to); the buildings (a barred door keeps the dead out until battered in, full cover inside against the yard, windows, the battle AI taking people indoors for the last stand); verticality (+2 sight a level, +5 aim down a level up to +10, half cover against shooters below, a watchtower's full cover, sight over a wall in front but not into the dead ground behind one near the target, climbing only by a ladder at 1 a level and 2 for the dead, a blow struck up −10); the fog of war (an enemy behind a wall unseen, its cell fogged, not targetable, a last-seen ghost; a manned watchtower seeing over a wall; at night plain sight 7, night vision and a thermal 13, the dead 5; binoculars +3 by day; floodlit ground seen at night); the tiers by the numbers (walls, a tower and the gate raise the wall tier, barricades and sandbags the yard, buildings the last stand; holdCheck's yard and buildings limits; raids held at the wall, pushed back from the yard or overrun; an auto-resolved base battle reports its tier); the AI falling back with half the team down on the wall and a break-in played out headless on both maps; saving and loading on each map playing on the same; determinism; a base without walls opening on the yard map.

```bash
node tools/siegetest.js 42
```

Siege equipment, fire, decoys and the Shepherd (`src/sim/siege.js`, `throwables.js`, `decoys.js`, `zombietypes.js`; or `node tools/siegetest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): grenades over walls (no line of sight to a cell a teammate sees, refused where nobody does and nothing used, a tile off by aim: 45% less 0.8 a point over 40, +15% blind, a pipe bomb more; 20–30 to everyone within a tile and nobody further, crates and barricades blown to rubble, a pipe bomb at the gate 40–60); ladders (the carrier a tile slower, set up from the foot in one action, the climb onto the walkway, no cover and +10 to be hit on it, the AI shooting climbers first, pushed off by a defender on the walkway at 50% (35% with a climber) with the climber falling and losing the turn, raised again, on the map as overlay data); the ram (a crew of three at 3 tiles an action that cannot shoot and has no cover, 3 × 16–24 a swing, the reinforced gate down in 3–4 swings, set down and taken up again, the crew keeping up with the lead, dropped with one left, rocks from the walkway hitting about 70% and glancing onto the crew); fire (a Molotov's 3 × 3 patch, 3 turns in the middle and 2–3 round it, thick smoke on every burning cell blocking sight through it, spreading a round by fuel: hay 45%, barricades 25%, a field 20%, grass 12%, never road, dirt, walls or water; a barricade burning out to rubble; one of the dead that has seen you walking into the flames instead and burning, most dead within two turns, a brute burning less; the raiders' AI and your AI not walking through a wall of fire, your own order can); decoys by sense (the table of senses per kind; an alarm clock pulls the ordinary dead, runners, brutes and spitters, a heat pack crawlers, a flare the ordinary dead, spitters and crawlers; screamers and Shepherds nothing; an aware runner walks to the ringing; an unaware guard looks where an alarm clock was thrown from, ignores a heat pack, still goes to look at a bottle); the Shepherd (tougher, the dead within 4 tiles herded and one further off not, a decoy and a fire pulling none of its pack, it will not walk into fire, shot first, the aura gone when it dies, the fold at tier 3 from day 1100 (year 10) unlocking it, the horde warning "led by something that isn't fooled", decoys turning its horde 30% as often, by the numbers decoys worth nothing against its pack); raiders' kit by preparedness (300 raids a year on a walled hub: none in year 1, about 63% in year 3, 86% year 5, 98% year 8, all by year 10, outsiders less often; none without walls; the same raid the same kit; the warning card's "They carry 2 ladders and a battering ram, and 2 pipe bombs to throw over the walls."; defended in person, the battle hands out the ram, the ladders and the pipe bombs); by the numbers (the wall tier −0.25 against 2 ladders, a ram and 2 grenades, by the raids' numbers and autoResolveCombat; The Carrier, its worm swarm and the human dead (`src/sim/carriers.js`, `worm.js`; every number in `hiveconst.js` `CARRIER`; a few seconds): the worm cyst is the first special dead a hive breeds (tier 0, after its first pit) and its hordes carry Carriers; a human body within 2 tiles of a Carrier gets up at once, with no warning (at the start of the enemy turn, or on the Carrier's own turn, acting at once; one of the dead never does, a body farther off does not; the map marks bodies in danger); Crush skull works on any body at any time and Double tap shoots one from range (a round, the noise of a shot), the loot stays, and the battle AI crushes a body in danger; fire destroys a body and everything on it; the swarm: a single bullet or bolt does 15%, a shotgun all of it, a blow more, fire 2.5× as hot; its bite leaves the worm in the living 35% of the time (one of yours keeps it: `catchWorm`, saved and loaded) and a kill always does; whoever has the worm gets up when they die, in a battle or on the State Map (others hurt and the body put down; alone on the road they walk off with their things); the risen wears what the person wore; no item is duplicated or lost; walking loot (a risen still walking at the end takes its things into `state.walkingLoot`, the death site says the body walked; a battle made near the pool has that person among its dead, in their armour, and its body is looted; still walking it goes back in; put down in a lost fight the things lie on the tile; entries drift a tile a day); carrying a body (slower, no shooting, put down, laid in the team's car; a body in the car cannot rise, comes home and is buried or burned; one with the worm left unburied gets up at the base); a raid's body containers; whole battles and a raid with Carriers end, and a fight by the numbers weighs them.

traders sell dear; zealots refuse liquor and cigarettes; an untagged item gets a category; valuables are light; war ×1.3 on weapons and ×0.85 on trinkets, a horde of 60 at their walls ×1.2 / ammo ×1.25, a horde attack, raided ×1.15, a base lost ×1.25 and gold ×0.75, starving food beyond the shortage curve and gold ≤ ×0.6, thirst, winter fuel ×1.4, late autumn ×1.25, a sickness spell meds ×1.5, everything at once held to ×2.6, rich groups ×1.2 on fine things; Seasons and weather: the season cycle (28-day seasons, day 1 = day 15 of spring), the same weather for the same seed and none drawn from the play RNG (a world that keeps looking at the weather plays out the same), the climate per season over 20 seeds × 2 years (weather mix, temperatures, snow cover, off-road slowdown, potato growth) with every factor in range, the forecast (3 / 4 / 5 days, tomorrow nearly always right), arrival estimates under real winter weather and under forced weather that flips between fair and very slow every day (trips, a queue with a camp, a redirect on the move: all must match to the hour without road-event delays; a trip crossed by a weather front that formed after the estimate counts as delayed), and a two-year run without NaN. The forecast is checked against the region's day records (its storms and fog are fronts that may miss the hub).

Regional weather (`fronttest.js [seeds]`): fronts are seeded (same seed, same fronts; none drawn from the play RNG), come in at a map edge (fog forms inland) at their kind's speed and live at most 72 hours, every kind turns up; the weather differs across the map (more than half the hours) and `weather(state)` is the hub's; travel is slower inside a storm or blizzard and exactly the day's table outside any front, an estimate made before a front formed does not know it, the arrival hour the weather report gives for a front is the hour it gets there; rivers freeze only in deep winter and thaw every year, north first, frozen river tiles can be walked (1.75 h) and never driven, a way on foot crosses the ice and a car's does not, a horde crosses frozen water, a party and a horde on ice that thaws get ashore, thin ice only near the freezing point; save and load mid-storm (same fronts, same weather on every tile, same world after two more days) and a save from before fronts gets the seed's fronts.

water (everyone drinks, the well fills up to the capacity and no further, water brought home is kept, cisterns, wells, crops watered only on dry days, filters, thirst killing faster than hunger with armor no help); seasonal water (1 / 1.3 / 1 / 0.9 a head by season and at least 1.6 on a hot day, at the base and for prisoners, crops a fifth more on a summer dry day and half again when hot, summer evaporation above the capacity, the summer outlook and what it says to build, the warning a week before summer: its log line and card, once a year, none for a base whose water holds, and the governor's summer plan on a hub of 40: filters wanted and scrap held, a broken well holding a part, no newcomers it cannot carry, the wells, cisterns and filters made before summer and no dry day in its first weeks, the same twice); food storage (`src/sim/storage.js`: the hub's 200 cellar and an outpost's 40 pantry, root cellars, storehouse and cold store adding room, food that fits spoiling slowly and food above the room stored outside and spoiling much faster, heat and rain faster and frost slower outside, the cold store best in winter and worst in summer, a warning in the log, preserved food needing no room and barely spoiling, fresh food eaten before preserved, jars coming back, the preserving kitchen's inputs and output and that it needs an operator and jars, drying racks taking only surplus), the food outlook and short rations; the winter crops (climates registered with weather.js, kale ripening before winter, winter wheat overwintering and ripening in spring on real weather over six seeds, no spring sowing of winter wheat, late potatoes judged too late, dry harvests going to the preserved stock, farmers sowing only frost-hardy crops in late autumn); the winter outlook (numbers, more mouths need more, an empty autumn store is short, a full granary covers it, the governor's food target); base scavenging (a small daily chance, the surroundings picked over); illness and injuries (building brings more cuts and sprains than resting, treatment costs meds and works once a day, untreated pneumonia hurts, the sick build less and leave stations idle and get hospital beds, flu spreading in a crowded base with an outbreak card, automatic treatment); fuel (common only at gas stations, truck stops, the airport and military bases; little at the start); and two long runs (Balanced autopilot and hands-off, 224 days by default) with no NaN or negative stock, printing harvests, spoilage, thirst and hunger days, meds per person per 30 days, fuel income, the preserved stock and food dried on racks, and checking that the governor logs the winter stores every week in autumn.
