the option defaulting to Detailed, saved, applied at start-up and switching the renderer), the map overlays (Known area and Coalition land off by default with a line each in the Layers box, the coalition's share against the 50% goal on its line, Help → Map layers and Map legend explaining them and the ruins; the autopilot run searching some ground; a team's looks staying off the map until it reports, then shaded with the day, never another group's, fainter after 30 days, searched tiles and a team's unreported looks surviving save/load; the shading worked out once and not again while panning, again at once after a new report, the same world drawing the same; ruins drawn where a base burned, not before you have seen them since, with salvage, danger and the 5 scrap in the tile panel, gone once your base stands there or the ruin is built over; Coalition land the same tiles `controlledLand` counts, each given to its nearest owner, worked out once over 40 frames and again when a member leaves; every size and zoom with all overlays on without errors or NaN, and the frame time with and without them on a Huge map), the battle view (`src/ui/isoview.js`, battles of every layout from `tools/isobattles.js`: field fights in a town, a forest and a city, a raid, a hive assault and a growth, an assault on a walled base, a base defence on the wall map at night and on the yard map after the fall back, a base without walls; the map sizes by context (`sim/battlesize.js`: fights in the open Medium, a hive Large, a fully built hub's yard Extra-large, a hub without walls Large, a big base's wall map as long as its yard less 3, no base 26×16 and 18×13); a new battle's camera on your people (all on screen, 1.5–2.5 CSS px an art pixel) and the edge markers drawn cleanly; each drawn at four rotations and zooms without errors or NaN; the same battle drawn the same twice; picking: every walkable tile's top projected to the screen picks itself back, or a nearer cell in front of it, never one behind, in all four rotations, raised floors at their height, a unit's body picks its tile; turning keeps the middle of the screen, the zoom clamped; I / , / . / + / − / B / V / the arrow keys, the panel's view bar, drag, click and wheel; Options → Battle view; Help → Battle view and this README), radio relays on screen (the Groups tab's radio header naming the base where a relay would reach a group, the group's radio panel "out of reach" with the same hint, the Coalition tab's "out of radio reach (build a radio relay at …" line, Construction offering the relay at an outpost and greying it out beside the hub's station with why, and with the relay up: in radio reach and the base card's "Relay linked"), and on every screen no undefined or NaN, balanced tags and a handler for every `data-action`.

```bash
node tools/skilltest.js 42
```

Skills, teaching, books and perks (`src/sim/skills.js`): the XP curve (a 3 becomes a 7 in a season of a full day's use, 15 → 16 takes about a year, a farmer on the Farm role for a season of real days), XP per action (wound, illness and field treatments, looking around, an hour on the move, fights by the numbers and on the map, a search hour, a road talk, a day on each role), the daily caps (15 use, 25 study, per skill, reset each day), decay (none in the first 14 days, never below 10 nor the starting level), teaching (the 4-level gap, invalid learners dropped, the learning rate, the teacher's XP, learners keep their jobs, Mentor, the study cap), books (one reader per copy, 6 hours and 24 XP a day, finished books are not read again, the next reader takes over, the book's max level, comics' mood, finds by site, deterministic rolls, traders' stock), perk milestones and choices (cards at 5, 10 and 15, Decide later, the other perk closed, picking from the tree answers the card, starting points for the two best skills, the autopilot by style), every perk's effect where it is coded (all 54), determinism, and a 200-day autopilot run without NaN.

```bash
node tools/exploretest.js 42 200
```

Exploration and upkeep (`src/sim/locks.js`, `blueprints.js`, `crashes.js`, `breakdowns.js`): locks (every lock kind's pick odds at Stealth 0/5/10/20, force with a crowbar, smashing without one only for light locks and with both actions, a safe cannot be forced, a key opens anything at its site; Heat and noise per method through the real combat action; a locked container cannot be searched until it is open; picking gives Stealth XP and wears the lockpicks until they snap), locked rooms (every door a locked door cell that blocks movement and sight, nothing inside reachable, the key opens every door of the room, then it can be walked into), locked loot (a gun cage against a plain box: more supplies, guns, blueprints, keys for lockable sites; keys lost when nobody gets out, on the key ring when someone does), the raid bot opening locks (24 raids, no crash or timeout, mostly by picking) and the hour-by-hour search's locked finds; blueprints (early buildings never locked, each locked building and craft names its manual, a second preserving kitchen, the "Needs: … Look in: …" line, studying on the Read role with the skill (6 h a day, study XP, the days it takes, then the building can be queued), finds only at fitting sites and never one you know, deterministic rolls, traders' stock, the autopilot studying one); crash events (the first between days 8 and 14 and then every 10–20 days, each burning out 7–10 days later and gone from the map; the circle always holds the wreck at spawn and after every narrowing, a look within reach finds it, the wreck is not on the map until found; groups racing for it and stripping it to 30%; a horde drawn by the noise; a search team sent from the card finds and searches it); breakdowns (wear by use, age and frost, Grease Monkey, never broken below 60 wear and always at 100 with a card, a broken generator does not run, a repair paid up front and worked only 08:00–20:00, jury-rigging and its faster wear, cancelling refunds, a broken well, walls and radio station and the radio back after repair, horde attacks wearing the walls and gate, the storm well-pump hub event as a breakdown, a steward repairing by itself, parked vehicles in the frost); and long runs (Balanced and Cautious autopilot and hands-off) with no NaN or negative stock, printing crashes, breakdowns, keys and blueprints.

```bash
node tools/raidcensus.js 365 all
```

Human-raid census (`src/sim/factions.js`, `diplomacy.js`, `radio.js`, `prisoners.js`, `visitors.js`; or `node tools/raidcensus.js [days] [cautious|balanced|aggressive|all] [seeds...] [--runs]`, default 365 days, all three styles, seeds 1–20: 60 runs, about 5 minutes): autopilot campaigns watched from outside (new raid parties each hour, each hour's log lines and the autopilot's *card → choice* lines; nothing in the sim is changed). Per style: the groups' raids launched per run, on you and on other groups, by source (ordinary, war, grudge) and target, by group type and attitude at launch, raids on outposts; raids on you per run (median, range, runs with none, the day of the first) and per 30 days over the runs still going; raid strength against the target's defence at launch and after your answer, the raiding group's strength and the hub's defence on days 30 / 90 / 180 / 365; how many were warned, how many only at the walls, the hours from the warning to the end, the autopilot's choice (prepare, pay, defend in person, hold); outcomes (held by the numbers, won in person, break-in, overrun, paid, base gone, faded on the road); the cost of a raid that reached you (stock value lost, deaths, health lost) and of paying; the damage per raid by outcome (buildings wrecked, damaged and destroyed, the repair bill in supply value and work-hours, the hours of the siege). The same for raiders from outside the state (warned, choice, outcome, losses) and rescue raids for prisoners. The gates: aggressive groups hostile at the start / ever / within 20 tiles, the days each could raid you (hostile, in reach, 8+ people), how many stopped being hostile, groups gone by day 90 / 180 / 365 / 730; peaceful groups that ever turned hostile, when, and their raids on you; groups alive, aggressive (their people and bases) and hostile (within 30 tiles) on days 365 and 730, newcomers among them, takeovers of a broken group's land and new gangs; wars between groups, war raids, pacts and peace. `--runs` adds a line per run. Exits 1 if a run crashed.

```bash
node tools/diplotest.js 42 365 1,2,3,4,5,6
```

Diplomacy (`src/sim/jobs.js`, `diplomacy.js`, `alliance.js`, market lists in `trade.js`): every job type made, accepted, done (horde cut to 40% with your team there, escort alongside half the road, a delivery handed over or fetched by their caravan, a rescue paid out, a hive stormed, a region scouted, a package carried home), failed at the deadline (−2 / −4 goodwill), called off with no cost, offers that expire or are declined (−1), and the rewards (supplies, gear, a blueprint, a recruit card, intel, an outpost handed over); the relations matrix (one number per pair, drift back to rest, pacts at 28+); wars (war raids, a base taken and its land, the floor of 8 people, peace by land, tribute or a white peace, captives home); brokering (failure −3, success ends the war, +8 and a thank-you, once a week, never with a hostile side); joining a war (enemy hostile, its raids come for you, your share of land or tribute); trade at war (+35% ammo and meds, +30% weapons, less to spare, buying back still loses); the coalition (each invitation condition in order, the council's vote, shared sightings, help against raids and hordes, allies at a hive, the three council requests, the seasonal meeting, a member leaving, a group asking to join); the alliance victory (false unless land > 50% and every living group a member; a hive standing or being built does not stop it; the reason on the victory); wants and surplus against each group's stock, weekly refresh and price arrows, radio offers and caravans from the lists, the market board's order; the autopilot's choices; determinism (two processes, 90 days); a 365-day autopilot run without NaN; and the yearly group collapse rate with and without wars between groups (each year in its own process, on the collapse seeds given; wars may add at most +0.15 a group a year plus 1.5 standard errors of the gap measured from the run's own seeds, ~+0.23–0.26 at twelve: twelve seeds' gap swings by ±0.05, and on seeds 1–48 it is about +0.07 to +0.11).

```bash
node tools/dettest.js 60
```

Determinism across worlds in one process (`world.js` `newId` is one counter for the whole process): for seeds 42, 7 and 1234 (or `node tools/dettest.js [days] [seeds...]`) world A runs 60 days on the Balanced autopilot, then world B on the same seed in the same process; a digest (stocks, buildings, wear and repairs, survivors' health / skills / XP / perks / mood / place, parties (with scavenging parties' hauls), hordes, hives, factions (and when they next scavenge), every site's loot and the loot you know there, the base's picked-over surroundings, diplomacy, jobs, the coalition, prisoners, crashes, tech, relationships, stats and every log line) must be identical, ids compared by their offset from the world's first id (each world here starts its ids at the next million, so anything hashed on an id shows up). Then each seed again with a world on another seed and style run in between, a pair after another world, and the same run in a fresh Node process. Last, a tactical battle played alone and with another world's battle (on a map turned to 13 × 18) set up while it is under way must play out the same (`combat.js` points its grid helpers at each battle's own size).

```bash
node tools/savetest.js 120
```

Saving and loading (`src/sim/save.js`, `src/ui/saves.js`, `src/ui/settings.js`): for seeds 42, 7 and 1234 (or `node tools/savetest.js [days] [seeds...]`) the Balanced autopilot plays 120 days; on day 60 the world is saved (through a JSON string) and loaded again after another world has run in the same process; the original and the loaded world both play to day 120 and must end identical, by the dettest digest (`tools/digest.js`) and by the whole saved state; then one seed again in a fresh Node process. Saves made mid-way through a team's plan, with prisoners in the cells and mid-battle (a fight at the hub, a raid, a break-in on the deploy step, a hive assault) must play on the same after loading; an open card cannot be saved (the message names it) and can once answered. A Small and a Huge world are saved, played on and loaded the same way (they keep their size; the save list shows it), and a save without a size loads as a Normal map (a 100 × 66 one as Huge). It reports the size at day 365 (JSON, gzip and what browser storage holds) and the times, checks the storage layer (slots, index, compression, a full storage, newer and unknown versions refused, damaged and cut-off saves refused, the values JSON loses) and renders the settings view for every tab, Help with several topics included (no undefined / NaN, every button handled).

```
node tools/titletest.js
```

The title screen (`src/ui/title.js`, `title.css`): the game opens on it (the desktop app fullscreen the first time, then as you left it; `?notitle` in the URL skips it). Continue (the latest save), New game (the new-run screen), Load game, Settings and How to play (the menu at its tab, over the title), Quit in the desktop app; Exit and Save and exit in the menu come back to it. Behind the spray-painted logo four animated backgrounds drawn in code take turns, each held 45 s and then cross-faded into the next: a ruined skyline at dusk (the dead on a cracked street, fires, a helicopter, blasts), a campfire at night (survivors round the fire, a tent and a pickup, the dead along the treeline) and a holdout's wall in a storm (a sentry, the watchtower's spotlight, rain, lightning showing the horde in the field) and a hive (the Broodmother breathing among the ruins, pores, egg sacs and veins pulsing with its heartbeat, worms, the dead swaying before it, a scout's flashlight on it). The test checks the menu with and without saves, the buttons, the four-scene cycle and the hooks in main.js, ui.js and settings.js.

```bash
node tools/savehealthtest.js
```

Rotating autosaves, the save list by campaign and the error screen (`src/ui/saves.js` writeAutosave, `src/ui/savelist.js`, `src/sim/savesummary.js`, `src/ui/crash.js`; or `node tools/savehealthtest.js`, about 1 s, on a stand-in browser storage). The summary line (day, people, season, then the run's end, a war you joined, a hostile group or a Hardcore band on the road); each campaign keeping its last 3 autosaves (the oldest written over, named by the summary), another campaign keeping its own, an older build's one-slot autosave replaced by its own campaign's first rotating one, none in Ironman, a full storage clearing the slot written over and then older autosaves (the newest stays); the menu's list with one head line per campaign (the game being played first), the summary under a manual save and the storage warning; the error screen: a crash stops the clock and writes the crash slot, errors while it is up are counted, a crash right after Try to keep playing comes back with a warning and keeps the first crash save, the report's contents, Load the crash save, Back to title, Ironman's crash copy beside its slot (removed when the game saves itself again), no crash save on the title screen.

```bash
node tools/savecorpustest.js
node tools/makesave.js --note "why this save is in the corpus"
```

The save corpus (`tools/saves/`, listed in `tools/saves/corpus.json`; or `node tools/savecorpustest.js [days] [file filter]`, about 5 s): real saves made by older builds, each loaded by the current code and checked (it loads; survivors as many as the save's list line says, each at a base or team that exists; a hub in a Normal game; known building types and countable plots; the mode, world size and migration marks; no NaN or negative amounts in any stock, team supplies or cargo; firewood from before firewood by weight ×30 once, saved and loaded again unchanged; Ironman kept), then played 8 in-game days on the Balanced autopilot without an exception, still sane, and saved and loaded again. The corpus: the first save format (38cccac), before woodcutting (d32694b, Normal and Hardcore), before purified water (3af87c5), before construction plots (ae6e77b, Normal and Hardcore), before firewood by weight (29af708, Normal and Hardcore), before the title screen (7353bc3), before battle QoL and Ironman (62479e4), and dev at suggestions-4 (c63a2d5: Normal, Hardcore, Ironman); seed 7, a Small map (Normal 64 × 42 for the first, which had no sizes), 12 days, 80–110 KB each gzipped. **Adding a build's save is one command**: `node tools/makesave.js` makes one with the code as it is (`--days 12 --seed 7 --size small --mode normal|hardcore --ironman --note "…"`) and adds its line to `corpus.json`; for an older build export it first (`git archive <commit> prototype | tar -x -C <scratch>`) and pass `--root <scratch>/prototype --commit <commit>`. Add one whenever a change needs a migration or adds state an old world lacks.

```bash
node tools/ironmantest.js
```

The battle-start save and Ironman (`src/ui/settings.js` autosaveCheck, `src/ui/saves.js`; or `node tools/ironmantest.js`, about 10 s, on a stand-in browser storage). **Battle start**: as each battle opens (before anything is done in it) the game is saved to its own slot (`battle`, the latest battle's; open cards are left out of it, as a card's choices cannot be saved), and the battle panel and its result screen offer *↺ Restart battle*, which loads it after a confirm: turn 1, everyone as they were. A later battle takes the slot, and the earlier battle's Restart is refused then; none with *Save as each battle starts* off (Options), on autopilot or in Ironman. **Ironman** (a box on the new-run screen, with Normal or Hardcore): the game is saved to one slot of its own (`iron…`) at the start, every dawn, when a battle starts and when it ends, at the end of the run, when the window is hidden and as the page closes (written at once, uncompressed); never to the autosave slot. A card waiting for an answer holds the save. Saving, loading, save files, exporting it and Exit without saving are refused; the menu offers Save and exit (and Continue on the new-run screen picks it up again), and a new campaign or quitting saves it first. The tool checks each of these, the menu and the new-run box.

```bash
node tools/battleuitest.js
```

Battle screen conveniences (`src/ui/combat.js`, `src/ui/isoview.js`; or `node tools/battleuitest.js`, about 15 s, on stub canvases): **AI plays my side** (the battle footer, Shift+A) hands your turns to the battle AI (`autoPlayerTurn`, the same as the autopilot's: moves, shots, searches, the retreat from a fight it judges lost) at the enemy turn's pace until you take back control; End turn is greyed while it plays and each battle starts in your hands. **The threat preview**: hovering an empty tile or one of your people lists the enemies you see that could shoot it (each with the odds its shot would have against the selected unit standing there, cover from that side included) or walk one action and strike it, and draws a line from each (solid red with ⚠ odds for a gun, dashed amber for a blow); an enemy you do not see is never counted. **Big maps**: the ground is painted once per battle in map space (16 × 16 texels a tile, the texels the ground colours use) and stretched onto the diamonds with one transform, so turning the view no longer repaints it, and the fog of war is a one-pixel-a-tile mask stretched the same way: on a 72 × 72 fort a move redraws in about 10 ms instead of about 100, opening the battle takes about 200 ms instead of 300, turning the view about 40 ms (upper floors of a building with storeys keep the old pictures). The tool checks the toggle, every threat against shotOdds and sight over thousands of tiles, every layout drawn in all four turns of the view with no NaN, and the Restart battle button (only with a battle-start save, never in Ironman).

```bash
node tools/audiotest.js
```

Sound (`src/ui/audio.js` plays, `src/ui/soundcues.js` decides what; or `node tools/audiotest.js`, about 1 s, no speakers): the Sound options and their store, card urgency, the gun classes, the battle cues (a 200-shot frame cut to a handful, your own shot kept, nothing replayed after a load), the map cues (cards, alerts, the horde sting, the alarm), the ambience mix by weather and hour, the throttle, and every sound built on a stub AudioContext with no NaN. In the browser, `(await import('/src/ui/audio.js')).audioDebug` renders one sound (`renderOffline(name)`) or a stretch of play (`offline(secs)` … `finish()`) without speakers or a click.

```bash
node tools/depletetest.js 42 365 1,2,3,4,5
```

Sites running dry and other groups' scavenging (`src/sim/aiscav.js`; or `node tools/depletetest.js [seed] [days] [curve seeds]`): a group short of supplies sends one party on foot to the best site in reach by its needs, which searches up to 8 hours (loot down by 0.03 × crew an hour), walks home and adds the haul to the group's stock (13 finds per 0.1 loot from a full site before the per-good multipliers); a horde at the site costs a quarter of strength and haul, a party below strength 1 is lost; a contested site (your team there) gives a *Contested site* card once per party and team, nothing is taken while your team searches there, a party driven off drops its haul into your cargo, a friendly group keeps to its side; sites in a group's own land lose 0.3% a day into its stock; the test switch (`state.aiScavOff`) brings back the old economy. Nothing grows back: every assignment to `.loot` in `src/sim` is in mapgen, scavenge, crashes or aiscav and none adds to it, and over a 150-day Balanced run no tile's loot rises except on crash sites (a burned-out wreck leaves its tile at its old loot); the base's surroundings (Scavenge role) do not recover in 20 or 30 days without scavengers and fall 0.4% per scavenger a day to the 20% floor. What you know: a site stripped out of view still shows *last seen …: rich* in the tile panel (with the warning) and *Loot: rich (then)* in the tooltip and still offers a search, a team's look brings it up to date, in view it says *· now*; radio news (*On the radio: … has been stripping …*) with a radio station updates only the loot you know (an older team report does not undo it), and without a radio and out of view there is no news. The autopilot goes past the picked-over ring around the hub (Balanced: range + 5 tiles) to the sites it knows are rich, counted as far trips, and picks a site it believes is rich over the real loot. Determinism (two worlds, one process) and a save / load round trip with parties out. Last, one year on each curve seed (1–12 by default: on five the collapse rate is mostly dice) in a process of its own with scavenging groups on and off (the world goes on after the holdout falls): loot left on days 120 / 240 / 365 (less on every seed with them, falling over the year, at least 25% left on day 365, at most 75% on average) and the group collapse rate per group per year (at most +0.15 with them, diplotest's base tolerance for wars, which adds 1.5 standard errors of its own seeds' spread).

```bash
node tools/upkeeptest.js 42
```

Upkeep per head (`src/sim/upkeep.js`; or `node tools/upkeeptest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): food and water are per head × heads (twice the people eat and drink twice as much, and the daily tick eats exactly that); people on an errand or a team do not eat the base's food and a team eats its own packs (about one ration a person a day); prisoners, guests and a holding supply convoy are counted in the base's food and water and eat preserved food once the fresh is gone; the dog; short rations by everyone fed there; an outpost feeds its own people and keeps a reserve by its heads (more with prisoners); prisoners raise the governor's food and water targets; a generator's fuel and the spoilage do not change with the people; the food, water and Upkeep lines, the Bases list, the top bar tooltip and the food alert show per head × heads.

```bash
node tools/needstest.js 42
node tools/needsbalance.js 100 20 normal
node tools/needsbalance.js 100 10 hardcore
```

Needs (`src/sim/needs.js`; or `node tools/needstest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): a team's hunger and thirst gauges fall by the hour and are refilled from its supplies (fresh game first, packed food, packed preserved, then found food; packed then found water) and never from nowhere; one ration and one water a day keep a person level (the trip estimates hold, the gauges counted); empty gauges cost health away from a base; at a base the gauges follow the stores and the base's daily totals stay upkeep.js's (they never take from the stock themselves); short rations sink to the lean level and hold; low gauges cut work (morale.js moodWork) and aim; short / none rations and who eats first when short (the hurt, or evenly); treats used by the evening rule (the glum, the gentlest first, valuables only below 20, drink only at rest) and by hand, keepsakes kept; an errand takes a day's food and water a day and brings back what is left; heating fuel per head below 0°C (×1.5 below −8°C), the Upkeep line ("Heating: 6 people × 0.1 = 0.6 fuel a day"), the cold without it, a shelter's lit camp fire, a team at a lit camp fire out of the cold; gauges saved, old saves start full. `tools/needsbalance.js [days] [runs] [normal|hardcore] [style] [first seed] [--noheat]` samples autopilot games: survival, deaths by hunger, thirst and cold, hub fuel on days 60, 75 and 90 (winter is about days 71–98), cold base-days and empty-gauge person-days.

```bash
node tools/visitortest.js 365 42
```

Outsiders (`src/sim/visitors.js`, `src/ui/visitors.js`; or `node tools/visitortest.js [days] [seed]`): spawns only on land tiles of the map's border (never within 6 tiles of a base; merchants on a road), the schedule (the first raiders on days 20–26, the gap shrinking from about 29 to 10 days over the year), band size and gear by day; fog (an outsider you cannot see or hear is not known, not listed, not in tooltips; in sight it is announced, in radio coverage heard, out of sight its last position); raiders at your base (the warning card and its five choices, a new warning when a band that was warned of at another base comes for this one, held after the 8-hour siege, a break-in whose loot the band carries off, paid off and never coming back, defended in person with the band's loot taken back, talked down some of the time), at a group's base (one per band), a group's caravan, merchants and a rich site, and leaving at a border tile; your teams meeting them (the raiders' cards and a fight's result card, merchants' trade window on the road, travellers' news into the team's report); merchants (rare goods, cheap fuel and dear food by trade.js prices, the trade window at your base and a manual bought there, the window's header and Rob button, moving on after a day, a trade with a group, the radio call and the invitation, reputation after an attack: −45, traders −15, communes and fortresses −8, the next merchants warier, hostile at −90, recovering 0.25 a day); explorers (intel lifting the fog only on the tiles given and dated when they passed, the gate card's news and recruit card, shelter's food a day and mood, thieves robbing the base that shelters them, ruins losing a little loot); the autopilot (trading with merchants when useful, a raid warning by style); determinism of two worlds in one process; a save with outsiders on the map that plays on identical for 30 days; the timelapse frames and captions; the Groups tab section, alerts and trade window extras (no undefined / NaN, every `data-action` handled); and a [days]-day run per autopilot style without NaN or negative stock.

```bash
node tools/caravantest2.js
```

Caravans on the road (`src/sim/escorts.js`, `src/sim/caravanraids.js`; or `node tools/caravantest2.js [seed]`): the escort tiers by cargo worth (light, armed with a pickup, armoured with a Humvee, an MRAP for the richest; guns and armour counted 1.5×) and the specialities that fill the wagons; a merchant caravan that comes in from the border and leaves at one; your team attacking a merchant caravan and a group's caravan on the map (every supply and item taken, the guards' gear, the escort vehicle joining the team or parked on the tile, the overflow left as cargo behind); the witness rule (seen: merchants' reputation −45, a group turns hostile; unseen: nothing); the meeting card and the Attack / Intercept orders; raider gangs robbing merchants and your convoys by the numbers (everything into their stock and onto their shelf) and a gang's ambush on a group's caravan; a save mid-journey that loads and plays on the same.

```bash
node tools/worktest.js 120
```

Work priorities and crews (`src/sim/work.js`, `src/sim/crews.js`, `src/ui/work.js`; or `node tools/worktest.js [days] [seeds...] [--dice N]`, seeds 1–10 and 4 rolls each by default, about 3 minutes on 20 threads): priority 1 first, then 2, back to 1 when a job turns up again, rest when nothing has work, and the why line; capacity (two stations, four on Workshop 1: two operate, each its own, the others say Workshop is full; a Manual operator's station never booked twice; a clinic's medic slots, filled while someone is hurt and left when everyone is healed; one lookout; two teachers at 12+ people with no learner in two lessons; one reader per copy); ties by skill (the better shot gets the lookout), a person's own cell over the role's (and back), the role's skill minimum; emergencies (the defences manned: everyone with Guard set, even at 4, people without it keep working; a horde the walls hold anyway pulls nobody, one of 5,000 everyone; a warned raid; health 20 and too ill rest; the watchtower alarm: none without a tower, a warning once for a horde 3 tiles out, Guard 4 moved up to 1 with the old 4 kept and their own cells untouched, Guard 1 or no Guard untouched, more lookouts, a horde of 5000 taking everyone with Guard set, a cell set by hand left alone, save / load mid-alarm, the horde gone: stood down and back to 4 and to the farm, a new warning when it comes back, leaving the base ends it, a horde that was beaten off and one 5 tiles out roaming ignored, one coming 5 out warned and kept a tile past the view, at night a tile of view, the dev autopilot moving nobody, and the governor posting more guards on the alarm (at most half); days off kept; a severe wound with nobody on duty pulls Doctor 5; a broken well pump pulls Repair 5 onto the job); the schedule (22:00 on the night watch guards, 06:00 asleep with the mood, 14:00 works; at midnight someone asleep counts at the day's farming, but not a guard while prisoners are in the cells); mood free time below 25 until 40; Manual and pinned people untouched, a station's fallback leaving people on priorities alone, a steward with Roles moving only Manual people, a fixed job from the drop-down (or the roster) putting someone on Manual; roles (the defaults, unique names, duplicate, delete puts members on Manual, everyone on priorities by what they do, newcomers); save / load (an old save loads with everyone on Manual and stays so, roles, schedules, minimums, members, own cells and tasks survive); crews (paused, a trip within range by its field priority with its come-home rules, a small road card answered the governor's way, a fight with people held for you unless *Handle everything*, the status line, the people at home planned while the crew is out, home again with the trip in its log after an hour-by-hour search, resting before the next; Recover at 1 before Scavenge at 2, nothing within an hour's limit, a member below the hurt line turning the team home, danger at home keeping it in); the Work tab (clean HTML, every action handled, left / right / Shift+click, the number keys and R, the schedule strip, the role editor and crews panel, the role drop-down, the roster); then [days] days on each seed with the Balanced autopilot, once with its own roles and once with everyone on work priorities (its test switch `governor.priorityRoles`: the governor plans trips, builds and crafts as usual but leaves people on priorities alone), comparing the food harvested and the buildings and crafts finished (with the governor's survey trips off, `state.surveyOff`: extra trips taken with spare hands that only add noise to this measure; with them on the priorities side finishes about 1.5% fewer buildings). One run per seed is a coin toss (advancing the world's dice by one draw moves the governor's ten-seed total by about ±30 buildings), so each seed is played on 4 rolls of its dice (advanced 0–3 draws before day 1), both ways, in parallel child processes (seeds 1–10, 120 days: governor 77,848 food, 1,401 buildings, 152 crafts; priorities 79,889 / 1,308 / 167; on 8 rolls 2,614 vs 2,603 buildings and 306 vs 309 crafts), with no NaN, negative stock or crash; and determinism (two worlds on priorities with a crew in one process).

Watch roster and defence plan (`src/sim/watch.js`, `src/sim/defenceplan.js`, `src/ui/defence.js`, the base's Defence tab; `node tools/watchplantest.js [seed]`): presets (two, three, four watches, night only), watches added, timed and removed by hand, people kept on a matching watch when the preset changes, Fill from the people here; standing the watch (someone on priorities on guard at 23:00 with "On watch: Night", a Manual farmer on guard and back to the farm after, counting at the farm at midnight, the watch in the walls' hold limit, the badly hurt, the away and the pinned left off, the coverage strip and its gaps, through the real hourly tick); the drill (the base's battle map with only your people, valid default posts, Start and the AI refused, a defender placed by hand, Save as plan, Close leaves no battle); the plan taken up at the deploy step of a real attack on the fort map (the planned cell, valid posts, the log line, everyone else at theirs), Default positions and Planned posts; on the yard map of a base without walls and a bridge barricade's wall map, both turned to face the attack (a plan made facing east stands at the same post when the attack comes from the north or the west), changed in the deploy step and saved back, a base that grew a wall (another map: the old plan not used), clearing; save / load mid-drill and an auto-played battle starting from the plan. Tiredness (`src/sim/sleep.js`): a normal day peaks at 16 hours of sleep owed and nobody is ever Tired without a roster; a 22–06 watch slept off the next morning ("Sleeping off the watch" for people on priorities), night after night without getting Tired, and the roster card's forecast agreeing; the night and the morning watch back to back warned of on the card (back to back, would end up Tired) and Tired in play; the Tired chip and the line under the gauges; aim −5 Tired and −12 Exhausted on the battle map, an exhausted guard half in the hold limit, the mood, away from base. Unwatched nights (`watch.js` surpriseAt, lookoutsSpot): caught asleep at night with nobody on guard, not by day, not with someone awake on guard, an exhausted lone watcher dozing off about half the nights, the alert and a tripwire alarm waking everyone; a raid by the numbers (everyone but the guards 0.5×, the walls not counted) and a horde (the defence buildings half); a raid party reaching the walls unseen at 01:00 (the card and the report say nobody was on watch); the watch's early warning (nothing seen at night unwatched, 3 tiles out in the dark and 2 tiles further by day with a watch, the cards saying the watch saw them early; a horde warning only with someone on watch); a horde at night (the log says why); a surprise battle (their volley, the deploy step, 1 action each) played out on a walled and an open base. The governor's night watch (`autopilot.js` govWatch): two watches at a base of 3+, the first on guard at 23:00 with their job kept for the day's work at midnight, others the next night, nobody Tired over eight days, no roster at a base of two.
Watch roster and defence plans (`src/sim/watch.js`, `src/sim/defenceplan.js`, `src/ui/defence.js`, the base's Defence tab; `node tools/watchplantest.js [seed]`): presets (two, three, four watches, night only), watches added, timed and removed by hand, people kept on a matching watch when the preset changes, Fill from the people here; standing the watch (someone on priorities on guard at 23:00 with "On watch: Night", a Manual farmer on guard and back to the farm after, counting at the farm at midnight, the watch in the walls' hold limit, the badly hurt, the away and the pinned left off, the coverage strip and its gaps, through the real hourly tick); the drill (the base's battle map with only your people, valid default posts, Start and the AI refused, a defender placed by hand, Save as plan, Close leaves no battle); the plan taken up at the deploy step of a real attack on the fort map (the planned cell, valid posts, the log line, everyone else at theirs), Default positions and Planned posts; on the yard map of a base without walls and a bridge barricade's wall map, both turned to face the attack (a plan made facing east stands at the same post when the attack comes from the north or the west), changed in the deploy step and saved back, a base that grew a wall (another map: the old plan not used), clearing; save / load mid-drill and an auto-played battle starting from the plan. Who is at a post when the attack hits (`openStart`): an attack on a base whose defences are not manned finds only the 2 on guard at their posts (a watcher on the tower), the rest in the buildings (the fort map's indoor cells), the deploy step's line ("Only Ana and Bo were on watch...") and the log, those not on watch refused a post and kept inside by Default positions, turn 1 with no actions for them and turn 2 with both ("4 more come out to fight"); nobody on watch (the attackers move first, then everyone comes out), a manned base ("Prepare defenses": everyone at their post), the wall map (no buildings: the back columns), a headless fight with nobody on watch played to the end. Several plans: the first is "Default", New plan from where everyone stands, renamed and tagged (the dead, at night), the drill switching plans, the attack taking up the plan that fits (a horde at night: the night horde plan; by day and raiders: Default; a plan for another attacker never), the deploy step's picker and "no plan", plans made only in a drill, a drill opened on a given plan, Delete, at most `PLAN.MAX`, save / load, an old save's one plan per map loading as "Default". Posts by role: roles from the weapon, All by role with one kept by name, the best shot away and the next best taking the post, the one who stood there keeping it when back, the post by name keeping its person. The governor's plan (`defaultRolePlan`, `autopilot.js governorPlans`): by role on a base without walls, none on the fort map, no draw from the game's dice, laid out once a change of the buildings, and paired fights with and without it (3 seeds, by day manned and at night with 2 on watch, a day later with one away and a newcomer) not worse.

```
node tools/fishgeartest.js
```

Fishing tools and gear durability (`src/sim/hunting.js`, `data.js` GEAR_DUR, `crafting.js`, `inventory.js` newItem; or `node tools/fishgeartest.js [seed]`, about a minute): the makeshift rod (crafted at a base or in the field), the steel spear (looted, a melee weapon) and the pipe spear (the makeshift spear) fish; a rod > a steel spear > a makeshift rod > a pipe spear for one fisher, a well-made rod catches more, one tool a fisher, the best first, and on ice the rod before the spear; spears fish by daylight and never through ice; a team's 12 hours of fishing wear a rod by 2 and a worn-out rod is gone (logged); a day of the Fish job wears the base's rod by 1; with no tool left the job cannot be worked and the governor queues a makeshift rod. Durability on real gear: weapons, armor, the surgical kit, fishing and camp kit wear (optics and books do not), makeshift several times faster; a fight wears a pistol, a vest and a helmet by 1 and a worn-out pistol is gone; found gear is part-worn (30–100% of its uses) and the roll draws nothing from the world's dice; a raider's pistol is worn by a random amount; toiletries and tablets are found full; starting weapons 55–100%; crafted uses by quality (the listed at 75%, +20% at 100%); a worn rifle is worth less; a ghillie wrap wears a day out in the field, not in the armory; an item from an old save starts full.

```bash
node tools/hunttest.js
```

Woodcutting (`src/sim/woodcutting.js`, `upkeep.js`, `needs.js`, `orders.js`, `plans.js`, `work.js`, `autopilot.js`, `crews.js`, `trade.js`, `ui/farming.js`, `ui/alerts.js`, `ui/expedition.js`, `ui/ui.js`; or `node tools/woodtest.js [seed]`, about 40 seconds): firewood by terrain, site and hive land, the season and the hour, the yields per woodcutter-hour by terrain (a table, bare-handed and with a fire axe), tools (fire axe > sledgehammer > machete > bare hands, spare tools lent), skill and the hurt, depletion and slow regrowth, the expected haul against the rolls, the *Cut wood* order (the haul up to the cargo room, the card, Heat, injuries, the queue, the plan step and its estimate), the base job (work type, role, the armory's axe, the daily cutting, the base's Heat, the land thinned), heating (firewood first, the fuel for the rest, cold without either; the governor's heating fuel plan counts the wood), a shelter's and a team's fire on bare ground, the autopilot stocking the winter's firewood from late summer (none with `governor.noWood`), firewood prices by the season, the alert, the top bar, the tile and team panels and the right-click menu, and save / load (old saves start with none; mid-order and determinism).

Hunting, snares and fishing (`src/sim/hunting.js`, `orders.js`, `plans.js`, `crews.js`, `autopilot.js`, `ui/ui.js`, `ui/expedition.js`, `ui/plan.js`; or `node tools/hunttest.js [seed] [autopilot days] [autopilot seeds]`, defaults 42, 110 days, seeds 1-6, about 3 minutes): the land (game by terrain: forest 1 > hills 0.8 > farmland 0.55 > suburbs > a city almost none; hive land almost nothing; a base's own tile little), the season (spring 0.8, summer 0.9, autumn 1.25, winter 0.95, × 1.2 with snow: tracks) and the time of day (dawn and dusk best, night poor); hunting weapons by category (hunting rifle best for deer, crossbow and bows silent, with arrows, shotgun for small game, pistol poor, melee none; firearms need ammo; knives only: no hunt, set snares), skill (Shooting 12 > 5 > 1) and the Hunter perk; the expected 8-hour haul by weapon, terrain and season (autumn forest with a rifle and a pistol about 23 food bagged), and 400 rolled hunts matching the expectation of what is kept (what does not fit is left where it fell); Heat (every gunshot costs 1 ammo and adds Heat; a crossbow none; wandering hordes 2 tiles off turn toward the shots); overhunting (a deer takes 0.3 off the tile, half of it back in 20 days, nearly all in 60; six days on one tile bring less each day); snares (wire or 2 scrap, the wire stays with the line and comes back when it is lifted, the catch after 1 / 3 / 10 days against the expectation, nothing stolen in the first 2 days, most of a 10-day catch rotten or taken and some lines lost, lines gone after 30 days); fishing (no rod or net, no fishing; a net worked by two about twice a rod; a deep river bend > a river > a pond; rolled against expected; spring best, winter worst; a frozen river needs a crowbar, machete or axe and gives half; fish run out and come back in about 10 days); carcasses (30 food of game weighs 30 kg: a heavier band and a slower pace on foot than the same food packed, nothing on a car's riders, bikes take part of it; a lone hunter keeps what he can carry); spoilage (a quarter of fresh game lost in a hot summer day in the field, almost nothing in a freeze; eaten before packed food; at a base fresh game spoils faster than other food, drying racks take it first, a hunting cabin smokes 6 into 5 preserved, eaten first); the hunting cabin (none in a city, a Hunt job for 2 with one, the defaults Hunter role and Hunting party crew, crews' hunt and fish jobs; two cabin hunters through an autumn bring in about 11 food a day with ammo used and the land hunted down; a fisher with a rod on a river); orders and the planner (a hunt with no hunting weapon fails with the reason, hunts kept within 2–24 hours, the Hunting / Fishing trip presets; 40 whole trips from the hub: out and the 8-hour hunt to the hour, home exactly as the team panel said after the hunt, the planner's food enough, a trip to hunted-out land to the hour for the whole plan, the plan's ETA within an hour on average and its expected haul matching what the trips bring); save / load mid-hunt and the same hunt twice playing out identically; the tile panel's *Game* and *Fish* lines with a yield estimate, the team panel's Land block, the planner's preset and expected haul, the right-click *Hunting trip* / *Fishing trip* and every button handled; and the autopilot in a lean year (half the starting food, no preserved food) with hunting off (its test switch `governor.noHunting`) and on, Balanced and Cautious: seeds 1–6, 110 days: Cautious without hunting 11 hunger days, 3 deaths of hunger and a fallen holdout, with it none (70 hunting and fishing trips); Balanced no hunger either way (49 trips, 3 cabins, 532 food from cabin hunters and fishers). The cabin hunters never use a base's last 3 ammo a head + 10 (kept for the walls).

Foraging (`src/sim/foraging.js`, `orders.js`, `plans.js`, `work.js`, `autopilot.js`, `ui/alerts.js`, `ui/hardcore.js`, `ui/expedition.js`, `ui/ui.js`; or `node tools/foragetest.js [seed] [autopilot games] [days]`, defaults 42, 6 hardcore games, 40 days, about a minute): wild food by terrain, site and season, the yields per forager-hour (a table), the foragers (skill, the Forager perk, the hurt, hands on one tile), depletion and regrowth, the expected haul against hour-by-hour rolls, herbs, honey and the bad mushroom, the *Forage* order and plan step, foraging in camp, the base job *Forage*, the autopilot (a hungry base forages, a team short of food gathers it, hardcore's road team), the alerts and panels, save / load mid-forage and determinism, and hardcore's lean start with foraging on and off.

The worm (`src/sim/worm.js`, `storage.js`, `hunting.js`, `parties.js`, `orders.js`, `staging.js`, `autopilot.js`, `ui/health.js`; or `node tools/wormtest.js [seed]`, about a second; the dice are pinned to 0 or 1 where a rule is under test): catching it from unboiled water on infected ground at a base but not with a water boiler (free to run; with an upkeep set it burns it and goes cold without), a water filter, on clean ground, from rain alone or at a shelter with its fire lit; on the road water packed at a base with unsafe water, drunk unboiled, cleaned by a filter carried, boiled by a camp fire (faster with a stove); raw meat eaten last at a base with no cook fire, cooked by a cook fire or a kitchen, the racks taking it first, a team eating the clean game first and the raw only as a last resort, a camp fire cooking it; infested animals (a quarter of the food, always with eggs, at the tile's infested share, one fewer in the herd each, a herd hunted out, clean land never infested, fish never "infested", the hunt estimate lower with a herd), a herd seeding its tile up to the cap, wandering, dying off, being born on badly infected land and never above the map's cap; camping only above the threshold and only while camped; the stages (hidden, gut: work, food and health; brain), spotting (a medic sooner than nobody; a team not before the gut stage's 7th day, then the card with its choices, the dewormer choice when one is carried; the brain-stage card with the purge disabled), the cures at a base (dewormer, failed and working purges, once a day, surgery only with a staffed Clinic, the night's care using a dewormer first then a purge, nobody treating what nobody has spotted), turning (at a base, locked away, the base card, on the road), the old random card gone, the autopilot building a water boiler, the boiler's upkeep (0.2 firewood a head a day, firewood then fuel, unlit and unsafe when the base cannot pay, counted in the firewood plan, an outpost's covered from the hub, nothing burned with a filter), searching infected sites (scaled by the eggs, nothing below 10% infection, rolled when the day turns or at home, washed by a lit fire or safe water at home, the planner's warning), crops and forage (a harvest on infected ground carries eggs, dry grain does not, forage at the base and in a team's cargo, eaten after the clean food and before the raw meat, catching it, cooked by a cook fire and a camp fire, dried after the meat), the other groups (the loss rate and the type multipliers, the boiler flag after the first loss, the news and the chronicle, never below 4, clean ground, a party on a herd's land) and save / load of the new state (a carrier's worm, the base's meat, crops and forage with eggs, the cold boiler, herds, stats, a team's unboiled water, meat, forage and hands, a group's losses and boilers). Since the week-long course (2026-10-06): the 7-day ramp (3, 6, 10, 15, 20, 24, 30 health, 2 … 8 mood a day; full health dies on day 7, hurt sooner; no healing while infected), a dewormer on days 1–4, surgery only from day 5, spotting from day 1, clean-ground un-purified water at `RAW_BASE` (about 0.9 cases per 10 people per 30 days), prisoners, guests, holding crews and your people held by groups catching it, every death-and-rise place (the base, locked away, the road card's put down / leave / crush the skull, alone, an errand, a holding crew, a crawl block's "One of you turned", a battle unit becoming one of the dead on its cell and floor, a battle hour's damage, a group's base held or visiting), the tablet order (on the move, a lit fed camp fire, a camp with nothing to burn, a filter first; at a base only with the boiler cold for want of firewood and no filter), the upset stomach (its odds, its own name, no health, it passes, meds shorten it), save / load mid-course and an old save's stages moved onto the new count. Since the half-healing change (2026-10-06): half the healing while infected from each source (rest at a base, a hospital bed, a treatment, camp rest, field treatment, a prisoner's rations; a holding convoy and a battle's patch-up by their code), the death day at full health (day 8 resting at a base, day 9 in a Clinic's bed with a medic, day 10 at best), prisoners and guests spotted with a log line and listed, a guest's dewormer (+8 goodwill) and failed purge (+3, once a day), surgery needing a Clinic, a prisoner's purge (+12 opinion) and release (+3 goodwill more), the night's care leaving them alone until *Treat prisoners and guests too* is on, the autopilot turning it on for guests; the boiler's burn order (firewood, then the allowed junk with its log line, then fuel; cold with nothing: the tablets, the filter first, else un-purified), the marks and the toggle (a weapon only when marked, *Keep* never, the toggle off), the wood plan keeping the boiler's firewood, breaking down (an armory item, metal refused, the bulk button sparing *Keep*, worn things refused, a team's book feeding its camp fire on bare ground) and firewood in the loot (a cabin's search hours, a barn's shelves, never a hospital). Since the huge-map census (2026-10-07): the autopilot gets the sick to a cure (an outpost with no dewormer and no meds sends them walking to the hub, cured there by its dewormer; with no food for the road the hub sends meds and the outpost purges it; nothing once the last gut night has passed, nor from a base that can cure it) and puts a queued boiler first with a builder at an outpost drinking un-purified water. `node tools/deathcounttest.js` checks the census tools count a worm death once (its rise line is not a second death).

```bash
node tools/discoverytest.js 42 365 3
```

Hidden sites (`src/sim/discovery.js`; or `node tools/discoverytest.js [seed] [autopilot days] [runs per style]`): every LOCATIONS type has a rule (public / road / secret), the road rule (a countryside site by a road is public, one more than a tile from any road hidden), towns, cities, landmarks and Guard bases public, no hive named after a hidden site; before it is found a hidden site is not drawn by the real map renderer (none of the unknown hidden sites' names drawn with the map centered on each, while a public site nearby is), the tooltip and the tile panel show bare ground, no *Scavenge / raid* in the right-click actions, it is not among the planner's nearby sites, tileLabel names the ground and the autopilot's and crews' site score refuses it; Dev Reveal draws it and hides it again; a team that has seen it knows it (and its nearby-sites step may pick it) before the hub does. Each discovery route puts it on the map with exactly one log line and it stays there: seen from a base, a team's sight radioed in, a team's sight brought home (not on the hub's map before), radio news, an explorer's map, intel paid for, a prisoner questioned (their camp on a hidden site), the coalition's reports, a retrieve job's pick-up place. Save and load keep the found sites and announce nothing again (an old save without `foundSites` announces nothing on load); the same seed finds the same sites in the same order with the same log. Then [runs] autopilot campaigns of [days] days per style: no team is ever sent to scavenge a hidden site the player has not found (raids on found ones are counted), and how many hidden sites are found by day 30, 90 and the end, near the hub (within 12 tiles) and on the whole map. Seed 42, 365 days, 3 seeds a style (Normal map, about 34 hidden sites each, 5–6 of them within 12 tiles of the hub): near the hub 41% found by day 30, 71% by day 90, 98% by day 365; on the whole map 14%, 32% and 58%; none raided before it was found. Before the Survey mission (`src/sim/survey.js`): 35%, 71% and 86% near the hub, 9%, 28% and 55% on the whole map.

```bash
node tools/surveytest.js 90 2
```

The Survey mission, rumours and optics (`src/sim/survey.js`, `src/sim/optics.js`; or `node tools/surveytest.js [autopilot days] [seeds per style] [first seed]`, about 3 minutes at 90 days): the hourly odds of spotting a hidden place and how each part moves them (Scouting 2 / 10 / 18, three people ×1.16, farmland against forest, a tile away against two and four, night ×0.35 and a flashlight ×0.5, fog ×0.5 and a thermal's ×0.9, a car ×0.6 and bikes ×0.8, stealthy against rushed, binoculars ×1.25 with a tile more reach, the best optic in full and a quarter of the next, a spotting scope only while still, the cap, a rumour ×3 for its place only with the survey centred in its circle) and the planner's word (three scouts on 2 tiles for 24 hours "good", one novice by car at night on 4 tiles for 6 hours "poor", with the reasons); the route (the survey's hours long, every spot inside the circle, one tile a step, back at the centre on the last hour, the same twice, stealthy covers fewer tiles); a survey in the field (the team panel's "Surveying 3 tiles around …: 14 of 30 hours, 1 place found", a hidden site found and logged with *Survey team (…)*, still on the map after a save, a load and two days, a team out of radio range knowing it before the hub); Heat building on a survey, road encounters on the tiles it walks into (against camps of the same hours) and hive land drawing the dead; 30 survey trips (on foot, on bikes and by car, every stance, 2–4 tiles, 8–36 hours) against the planner: home on the estimated hour, the estimated food and water and fuel when no road card delayed them or changed their load (a card during the survey costs survey time, not trip time), and the team panel's queue estimate while it surveys; rumours (the circle holds the place off its centre, the vague "somewhere north of …", fading after 20 days, struck off when the place is found, none without a source, more with travellers at the gate than a radio alone, travellers' news about half the time, the world's random numbers untouched); optics (five items with weight, value, description, effect and their sources; the military and raiders value them; binoculars +1 sight tile and seeing the other side first more often, a thermal more at night, a thermal in the off hand sees like night vision in a night battle, a telescope in the armory widens a base's view, a survey packs the hub's optics and other trips a pair of binoculars); crews with Survey at priority 1 (out with the hub's binoculars, home with the trip in their log), the autopilot's surveys, survey targets that are the same with every unknown hidden site removed (no peeking) and no team sent to scavenge a site nobody has found; a save mid-survey that plays on the same, the same survey twice in one process, and a right-click survey for a team out there. Then how fast the autopilot finds the hidden sites within 12 tiles of the hub with and without surveys (`state.surveyOff`), Cautious, Balanced and Aggressive. Seeds 52–53, 365 days (36 sites near the hub): without surveys 39% by day 30, 61% by day 90, 97% by day 365; with them 44%, 61% and 97% (25 surveys). The autopilot's hunting, fishing and water trips already roam the land near home, and its surveys wait for spare hands above a defensive floor (without the floor: 58%, 75%, 97%, but Aggressive lost its hub early on two seeds of four). Before those trips existed: 44%, 58%, 83% without surveys and 69%, 78%, 97% with.

## Controls

| Input | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Left-click | Inspect a tile (a tile with one of your teams in contact opens its team panel) |
| Right-click | Actions for a tile, or move a waiting team there; also 📌 Pin the base, group, hordes or teams on it, and 📝 Add note / Edit note |
| 📌 (survivor screen, team panel, Groups and Bases tabs, tile panel) | Pin it to the Watching panel at the top left of the map (again to unpin); click a row there to go to it |
| Note editor | Pick an icon, type the text; Enter saves, Esc closes |
| Hover | Description of any button or option |
| Drag / wheel | Pan / zoom |
| W A S D or arrow keys | Pan the map |
| Space | Pause / run. A new game starts paused (a banner at the top of the map says so; when a pause rule stopped the clock, it says why); orders given while paused start when you unpause |
| 1–5 | Speed 0.5× / 1× / 2× / 3× / 5× (an in-game hour takes 40 / 20 / 10 / 7 / 4 seconds; a day 16 / 8 / 4 / 2.7 / 1.6 minutes). At 3× and 5× the clock drops back to 1× by itself when a card (one that stops the clock or one for the tray) or a new red alert arrives (Options: *Drop to 1× when something happens*, on by default) |
| H | Center on hub |
| I | Infection overlay |
| B / P / G / T | The Bases / People / Groups / Teams tab on the left |
| Tab or N | Next team waiting for orders: centers on it and opens its team panel (Shift+Tab goes back) |
| F1–F9 | Jump to your bases (F1 the hub, then the outposts in the order they were founded) |
| Ctrl+1–9 (or Alt+1–9) | Save the camera view as spot 1–9 (Alt for browsers that keep Ctrl+1–9 for their own tabs) |
| Shift+1–9 | Go back to a saved camera spot (1–5 alone are the speeds) |
| J | Diary: every day of the run (also the 📖 Diary button in the top bar and the log); J or Esc closes it |
| R | Roster: everyone in one table (also the 👥 Roster button); R or Esc closes it |
| C | Codex: every kind of the dead, item, building, illness and group you have met, with its numbers and how to deal with it (also the 📚 Codex button); C or Esc closes it |
| Ctrl+K or / | Search (also 🔍 in the top bar): a box in the middle of the screen that finds people, bases, teams, groups, known sites, the buildings you can build at the selected base, the menu, Help topics and codex entries by a few letters; ↑ ↓ pick, Enter goes there, Esc closes. Empty, it shows your last picks |
| E | Ledger: production and use of each resource, stock, people, mood and Heat, per base or for all, over 30 / 90 / 365 days or the run, and the run summary (also the 📈 Ledger button); E or Esc closes it |
| M | Minimap on / off (also its – button, and ▣ Minimap in the same corner when it is off; remembered). Click or drag on the minimap to look there |
| Enter / Esc | In the question an order that cannot be undone asks first (and the menu's questions): yes / cancel |
| [ / ] | Fold or show the left / right panel (or click the slim bar between it and the map; drag that bar to make the panel wider or narrower) |
| L | Fold or show the log (folded, its strip shows the latest line; drag its bar to make it taller) |
| Alt+[ / Alt+] / Alt+L | Close the left panel / right panel / log completely, strip and all, so the map takes the room; again to bring it back |
| ✕ on a folded strip, or ◧ ◨ ⬓ in the top bar | The same close, by click; the top-bar button (dark while the panel is closed), the slim tab at that edge of the map (⟩ ⟨ ▴), Alt + the panel's key, the panel's plain key, or picking something it shows opens it again. Remembered like the folds |
| Shift+[ / Shift+] | Fold or show the running orders / the alerts on the map (folded alerts are one button with the count, red when one is urgent) |
| \ (backslash) or ▣ in the top bar | Map only: fold every panel and box over the map; again to put them back as they were |
| Window edges, corners, title | Drag to resize or move a big window (base screen and its Work tab, survivor, trade, radio, caravan, inventory and loot, planner, route, diplomacy, memorial, the menu with Help, diary, roster, new run); double-click the title for its usual size and place. The timelapse's two boxes resize from their corner |
| ` (backquote) or Dev button | With *Show dev tools* on (⚙ Menu → Options → Display; off by default, and the Dev button is hidden until then): dev tools: reveal the map (no fog of war; display only, your intel is unchanged), infection overlay, 10× / 50× / 200× fast forward (Space goes back to your last normal speed, never to a dev speed), *Show FPS* (frame rate, frame time and simulation ticks per second in the map's corner), and a tracker of each group's population, bases and land plus zombie hotzones, hives (count, resources and income, Wallbreakers waiting), hordes, the hive build sites (dead gone in of 1,000, gathered mass, score), the next build-site and border-horde days and the hive record (built, destroyed, attacks held, break-ins beaten, bases overrun), with the change over the last 7 days |
| Shift+P (or Dev tools → Autopilot) | Autopilot on / off, with *Show dev tools* on (P alone is the People tab): an AI governor runs your holdout (pick Cautious / Balanced / Aggressive in the dev panel). Pairs well with Reveal map and fast forward |
| Esc | Close the top window, one layer per press (inventory, then the survivor screen, then the base); then move mode, then the team panel; with nothing open, the menu |
| ⚙ Menu (top bar, or the battle panel) | The menu: Save, Load, Save and exit, Exit without saving, New campaign, Quit to desktop (desktop app), save files (Export, Save to file, Load from file), Options (autosave and how often, confirmations, pause when the window loses focus, sound volumes and mutes, *Pause for…* (the pause rules and the drop to 1×), the battle's end-turn question; Display: UI size, map detail and animations, zombie look, colour-blind-safe colours, larger map text, reduce motion, the minimap and its size, show dev tools; Confirmations: the kinds of order you stopped being asked about, with Ask again; what the mouse does), Controls (every key, rebindable) and About. The game waits while it is open. On the new-run screen, *Continue* loads the latest save and *Load game…* opens the list |
| Battle | Click a unit, then a tile to move or an enemy to shoot; Tab or 1–9 select units; F shoot, I reload (1 action: fills the gun's magazine from the team's ammo), E melee, O overwatch, H hunker, M meds, U crush the skull of a body next to the unit (any human body, any time: a Carrier raises the human dead near it at once; the loot stays), J double tap a body in sight (1 action, 1 round, the noise of a shot), P pick up or put down a body (3 tiles an action and no shooting while carrying; a carried body cannot get up), Z lay a carried body in the team's car (a raid's vehicle zone, a field battle's evac strip), R retreat (one unit, from the evac strip), Shift+R or *Sound the retreat* (everyone at once: a confirm in the panel shows each unit's odds, who carries whom and a *Leave* / *Carry* button per downed teammate; Enter gives the order, Esc keeps fighting); Enter ends the turn; Space (or the End turn button) ends it too, but while units still have actions the first press asks in the panel ("2 units still have actions — press Space again or Enter") and the second ends it (*Don't ask again* or the checkbox under the panel switches this off, remembered in the browser); Esc or right-click cancels. Shift+C or *Area clear: finish* ends a fight with only a couple of far-off unaware stragglers left, as a win. On the result screen Enter or Space is *Take all and continue*. Stealth kit: C subdue (a takedown on the unaware or staggering, else a non-lethal blow), T tie up someone knocked out, G throw a bottle / noise maker / firecrackers (then click a tile), Shift+D drag a body, someone knocked out or a downed teammate (click it, then where to). Search, quick search and drag are on Shift (Shift+S, Shift+Q, Shift+D) because W A S D pan the view. Before a break-in (deploy step): click a defender, then a green cell; *Hold the gate*, *Default positions*, floodlight aim; Enter or *Start the fight* begins it |
| Battle view | Always isometric (the top-down grid is no longer offered). Drag the map (any button), hold W A S D or the arrow keys, or push the mouse to the edge to pan; wheel or + / − zoom (9 steps); , and . turn the view a quarter; V centers on the selected unit; B roofs on / off; in a building with several storeys PageUp / PageDown (or the view bar's Floor ▲ ▼) show the floor above / below, selecting a unit shows its floor, clicking the ▲ / ▼ marker of someone on another floor jumps there. A click is made on release (a press that moves pans instead); right click cancels |
| Raid | As in battle, plus Shift+S search the container next to the unit (1 action, everything shows; opens the search window: drag what to take), Shift+Q a quick search (a rummage: no action, once a turn, 30–50% of it turns up, loud: Heat +5 and the unaware within 7 tiles come to look; 6% chance of cutting a hand), N open the vehicle when standing at it, and X extract from a green extraction zone (R explains this instead of retreating); inside a building K peeks through the door or doorway next to the unit (free, once a turn), Q closes the open door next to it, Y braces a closed door, L climbs through the window next to it (with several doors next to the unit, click the one you mean). Next to a locked container or door (padlock marker, brown door) the panel shows *Key* / *Pick N%* / *Force N%* (or *Smash*) buttons with the odds (*Pick N% a try*: 1 action an attempt; hover for how Stealth and the difficulty make the odds). In the search window click a supply stack to pick how many to move (1, −, the number, +, ½, All, What fits), then click where they go; Shift+click sends all that fit straight across; drag moves all that fit (Shift on the drop: half, Ctrl: one) |
| Ctrl+Z | Undo the last change, one step at a time (up to 20), in the trip planner, a base's Construction tab (the build queue) and its Work tab; the ↶ Undo button there does the same. Nothing else (never in a battle) |
| Work tab (a base screen) | Priority cells: left-click counts up (blank → 1 … 5 → blank), right-click counts down, Shift+click changes the role's default for everyone in it; with the mouse over a cell 1–5 set it, 0 / Backspace / Delete clear it, R puts it back to the role's default. The schedule strip: click Work → Guard → Free → Sleep, right-click back |
| Timelapse (game over or dev tools) | Space play / pause, ← / → one day, Home / End, 1–4 speed 1 / 2 / 5 / 10 days per second, I infection, T territory, F fit the map, drag / wheel / WASD pan and zoom, click a caption to go there, Esc close |

Every key above (but Esc and the Work tab's number keys) can be changed in **Menu → Controls**: the keys by group (Time, Map, Panels, Windows, Teams, Bases, Camera spots, Battle, Battle view, Timelapse…); click a key and press the new one (Esc cancels), + adds a second key, ✕ takes one off, Reset / Reset all. Two actions on the same key where both work (the map, a battle, the timelapse) are flagged in red, and only the first answers until one gets another key. The keys are kept in the browser with the other options, not in the save. **Help → Keys** is the same list as a key sheet, with a 🖨 Print button that prints only the sheet. The table above lists the default keys.

The top bar keeps the clock and weather, the stock, the head count, 📖 Diary, 👥 Roster, 📈 Ledger, 📚 Codex, 🔍 Search, Help and ⚙ Menu (and the panel buttons); UI size (75–200%), the zombie look and the readability options are in ⚙ Menu → Options → Display. Map shortcuts do nothing while a window is open (a card, the planner, a base, Help) or on the new-run screen; only J, R, C and E work in the diary, roster, codex and Ledger (they close it, or switch from one to another), and the search box (Ctrl+K) opens over any window but a card. Help (top bar) opens the menu's Help tab (`src/ui/help.js`): the game as it is now, one topic at a time (the help's paragraphs plus Map layers and Map legend), with ◂ / ▸ to step through. Small ? buttons open it at their topic: the Layers box (Map layers), the paused banner (Time) and the right panel with nothing selected (Map).

Windows and panels (`src/ui/layout.js` rules, `src/ui/windows.js` on the page): a folded panel gives the map its room (the canvas is measured again and the camera kept on the same land; one redraw). A folded panel opens by itself when you pick something it shows: a tile, site, base or team on the map, a team or tile from a list, an alert or a log link open the right panel; a tab key (B P G T) the left one; a new urgent (red) alert opens folded alerts, except in map-only mode. The log never opens for a new line. *Auto-open panels on select* in Options switches this off. Folded panels, panel widths and each kind of window's size and place are remembered in this browser (Options → *Reset window sizes* puts them back); window sizes are kept in rem, so they follow the UI size, and are clamped to the screen. Help → *Windows and panels* lists all of this.

## Hardcore mode

Chosen on the new-run screen (**Mode**: Normal or Hardcore; `src/sim/hardcore.js`, which dispatches to one module per part). A normal game is unchanged: the same seed builds the same world and plays the same run. A save remembers its mode (the save list and the menu's *This game* line say which); a save without one is Normal.

- **The timeline:** Hardcore day 1 is the day the State of Calder falls; the normal game begins 6–12 months later (game months: a game year is 112 days), when people have built their hubs. Each hardcore world sets that point by its seed: `state.hardcore.eraOffset`, days 56–112.
- **You** (`src/sim/hardcore-start.js`, numbers in `HC_START`): three survivors on the road, no hub, one car between them. **Starting people** (new-run screen, Hardcore only; `hardcore.js START_PEOPLE`, `createWorld({ startPeople })`, kept as `state.startPeople` and in the save; a hardcore save from before it loads as three): 1–4 flee together, 3 by default (the start below; the same seed with 3 builds the same run as before). Each one rolls their own grab, so the team's own food and water scale with the head count (1–3 days each, `TEAM_MIN`..`TEAM_MAX` per person; food by background, below); the car, what is in its back and a prepper's bag stay one of each (one car fled, one bag was packed), so one or two have more of those per head, four less, and fewer hands carry, scavenge, build the camp (the same materials) and fight slower. **One or two** (`HC_START.SMALL`, 2026-10-07): so a solo or a pair is not several times deadlier than three, for each seat short of three (two alone, one for a pair) everyone on the team gets +1–2 in Scouting, Stealth and Medicine (finding food, slipping past trouble, patching yourself up: +2–4 alone) and the car's empty seats were loaded too, 3–6 food, 3–6 water and 1 meds a seat on top of the trunk (alone: 6–12 food, 6–12 water, 2 meds); the orders card and day 1's log say so. Three or four draw no dice for it, so a default start is the same start. On the road a small team also meets fewer armed strangers, and fewer of them (wanderers.js `HC_SMALL`, under People on the move). A normal start is a full hub of six and does not take it (the select is greyed out with the reason). They spawn on open land (no site on the tile), away from the outbreak's origin and hives (14+ and 8+ tiles), a city centre (3+ tiles; its blocks can be close by), the military sites and the other groups, out of the map's outer fifth, with a town, a city or any other site within 5 tiles (somewhere to drive to on day 1; 8 relaxed), and a river or lake within 6 tiles (a camp has no spring; 10 relaxed) (relaxed a step when no tile qualifies). New-run crew edits apply to the three (the leader too; a leader picked from the normal crew's last three falls back to the first). What each grabbed as they fled is rolled: half fled with a bag (a school backpack, a sling bag, now and then a hiking pack) holding 2–5 things (a flashlight, a bandage kit, chocolate, a novel, toilet paper, batteries, a bottle, now and then binoculars or a water filter); the others with only their clothes and 0–4 small things in their pockets (a phone, lighters, family photos, a flashlight, candy, cigarettes). A weapon from the house for about 65% with a bag and 40% without (mostly a kitchen knife or a bat; a crowbar, a pistol, a machete, now and then a revolver or a hunting rifle), a gun with a few rounds (3–12). Food and water: 1–3 days each (3–9 of each for three), a bag holds more; **food by background** (`ARCHETYPES` `food`, 2026-10-07): a farmer grabbed 2–5 with a bag (1–2 in pockets), a hunter 2–4 (1–2), a trucker 1–4 (1), and they lift the team's food cap to 5, 4 and 4 for their head; a veteran 1–2 (0–1), a nurse, a mechanic or a cop 0–2 (0–1), who ran with ammo, a vest, meds or tools instead; the others the common 1–3 (0–1). Weighted over the backgrounds a head averages about the common grab (+0.1 food), so a team of three carries about what it did and a fourth brings food by who they were; maybe a bottle of pills; no fuel, scrap or parts beyond what is in the car. **Who they were** (`HC_START.ARCHETYPES`, 2026-10-06): each of the three is drawn, no two alike, from twelve backgrounds (prepper, veteran, college student, office worker, blue-collar worker, nurse, farmer, hunter, mechanic, police officer, trucker, teacher; weighted). A background bumps two to four skills (a nurse +4–7 Medicine, a veteran +3–5 Shooting, a mechanic +4–7 Mechanics...), pushes a fitting trait 60% of the time when a slot allows (a veteran Brave or Tough, a hunter Keen Eye, a nurse Former Medic), and shapes what they grabbed. **Balance** (2026-10-07): every background's skill bumps average 6–8.5 points (before: 5 for an office worker, a blue-collar worker or a trucker, 9 for a hunter), and the ones that bring the most kit get the fewest: a veteran (+3–5 Shooting, +1–2 Leadership and Scouting, a vest 35%, a hunting rifle less often, 1.5× rounds) and a cop (+2–4 Shooting, a vest 50%, 1.5× rounds) carry less than they did; an office worker (+1–2 Scavenging, Keen Eye likelier than Bad Back, a first-aid kit and maybe pills), a teacher (+1–2 Medicine, a school first-aid kit, maybe pills), a trucker (+3–5 Scouting, +0–2 Shooting for the revolver in the cab, Night Owl likelier), a blue-collar worker (+3–5 Mechanics, a tool to hand 70% of the time), a student (+1–2 Scouting) and a farmer (+0–2 Shooting for the rifle) more; the prepper's packed bag turns up less often (weight 0.9, was 1.2; car odds 1.3). It shapes what they grabbed: the bag (a veteran an assault pack or rucksack, a student almost always a school backpack, an office worker a sling bag half the time), the weapon (a cop a pistol with double the rounds and a vest, a hunter a rifle or a bow, a tradesman a crowbar or a fire axe), and the small things (a student's laptop and candy, a trucker's pocket radio and coffee, a nurse's bandage kits). The prepper keeps the packed duffle bag above. `s.background` holds it; the People tab shows it as a chip. **Customize and custom backgrounds** (2026-10-07, user request; not with Ironman): the new-run screen's *Customize* box sets any skill level 0–20 with no point budget and picks traits (up to 3, never Brave with Coward), and in Hardcore a *Background* select on each colonist picks who they were: the dice (what was drawn), a preset, or a custom background. Picking one builds the world again on the same seed (the first pick gives the crew dice of their own, so the map and groups stay put from then on; the others keep who they were); that colonist's skills and traits start over from it. *Backgrounds…* opens the editor (`src/ui/backgrounds.js`, kept in this browser under `brokenstate.customBackgrounds`, at most 24): name and blurb, skill bumps (0–10 each), traits (up to 3, always given and first), what they wear and hold (hands, off hand, body, head, arms, legs, bag), up to 12 carried things (a tool to the off hand, small things to the four pockets, the rest to the bag; it warns what would be left behind and shows the kg), the supplies on them (food and water on top of the team's clamped 1–3 days, like a prepper's bag; ammo, arrows, meds), the car (odds 0–10 that it is theirs, 0 never; its kind; fuel, food, water, ammo, meds, scrap and parts and up to 10 things in the back). *From a preset…* starts from a preset's dice made exact (the middle of each roll, its likeliest bag, weapon and things). A custom background draws no dice (`src/sim/custombg.js` makes it safe; `hardcore-start.js` `archetypeOf`, `pickArchetypes`, `rollCustom`); the world keeps its own copy (`state.customBackgrounds`, saved), the chip is marked ✎. **Ironman**: the crew starts as rolled. Ticking Ironman turns Customize off, greys out Backgrounds… and every skill button (moving points too), and if the crew was customized (`s.startCustom`, `s.bgPicked`) builds the world again with only the names, looks and leader kept (the crew's original dice); Start refuses an Ironman run with a customized crew. A run started customized has `state.customStart`. `node tools/custombgtest.js` checks it (about 20 s). **The car** (`HC_START.CAR`, `rollCar`): one of the three fled in their car, whose by their background's odds (truckers, preppers and tradesmen likeliest, students least): preppers and blue-collar workers mostly pickup trucks, a prepper now and then a Hummer, students, office workers, nurses and teachers old sedans or SUVs, veterans trucks or Hummers, cops SUVs, truckers and mechanics work vans (`vehicles.js`: the SUV, the Hummer and the work van are new, drawn and priced as a truck where nothing says otherwise).
