Its tank is 50–100% full, its condition 55–90%, and what the owner kept in the back is the team's cargo: a farmer's harvest and jars, a trucker's food and jerry cans, a tradesman's tools and lumber (scrap and parts), a prepper's gear and rounds, a nurse's meds, a student's laptop, an office worker's lunch. The team sets off in it and it parks at the first hub when the camp is founded. The run opens on the team's orders card, which says who each was, what they grabbed, and whose car it is and what is in the back. You are with the team: it always takes orders and you see live around it (as if in radio contact), and the map centres on it. People met on the road can join it.
- **The first home: camp → shelter → base** (`src/sim/staging.js`, `buildings.js` SHELTER and hub_up): your home grows in three stages. With no base, a team on a land tile gets the order **Establish base** (it replaces the old instant founding). It pitches a **staging camp** there (one at a time; not on water, a hive or another group's land): a site on the map in your color (a tarp shelter, crates and a fire; then the first stakes and the posts of a shack; the shack half boarded and the barricade on both sides; the shack roofed and the barricade all but closed, with a flag), with a bar of the work done. The camp lists what the shelter takes: **15 scrap, 2 parts, 10 food and 10 water** (10 scrap less on the ruins of a burned base): the old cost of the hub (30 scrap, 4 parts, 15 food and 15 water, 108 person-hours) is split over the two steps. The team unloads into the camp's stores (the same order at the camp: *Unload and work on the camp*): all its scrap and parts, and its food and water beyond two days of rations (down to one day while the stores are short); it can leave to scavenge and come back as often as it likes. Then the **work**: 60 person-hours of daylight work (07:00–19:00), each worker 1 an hour + 3% a Mechanics point (× health and the Handy / Master Builder perks, as base construction): **under 2 days for three people**, about 5 for one alone, 1 for six (more than six add nothing). The work goes on as far as the scrap and parts in the stores allow (half of them: half the work) and stops there with a report; the shelter stands once the work is done and the stores hold everything. Nobody at the camp, no progress; at night the workers rest (the hurt heal as in a camp); they eat their own rations and from the camp's stores only once theirs run out, and a team at the camp takes food and water for the road (up to 2 days) from what the stores hold beyond the needs. Food and water go in up to twice the need (the rest stays with the team and joins the shelter's stock when it moves in). **Water must be found:** the camp has no spring. Beside a river or lake the people there drink from it and haul 10 a day into the stores (nothing while nobody is there); anywhere else every drop is carried in (*Fetch water* at a shore, water found searching), and a worker team with no water left and dry stores stops and says where the nearest water is. The order's text, the camp's log line, its card and the tile panel say *Water: river 1 tile away* (or 2–3 tiles, or *No water source nearby*). **Camp danger** (`fieldheat.js` CAMP_HEAT): everyone at the camp shares its Heat, made of what the camp does, per hour: camped there 0.3, 0.12 a person, the **fire** 0.5 by day and 1.4 at night (its light carries; a smokeless stove ×0.6), 0.5 a car and 0.7 the pickup parked there (and +0.12 danger each: raiders want them), **shooting** at or next to the camp lately 0.1 a shot (every fight's shots go on the map; a neighbour tile counts half; it halves every 18 hours, at most +3), and 0.3 while the building work goes on; × the place (city, suburbs, infection, hive land), stealthy and the Ranger perk, and the camp kit. The Heat **settles**: 3% of it fades each hour, so it levels off near 33 × the hourly gain (three working with the fire lit day and night near 60; the fire out at night near 35) instead of climbing to 100; one roll for trouble an hour for the whole camp (the dead, a horde close by, raiders). The camp's card shows it as *Fire at night: +1.4, 4 people: +0.48, Truck: +0.7, Shots yesterday: +0.6*, where it settles and the Heat now. **The fire** is a toggle on the camp's card (and in the team panel): lit, it keeps the cold off (cold sickness ×0.35), cooks the food and boils the water (no stomach bugs from raw food: 1.5% a person a day without it) and lifts spirits (+0.3 mood an hour resting by it, +0.1 without); new camps start with it lit. **Neglect:** nobody there for 10 days and scavengers may come (35% a day): 30–60% of each store and a tenth of the work gone each time; empty and unworked for 20 days it is gone. A team that dies there leaves the camp standing for the next. *Strike the camp* (at the camp) packs what the team can carry and leaves the rest as a cache on the tile. **Done:** the camp becomes your **shelter** (tier 0, "… Holdout"), bare: no buildings, no truck, the scrap and parts used up, the rest of the stores and everything the teams there carry its stock, everyone at the camp its people, starting jobs by skill as a normal start; a log line (a timelapse caption) and a card mark the day. **A shelter** is a real base you live at (the hub: stores, rest and healing, the armory, people with jobs, the base panel), but small: tarps and a shack behind a scrap barricade (its own map sprite, labelled *(shelter)*), **no wall strength**, beds for 4, 2 crop plots (a garden patch, as an outpost), **1 tile of vision**, **no well** (beside a river or lake its people haul 10 water a day; else rain catchers and what teams bring in; water holds 40), and room only for **2 rain catchers, a drying rack, a barricade and one farm plot** (no walls, towers, wells, bunkhouses or anything else). In a base battle it is the base defence with only its bunk shack behind the barricade on a small yard map, so it holds back far fewer of the dead. Its Heat is the base's (people, workshops) with its fire (1 by day, 3 at night), its parked vehicles (1.5 a car, 2 the pickup) and shooting nearby (0.25 a weighted shot, at most 6) in place of the hub's flat 3; its fire is a toggle in the base panel (lit: warmth indoors, boiled water, +0.5 mood a day; out: the tarps are as cold as outside, dirty-water sickness doubles, and cold nights cost 1 mood). The base panel shows *Camp → [Shelter] → Base*, what the next step takes and the button: **Build up into a base** (Construction; shelters only): **18 scrap, 2 parts and 96 work-hours** by the shelter's builders (about 2 days for two). Done, it is the hub as it always was (a main house, the dug well's 12 water a day, the cellar, wall strength 3, 3 tiles of vision, beds for 4 and every building). A camp from a save made before the tiers becomes the full hub it was promised. Losing that hub later is the exiles' camp below; later outposts keep *Build outpost* (15 scrap). Before the hub exists the top bar shows the team's food, water, scrap, parts, ammo and meds and the camp (built %, what it needs); the Bases tab has a *Your first hub* card (with no camp: what it takes and the best spots within 4 tiles; with one: its stores against its needs, built %, the materials' limit, workers and days left, days alone, threats, the stage and what the next one takes, its water, its danger by cause and the fire toggle); the Goals card starts with *First: found your hub* and the camp's state; the alerts warn when the team's water or food runs under a day and a half, keep the camp's missing materials up, and flag a horde within 2 tiles, Heat 60+ or raiders close (camp in danger), nobody there for 3+ days, and everything in with nobody at work; the camp's tile panel has its card and the map tooltip its line; the tile panel measures the walk from the team and rates the tile as a home (sites around, and *Water: river 1 tile away* or *No water source nearby*); the planner says there is no hub to send from.
- **Autopilot** (`src/sim/autopilot.js` roadAct, campAct): on the road it pitches a staging camp at the best spot within 4 tiles (water decides most: beside a river or lake +6, 2 tiles away +1.5, none within 3 −2; then sites around, few of the dead; with no spot beside water within 4 it looks out to 9 tiles for one) once it carries 30% of the scrap or from day 3, then ferries: it searches the best known sites for what the camp still lacks (pulled toward the camp; water and food first when under two days), hunts or fishes when food is what is missing and the land promises 4+ rations in 8 hours, fetches water at a river or lake, rests when someone is badly hurt, leaves behind what the camp does not need, takes in people it meets while its food lasts, and goes back to unload when it carries everything missing, half the missing scrap and parts within 6 tiles, a full load, or when the camp has materials and nobody at work; there it works until the materials run out. While the hub has four or fewer people one person minds it, the leader goes out too, and a trip leaves the hub a day of food each and takes the rest. At the camp a team short of food or water takes what the stores hold beyond the needs. A 20-game sample (`node tools/hcstarttest.js 20 60`: seeds 9000–9247, Balanced, the hardcore outbreak) pitched the camp on days 1–3, all 20 beside a river or lake, had the shelter in 19 of 20 on days 3–12 (median 5, the hub of old was median 6), after 1–4 deliveries, and built it up into a base in 18 (days 7–33, median 17); 18 of 20 still had people alive on day 60, none died of thirst (the one that never finished its camp starved on the road, short of parts and food). At a shelter it queues Build up into a base first (saving for it), takes water runs with whoever it can spare, and puts the fires out on mild nights. Exiles (8 normal worlds after day 20 with every base lost to a garrison too strong to retake) pitched at once and had their new hub 1–10 days later, all of them alive. The governor gathers fast (it knows the loot tables and never pauses); a careful player will likely take longer.
- **The outbreak** (`src/sim/hardcore-outbreak.js`):
  - **Day 1:** one hive, in a city: the first hive rises among the blocks of the city nearest mapgen's outbreak origin (open ground within 5 of its centre, city and suburb blocks first; 25 more resources than a young hive, so it has a guard within days), and the infection starts there. Every hive mapgen placed is taken off the map (their tiles are where the first year's hives rise). Your three never start within 14 tiles of that city (10 relaxed). The military base's hive comes later (below). With no military group, a second hive starts at another hot spot half the time. Infection is 0 everywhere but within about 4 tiles of the city's hive.
  - **The first year** (until the era offset, numbers in `HC`): each tile keeps the infection a normal start would give it as its goal (`t.infGoal`) and climbs toward it: a front widening out from the hives, the cities and towns filling first (each on its own day, sooner near the outbreak, cities before towns; the dead pack them at 0.55 / 0.4 or more early on, settling back to their normal level), open country slowest; tiles below their level rise up to 0.03 a day faster. By the normal start every tile is at its normal level. New hives rise at the spots a normal start has them, each as a build site on its own day (the origin's cluster first, at 12–50% of the year; the others at 30–80%), standing in 12–20 days, until the state holds a normal start's count. Sample seeds: land infection 0.01–0.02 on day 1, 0.08–0.15 on day 30, 0.19–0.29 on day 60, normal (0.20–0.29) by day 90–112; hives 1–2 → 5 by day 112.
  - **The eras** (`eras.js rampDay`): every ramp keyed to the year or the game day (the tech tree's tiers and special dead, bigger hordes, the outbreak beyond the border and its floor, the year victories open) counts from the normal start, so hardcore day N ramps like normal day N − offset. The hives' own new-site cadence begins after the offset and border hordes at half of it.
  - **The military base's hive:** 1–3 days after the military's strike force leaves for the city (below), a mass of the dead gathers 3–6 tiles from the base (a radio call: "The dead are massing…") and builds a hive in 8–14 days while the garrison is thin; while the mass is short of what the hive still needs, the dead of the surroundings drift in (up to 40 a day), so the garrison's shelling delays it but does not stop it. Once it stands it grows faster (+3 a day) and sends hordes at the base 2–4 days later, then every 6 days down to every 3, each sized from what would break the thinned garrison (0.3× at first, 1× at 70% of the way to the overrun day, 1.4× by it). The overrun day is set by the seed at days 24–48, never sooner than 10 days after the hive stands. Destroying the hive (or its mass) stops it. The Goals card shows "The fall · the outbreak is spreading (N days to go)".
- **The loot** (`src/sim/hardcore-loot.js`, `HC_LOOT`): the buildings are only half picked over: every site starts with twice what a normal start leaves (`tile.loot` × 2, the normal level kept in `tile.lootNormal`), and the surplus wears off by the same share a day (the panic looting nobody tracks) to a tenth by the day a normal game would start (`state.hardcore.eraOffset`), and on toward nothing; searching a place takes from it as always. The raid map's richness cap is 2 in hardcore (1.2 in a normal game). More people are on the move in the first weeks (People on the move, below).
- **The other groups** (`src/sim/hardcore-groups.js`, numbers in `HC_GROUPS` and `HC_MILITARY`):
  - **Bands:** every group but the military starts as a band on the road with no base, 14–34 tiles from you near a site its type likes. It is already what it will become: traders, a commune, a fortress, zealots or expansionists (never raiders). It carries more than you, by type: traders fuel, meds, parts and preserved food; a commune about 110 food, tools and jars; a fortress 12–17 people and about 150 ammo; zealots 16–22 people; expansionists about 170 ammo. Bands walk in daylight, scavenge a site on the way, eat and forage, lose people to hunger and hordes, and found their hub at a site their type prefers (factions.js `PREFERRED_SITES`) on a day between 8 and 29 by the seed (one still on the road 10 days later settles where it stands). Founding makes it a normal group with the same name, colour, people, stores and goodwill. A band is not in `state.factions` until then (it lives in `state.hardcore.groups.bands`), so diplomacy, radio, trade, wars, the coalition's land and conquest never see a group without a base. A band you see is drawn as a party; a team of yours within a tile gets a card (trade news, give 5 food, ask a friendly band for help, go on); the Groups tab lists bands met *On the road*.
  - **How the military fell** (the hardcore era's story of it): the military always exists in hardcore: its base on a National Guard site, 12+ tiles from you when possible, 36–46 soldiers with plenty of ammo, known from the start; it neither expands nor grows.
    - *The strike:* on a day from 3 to 12 (by the seed) it sends 50–65% of its soldiers (keeping at least 13) with their share of the ammo against the hive in the city, as a column of trucks drawn on the map like a group's convoy where you can see it (driving by day; the tile panel and the Groups tab say where it is). At the city it fights 2–4 days by the numbers: its guns cut down 5–9 of the dead a soldier a day, the city's own dead first (500–900, more where the city is thick with them, 60–140 more pouring in each day), then the hive's guard (at most 60% of it) and 2–5 of the hive's stores a day; it loses 50–70% of its soldiers in all (more against more of the dead), and the soldiers who fall join the hive's guard as armoured dead. It fails: the hive stands, weakened. The survivors fall back to a site 3–8 tiles from the hive and are cut off: they hold out there while the base's hive stands (60 days at most) and only go home when it is gone. Radio lines through the fight, a timelapse caption when it leaves and when it fails, and a card each time when you are in contact (a radio at your hub, a friendly Guard, or your people within 8 tiles or seeing the column).
    - *The garrison* fights the hive by its base: sorties against the free dead within 6 tiles (30% a day; never a hive's guard or the mass building one) and shelling every 10–16 days (the hive's stores and guard, or 8–16% of the gathering mass before it stands). Down to 85% of the garrison it asks you for help (40 ammo: harder sorties for two weeks; 6 meds: 2 soldiers back on the walls); at 70% it sends you its civilians. It falls when the garrison is down to 65% of what stayed (8 at least), loses the base or is broken: the base becomes a ruin full of the dead (a horde of the fallen in body armour, infection, the armory's loot left behind), a last radio message, a card and a timelapse caption; 10–25% of the last defenders die and 1–3 soldiers reach you as recruits.
    - *The splinters:* the military is gone, and what is left of it becomes groups of its own, each a band on the road that founds its hub 8–20 days later somewhere defensible (its type's preferred sites, 10+ tiles from any hive): the garrison's survivors as the base's **Holdouts** ("Marrow Guard Holdouts", a fortress, with a fifth of the base's stores) and the strike force's stragglers as the **Remnant of the 3rd Battalion** (or "of Bravo Company"; military, with the ammo they have left). Fewer than 5 join the other; a handful in all make for a peaceful group or come to you. Soldiers on the road lose fewer to the dead than other bands and a horde never leaves them too few to found. A splinter counts as desperate when the raiders' day comes, so the Holdouts may turn to raiding. Destroying the hive by the base saves it: the strike force's survivors come home.
    - Sample (16 seeds, the player kept fed, to day 110): the strike leaves on days 3–11 and fails on days 7–19 with 52–71% lost; the base's hive stands on days 17–29; the base falls on days 27–41; two splinter groups on all 16 (Holdouts 5–8, Remnant 6–10 people), both founded their hubs on all 16 (days 36–68); 4 of the Holdouts turned to raiding, one broke up later.
  - **Raiders:** no raider groups, outsider raider bands or new gangs until `state.hardcore.raidersFrom` (0.6–1.0 × the era offset by the seed, about days 35–112). From then struggling groups may turn to raiding (0.4% a day, more when hungry, small or aggressive; the most desperate one within 14 days; at most two this way), and outsider bands and gangs come as in a normal game. Zealots and expansionists do not raid and nobody declares war until 10 days after the last band has its hub (day 60 at the latest).

`node tools/hcoutbreaktest.js` checks the first hive in the city nearest the origin (16 seeds) and your start far from it, infection 0 away from the outbreak, towns and cities ahead of open country, normal-start levels by day 112, the dead massing by the base and their hive standing, its hordes at the base and the fall on days 20–60, the era offset and save/load. `node tools/hcgroupstest.js` checks the bands on day 1 and their stores by type, meeting one, founding by day 30–45, no early raiders, save/load mid-founding, the military's arc on 8 seeds (the strike force leaves in its window with half or more of the soldiers, reaches the city hive, fails with heavy losses while the hive stands, stays cut off; the hive by the base stands; the base falls on days 20–60 on most seeds; ruin and radio message; splinter groups formed and founded; recruits), save/load with the strike force on the road, destroying the base's hive saves the base and brings the strike force home, holding without a hive, raiders after their day, and 45 days of autopilot.

`node tools/hcstarttest.js [games] [days]` checks the start: the spawn rules on 12 seeds, the gear rolls (bags and pockets, valid items in valid places, varied), the low supplies; the staging camp (the order offered and no instant founding, the needs, nothing to build with, one camp at a time, a first trip of half the materials and the work stopping at half, no progress while away, the second trip, the hub it becomes: bare, the materials used, the stores as stock, the finds in the armory, everyone moved in, jobs by skill, the log lines), the work by crew (under 2 days for three, over twice that alone, under 60% with six, built in 1–3 days), Heat while building, the camp looted after 10 days alone, striking it (a cache), a team lost there (the camp stays); the hubless views (top bar, the four tabs, tile panel, team panel, cards, planner, diary, roster, alerts, settings, the new-run screen in both modes) clean before and after two weeks on the road and with a camp (its card, the Goals line, the alert, the map tooltip); the three stages (the camp becomes a shelter: tier 0, small walls, beds, plots and vision, no well, its upkeep per head, the shack-only battle map and lower hold, only a rain catcher, drying rack, barricade and one farm plot, the base panel with the stage, the step-up button, the water source and the fire; Build up into a base by its builders into the full hub); camp danger (the fire lit and out, worse at night, one more person, a truck parked and raiders wanting it, shots at and next to the camp fading over days, the Heat settling, the team panel text, a team's own camp and its fire toggle); water (no spring: a dry camp gets nothing by itself, water carried in goes to the stores, a riverside camp fills them, the tile panel's *Water: …* row, a shelter's river haul and rain catchers); saves before, during (the camp, its workers, its fire and its target come back, and the loaded world finishes it at the same hour) and after founding (the shelter and its fire), and an old save's camp (no tier: it becomes the full hub); and the autopilot pitching a camp (beside water in most games), founding a shelter in most games within four weeks and building it up into a base (default 8 games, 40 days). `node tools/hardcoresmoke.js` creates a normal and a hardcore world and runs five days. `hcstarttest.js` also starts 1, 2, 3 and 4 people (each that many on the team, the food and water 1–3 days each, the default 3 the same run as leaving it out, out-of-range counts clamped, the count through a save) and `uitest.js` checks the select (four choices, greyed out for a normal start). Its section for 2026-10-07 checks the food by background (each in its own ranges over 300 rolls, the food backgrounds above the common grab and the armed or skilled ones below, the weighted mean about the common, the team's food cap, fours with a farmer, hunter or trucker carrying more a head), the small teams (the skills bumped and nothing else, no dice drawn for three or four, the seats loaded within their ranges, the card and log lines, the load, a save) and the road (the small-team rule for one and two only and only in hardcore, the band sizes, the one-stranger card and its fight, 4000 road rolls on city ground: fewer aggressive meetings alone and for a pair, as many people met in all).

## What is in it

- **Minimap** (`src/ui/minimap.js`, M): the whole state in the map's bottom-right corner, as far as you know it (Reveal map in the dev tools shows all): land bright where you see it, dimmer where you have a report, dark where you have none; water and roads; the Territory, Infection and Coalition layers tinted like the map when they are on; your bases (blue squares, the hub bigger) and teams (yellow when waiting for orders, faded at the planned position out of radio contact); the hordes (faded where last reported) and hives you know of; other groups' known bases with the Territory layer; the camera's frame. Click or drag on it to look there. Small / Medium / Large in Display, in rem so it grows with the UI size; remembered. Cheap: a pixel block per tile in one small image, rewritten only where tiles changed when the hour, fog, layers or colours change (at most twice a second), dots gathered twice a second, the canvas redrawn only when something or the camera moved (at most 30 times a second); hidden, one check a frame.
- **Readability** (⚙ Menu → Options → Display; `src/ui/display.js`, `src/ui/palette.js`): *Colour-blind-safe colours* (good / bad as blue / orange instead of green / red everywhere: moods, stock, odds, health, friend and foe through the CSS variables, and the canvas code reads one palette table: battle hit chances, health bars, threat lines, extraction zones, the map's Infection layer and hive land in purple), *Larger map text* (names, badges and crew tags on the State Map ×1.4, apart from the UI size), *Reduce motion* (map animations off, teams jump tile to tile instead of gliding, no pulsing hives or sieges, no CSS animation or transition anywhere). *Show dev tools* brings back the Dev button and the ` key (the Shift+P autopilot too); `window.game.dev()` opens them for a session (tools, the desktop perf driver). Map animations Off is now remembered across reloads (it was lost).
- **One confirmation style** (`src/ui/confirm.js`): every order that cannot be undone asks first in the same box over everything, with the consequence spelled out, Yes / Cancel and Enter / Esc, and *Don't ask again* for that kind of order (remembered; ⚙ Menu → Options → Confirmations lists the kinds stopped, with Ask again): executing or releasing a prisoner, pulling a building down, breaking gear worth more than junk down for firewood, attacking a caravan or robbing merchants, joining or leaving a war, expelling a coalition member, giving up a job, ending a trade route, deleting a supply run or a work role, digging a growing crop under. The menu's own questions (load, exit without saving, new campaign, delete a save, save over…) are the same box; the leaving-with-progress-not-saved one can be stopped there (the option *Confirm before leaving a game*). Battles keep their own safety: "Space again" to end a turn with actions left, and the retreat and fall-back panels with each unit's odds.
- **Procedural State of Calder** (64 × 42 hexes on a Normal map, `src/sim/mapgen.js`): terrain, 3–4 rivers, roads, an outbreak origin with infestation hives (plus a second hot spot in the other city), and about 185 sites in four zones. New map per seed.
  - *World sizes* (`src/sim/worldsize.js`, chosen on the new-run screen; the numbers below are for a Normal map): everything that comes with land is scaled by area: towns, countryside sites, landmarks, highway stops, rivers (by width), the hives (about 2 per 1,000 tiles), how often border hordes arrive and new hive sites are picked (and how many can be built at once), and how far from the hub the groups settle (by the square root). Bigger maps are for longer games: raid reach is the same 30 tiles on every size, only 5 (Large) or 4 (Huge) groups start within it (the rest come into reach as they expand), and the hub is placed at least 28 (Large) or 34 (Huge) tiles from the outbreak origin, so about as many hives start within striking range of it as on a Normal map (5.3 and 5.4 within 45 tiles, Normal 4.9); over two years (30 autopilot runs each) about half the runs fall on Normal, Large and Huge alike (Huge was 63% before). Normal is the original map: its seeds and old saves build exactly what they did. A save remembers its size (one without it is a Normal map).

    | Size | Hexes | Tiles | Cities | Towns | Sites | Hives at the start | AI groups (default) |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Small | 48 × 32 | 1,536 | 2 | 7 | ~128 | ~4 (3 at the origin) | 3–6 (4) |
    | Normal | 64 × 42 | 2,688 | 2 | 13 | ~186 | 5–6 | 5–10 (7) |
    | Large | 80 × 52 | 4,160 | 3 | 20 | ~282 | ~9 (plus a second outbreak of 3) | 7–14 (10) |
    | Huge | 100 × 66 | 6,600 | 4 | 32 | ~419 | ~14 (5 at the origin, two more outbreaks of 3) | 10–18 (15) |

    Headless, a day takes about 15–20 ms on every size with the autopilot (the cost follows the hordes and groups, not the tiles); a Huge map is generated in about 120 ms. On a Huge map trips are longer (vehicles and outposts matter more), the groups raid you less (they sit farther out) but more hives are within reach of your walls, so more hordes come.

    *Custom size (experimental):* the size list's last entry, *Custom…*, takes any width × height from 32 × 24 to 200 × 140 hexes. Everything the presets set by hand (cities, military bases, extra hot spots and their hives, groups near the hub, how far the hub sits from the outbreak) is worked out from the area so it gives the presets' own numbers at their sizes; a custom 64 × 42 map is the Normal map. The screen warns that custom sizes are not balanced like the presets and that maps bigger than Huge may run slowly (it says how many times Huge it is). Measured headless: 200 × 140 takes 4–6 s to generate (each change of width or height builds it again) and about 25–35 ms a day with 50 groups. Saves remember a custom size (the save list says *Custom map*).
    *AI groups and starting hives* (any size): the AI group count is 0–50 on every size (the tip gives the range that suits the size; a new size still picks its default). Each group needs a site of its own 8 hexes from the others, so on a small map not all of them fit: the screen says how many found room. *Starting hives* is *Default* (what the map makes, shown in brackets) or 0–10: fewer are taken from the outbreak origin outward (the origin keeps its cluster, the lone hot spots go first), more are added one per hot spot in turn. With none, new hives still rise near the border. Hardcore always has the National Guard, and its outbreak still starts at one hive: the count is how many the first year grows to.
  - *Cities:* Calder City in the south (a cluster of about 50 city and suburb tiles) and a smaller second city far from it, packed with urban sites, several of a kind and named by district ("Calder City North Precinct", "Calder City Eastside Auto"): police stations, auto shops, pharmacies, supermarkets, gun stores, hardware stores, gas stations, apartment blocks, offices, schools, fire stations, warehouses, a hospital and a mall each, a university, a clinic, churches. Rich, but danger is high (more of the dead).
  - *Small towns:* 13, spread out. Each has a police station and an auto shop next to its center, 0–2 other shops (grocery, pharmacy, clinic, diner, hardware, gun store, gas, church, school, motel, feed store), and 1–2 satellites a few tiles out (gas station, diner, motel, church, feed store, clinic, farm). Moderate danger, a little poorer than the same shop in a city.
  - *Countryside:* farms, barns, ranches, country churches, cabins, hunting lodges, campgrounds, ranger stations, radio towers, quarries, crash sites, plus truck stops and gas stations on the highways. Quiet and poorer; many sit off the road.
  - *Landmarks:* at least one National Guard base (sometimes an Army depot too) off-road in the hills or woods, away from settlements and the hub; a state prison, a dam, a regional airport near Calder City, factories near towns.
  - Highways join every city and town (a spanning tree plus shortcuts where the roads made a long detour); city and town sites have streets to them. The hub starts in a small town away from the cities, the outbreak and the military bases; AI groups start outside the cities, spread apart (at least 8 tiles), mostly within 30 tiles of the hub (23 on a Small map, 37 on a Large one, 47 on a Huge one).
  - On the map, each site is a pixel sprite of its type on a plate in its zone's color (fading in steps by the loot left as you last saw it: rich, some, little, picked clean; with a row of skulls under its name for its danger when zoomed in; where the name is not shown the skulls sit under the sprite); zoomed out, small sites are dots and only cities, towns, landmarks, group bases and your own bases keep their names (overlapping names give way to the more important one); you can zoom out until the whole state fits. The tile panel and tooltip show the site's zone, danger and likely loot, and the loot left as you know it: *now* in view, else what you last saw (or heard on the radio) with its age, and a warning once it is 3 days old (other groups strip sites too).
- **Customizable start (new-run screen):** in Hardcore, *Starting people* 1–4 (above, under Hardcore mode; greyed out for a normal start); per colonist: rename or random name, move skill points (fixed point total), edit or randomize the look, randomize name / skills / look / all; "Randomize all" for the whole crew (names, skills, traits, gear and looks; in Hardcore new backgrounds, grabs and car on the same map); pick the leader; choose the world size (Small 48 × 32, Normal 64 × 42, Large 80 × 52, Huge 100 × 66) or a custom width × height (experimental), the AI groups (0–50 on any size; 3–6, 5–10, 7–14 and 10–18 suit the presets, and a new size picks its default, 4, 7, 10 or 15) and the starting hives (Default or 0–10). Edited colonists are kept when the seed, size or group count changes, with their traits and unspent skill points ("New map and colonists" rerolls them). No two colonists or recruits share a first name while a free one is left, and renaming someone to a full name already in the group is refused. When the run begins the starting jobs are handed out by skill: the two best farmers farm, the best scavenger scavenges, the best shot guards, the best mechanic builds and the sixth farms; the ammo bench starts without an operator. Next to a skill the screen shows what it counts as in play when a trait changes it ("5 →3" for Stealth with Loud). The seed is a whole positive number. The game starts paused: look around, give the first orders, then press Space.
- **Sprites:** procedural pixel sprites drawn in code (`src/ui/sprites.js`, no image files). Survivors are built from their `appearance` (build, skin, hair style and color, facial hair, top style and color, pants, accessory) plus worn armor and weapon; edit it from the survivor screen ("Customize look"). Zombies come as walkers, runners, bloated and brutes (by strength: weak dead are walkers, badly infected ground brings runners, bloated ones and brutes); enemy humans wear raider / soldier / zealot kits in their group's color. On the State Map (`src/ui/mapsprites.js`) everything is a sprite too. Your teams: a pickup or sedan (the actual vehicle) for motorized parties, walkers with packs on foot (armed for raids, scouts with binoculars on recon, a relief squad behind a banner), a rowboat or a raft with the vehicle (and a foam wake) while crossing water; trade-route convoys haul crates (lime) and supply runs haul drums (teal), by truck or pulling a handcart; on a ring in the team's status color, facing the way they travel; a faded sprite with a "?" badge marks an estimated position. Status badges are pixel icons too (waiting "!", wrench for work, radio contact, crossed swords for a siege). Other groups' parties look like their group: raiders on bikes and in a spiked pickup, a military humvee and helmeted squads, robed zealots with a banner and torches, an expansionist armored truck and shield troopers, a trader caravan (covered wagon, pack mule), a commune tractor hauling hay and pitchfork militia, fortress spear-and-shield militia; raids stand on a red-tinted ring and a siege pitches a camp (tents, barricade, fire, banner) beside the base. Sites: one sprite per location type (about 40, from the city skyline and the town water tower to the police station's light bar, the pharmacy's green cross, the gun store's rifle sign, the red barn, the radio mast and the smoking crash site), on a plate in the zone's color, faded when picked over, with danger skulls up close. Hordes are a crowd of shamblers (2–7 figures by size, in the zombie palette, facing where they drift) with their size on a badge, stale ones as faded ghosts with "N?"; hives are a festering mound over a dark pool with glowing pods, bigger and redder on badly infected ground, pulsing while in view, their land tinted dark red and the ring around them brighter with a red skull on every tile; a hive being built is a raw mound of the dead with bones sticking up and a red bar for the dead gone into it (of 1,000); a Wallbreaker is a grey brute with a slab of concrete for a fist at the head of its horde (`src/ui/hivesprites.js`, which also paints the two bosses for the battle map: the Wallbreaker and the bloated, bone-crowned Broodmother with her egg sacs). Markers: a grave (with the gear still lying there), your abandoned truck or sedan (dusty, a red jerry can when it needs fuel, smoke when badly damaged), a rusted wreck (up on blocks once stripped), a cache of crates and a duffel (opened once emptied), an abandoned camp (collapsed once picked through). Bridges are railed decks along the road. The **Zombies** button (top bar) sets palette, gore, variants, eyes and clothes, saved in the browser.
- **Map animations** (`src/ui/render.js` anim, Settings > Display > Map animations, on by default): the State Map's sprites come alive by motion and small overlays drawn each frame (the sprites themselves stay single cached canvases). Teams and parties on the move bob in step (yours, other groups', outsiders, hardcore bands), a cart a little slower; a vehicle on the move rumbles and raises dust; the crossing boat rocks; live hordes shamble (a sway and a heavy bob, more while they move; last-seen ghosts stand still), with the Wallbreaker at their head; infested herds graze; campfires flicker with sparks and smoke (a team's camp, the staging camp, a shelter's fire pit) and a siege camp smokes; a base with people in it smokes from the hearth (a shelter from its stovepipe); a crash's smoke column keeps rising; a hive build site under way glows; a hive's growths swell; the waiting badge bounces and the radio badge sends out a ring. While anything animated is on screen the map draws about 15 frames a second (otherwise only when something changes, 4 a second, as before; a live hive or a siege still pulses at 40); off, everything stands still. The timelapse never animates.
- **Sound** (`src/ui/audio.js`, cues in `src/ui/soundcues.js`; ⚙ Menu → Options → Sound; Help → *Sound*): every sound is synthesized on the spot in WebAudio (oscillators, filtered noise, envelopes; no audio files). *Effects:* a soft click on buttons; a card chime by urgency (routine: two light rising notes; warning: a falling minor third; crisis: a low tense sawtooth chord with a thud; a raid on a base is the alarm instead); a bell for a new warning or danger in the alerts (an alert is heard once: a new count is not a new alert, one that went for under 12 hours and came back is not heard again, one turning from warning to danger is); in battle a report per gun class (pistol, revolver, rifle with an echo, pump shotgun with its pump, assault-rifle burst of three, suppressed, bow, acid spit), a thud for a hit and a whiz or ricochet for a miss, melee thumps (a crunch on a critical), a whoosh for a melee miss, bites and groans of the dead (more often the more of them are up), screams, a pipe bomb's or grenade's blast, a Molotov's glass and whoosh and crackle, smoke's hiss, a flare; a body falling and a dropping tone when one of yours goes down, a low minor chord when one dies; two drum beats when a battle opens. *The map:* a low grinding sting (a thud and a minor-second brass swell) when a horde you can see is newly within 6 tiles of a base or 3 of a team (once per horde); a hammered alarm bell for a raid warning, a break-in alert or a battle at your own base. *Ambience* follows the weather and the clock where the map is looking (the battle's tile in a battle; read through `weatherAt`, so a regional sky works): wind with gusts (stronger and higher in storms and blizzards, still in fog), rain and a storm's low roar, thunder every 12–40 s in a storm, a low drone fading in at dusk (19–21) and out at dawn (5–7), birds on fair days from spring to autumn (a dawn chorus; quiet in a battle), crickets on warm nights; the title screen has wind and the drone. *Options:* sound on / off, Master / Effects / Ambience sliders (squared, so the low half is usable; defaults 70 / 80 / 50%), *Mute when the window is not focused* (on by default), *Hear* any one sound to set the volumes; kept with the other options. Browsers start sound only after the first click or key press; muted or in the background the audio is suspended (no CPU). A volley is cut to 5 sounds a frame (the loudest kinds first, your own shots first) spread 70 ms apart, each sound has its own gap and voice limit (`LIMITS`), and no more than 28 play at once. On autopilot the cards are answered at once and do not chime.
- **Bases (Manage):** the hub and outposts share the same rules: roles (farm, scavenge, operate, build, guard, rest, medic, craft, teach, read), Heat (people, plus 3 per workshop station with an operator at work), defense, beds, healing, morale and desertion. Farmers tend the crop plots (see *Farming, water and food*). Under a week of food left, everyone goes on short rations (60%, costs mood, not health). A base that runs out of food starves: everyone there loses health every day it lasts (6, then 8, 10 … up to 20 a day; armor and Tough do not help) and nobody heals until there is food again, so about a week without food kills. Stock never goes below zero. Trade-route escorts waiting between trips live at the hub: they eat, heal and can be treated there, but do no base work. If the leader dies you pick a new one from the six best free people by Leadership. The run ends when nobody is left alive: deaths, desertion and a captive changing sides all count. Tabs for overview (with a food forecast line: days of food left and the next harvest, and water per day, stock and days left; the Bases list shows the same), farming (see below), workshops (base scavenging, stations with operators and running status), buildings and their bonuses, a construction queue with builders and timers, an armory with everyone's equipment (skill-gap rule shown), and vehicles.
- **Construction plots and water barrels** (`src/sim/buildings.js` PLOTS, WATER_BARREL, demolish; `economy.js` waterCapacity / rawRoom / waterLayout; `storage.js` crates; `node tools/plotstest.js`): every building stands on a plot, and the plots grow with the base (building / utility): a shelter (the camp stage) 0 / 2, an outpost 6 / 6, the main hub 16 / 14. Building plots take anything with a roof (bunkhouses and barracks, the storehouse, cellars, the cold store, cisterns, workshops like the machine shop and preserving kitchen, the radio station, garage, cells, greenhouse, common room, chapel, hunting cabin, the hospital: one plot whatever its tier); utility plots the small things in the yard (the water boiler, water barrels, storage crates, the watchtower, the cook fire, rain catchers, drying racks, wells, the ammo bench, med station, generator, still, the radio relay). Walls, defences and farm plots need none. A queued building holds its plot. **Beds** (`BEDS`, `baseBeds`): the hub 4, an outpost 2, a shelter 4, a **bunkhouse** +6 (20 scrap, 30 h, up to 6) and a **barracks** +12 on one building plot (40 scrap + 2 parts, 54 h, up to 4), for a base whose plots run short; a standing bunkhouse can also be **expanded** into a barracks where it stands (On the building plots: *Expand*; 24 scrap + 2 parts, 30 h, no new plot, its 6 beds in use meanwhile; the job counts against the barracks' 4 and the bunkhouse cannot be pulled down while it runs). A broken bunkhouse or barracks sleeps nobody until it is repaired (`breakdowns.js`: raid damage; repaired for 8 scrap, 14 for a barracks). *Demolish* (two clicks) pulls a building down to free its plot and salvages half its scrap and parts. **Water barrels** (up to 4, utility plots): each is upgraded on its own (water barrel 25 → barrel rack 50 → water tank 100) and set to hold purified or un-purified water: un-purified barrels give the well, rain and river water more room; purified barrels keep room only safe water may use (the filters' surplus, bottled water brought home), never crowded out by the well. **Storage crates** (up to 4): +30 dry food room each. The Construction tab shows the plots, the queue, what stands on each kind of plot (a bunkhouse with *Expand*), the barrels (tier, what they hold, Upgrade, Demolish) and the build list split into Buildings, Utilities and Walls, defences and fields. Old saves with more buildings than plots keep them; nothing new goes up on that kind of plot until some come down.
- **Expeditions:** trip planner (crew, vehicle, bikes or on foot, stance, supplies with round-trip estimate). Packed supplies count against what the crew can carry (by weight, see *Weight and load* below): a launch over the limit is refused, as is a crew member who is dead, away or already on a team, or an outpost on water. Food, water and fuel drain hour by hour (water at each day's thirst: 1.3× in summer, at least 1.6× on a hot day, 0.9× in winter; the planner counts each hour at its own day's rate, so a trip into a heat wave packs more); running out hurts (armor and toughness do not help against hunger and thirst). A team keeps the name it left with. If the last member of a team dies or leaves, the team is lost: what it carried lies at a death site on that tile and its vehicle stands abandoned there, both marked on the map; a team that loses people drops what the rest cannot carry on the spot (also recoverable).
- **Order templates and Repeat trip** (`src/sim/trips.js`, `src/ui/trips.js`): the planner's *Templates* box saves the setup as a named template (*Save as template*; the same name again updates it): mission, stance, retreat policy (the box's *Retreat* list, set on the team when it leaves), the crew by id with each person's job and best skills, the vehicle, supplies, tablets and lights, the plan's steps and conditions and the Survey settings. Click a template in the box to apply it to the trip you are planning; the Teams tab lists them (rename, delete with two clicks, *Plan →* the target it was saved at, *Pick target…* for the next trip you plan). Applying one keeps everyone fit and puts in a stand-in for anyone dead, away, hurt (health under 60), in a hospital bed or too ill: the best fit person at the hub with the same job, else the same best skills; a missing vehicle becomes one of its kind (else on foot); *Move to a site* steps planned from another target are dropped; supplies are packed as saved, as far as the hub has them (the shortfall is flagged), and the planner's usual warnings check them against the new trip. Every change is listed in the box. A team sent from the planner remembers its setup: when it gets home an alert (🔁, for a day) and the Teams tab's *Recent trips* (the last 6) offer *Repeat trip*: the planner on the same target with the same mission, plan, supplies and people (stand-ins as above), one click on *Send team* to confirm. Saved with the campaign.
- **Undo in the planners** (`src/ui/undo.js`): ↶ Undo (or Ctrl+Z on that screen) takes back the last change, one step at a time, up to 20: in the trip planner (crew, transport, stance, mission, supplies, tablets, lights, retreat policy, plan steps and conditions, a template applied), a base's build queue (queued, cancelled and moved jobs: ▲ ▼ on each job reorder the queue, and the first job is the one being built) and the Work tab (priorities, roles, schedules, minimums, crews' settings, who is in which role). Each step keeps a copy of just what that screen edits. What the sim has acted on stays: a team sent is gone from the planner, and a build step is refused once its job finished, the builders started on a job it would take back, or the materials it would hand back have been used. Never in a battle; the steps are not saved.
- **Pause rules and the card tray** (`src/sim/cardrules.js` kinds, defaults and staleness, `src/ui/pacing.js` the tray, the rules and the speeds; Options → *Pause for…*): you choose which moments stop the clock. Each card gets a kind when it arrives: ⚔ combat and engagements, 🛡 a raid or horde at a base, ✝ a death, 🤝 first contact with a group, ☠ a hostile act or war, 🧟 a horde sighted near a base, 🐎 trade visitors, ❗ team orders needed, 🃏 other cards (road and hub events, people asking to join, perk points, morale, illness, crashes, job offers, reports). All are on at first except trade visitors and other cards. A card of a kind that is on stops the clock as before; the others go into the **card tray**, a strip at the top of the map (newest first; each chip shows its icon, title and the in-game hours it has left, its tip the text and the answer it will take). The clock keeps running; click a chip to answer it (the clock waits while it is open; Esc or *Back to the tray* puts it back). A team whose card waits holds where it is (it still eats). Left `TRAY_HOURS` = 8 in-game hours (2 min 40 s at 1×, 32 s at 5×), a card takes its default answer by itself with a log and diary line ("Left unanswered for 8 hours, "Storm" (near …) took its default: Shelter and wait (6 hours)."): a choice marked `default: true`, else the first "do nothing" answer (OK, Hold here, Leave it, Not now, Decide later, Turn them away…), else one that steps back from trouble (Shelter, Go the long way, Hide, Return home, Pay…), else the first that is not a gamble (no fight, no odds, no risky word); never a greyed-out choice, one that needs the map or a fight. A card with nothing like that left always stops the clock, as do a card over a battle and one whose answers all need the map; a battle, a search, the trade window and a city crawl always stop it too. A card about a team, person, group, visitor, base or job that is gone leaves the tray with a log line; a choice that throws is dropped with a console line. *✓ all* answers the whole tray with its defaults, ⚙ opens the rules. A death, a first contact, a group turning hostile and a new horde or break-in alert near a base stop the clock even with no card (each by its switch; the paused banner says why, and Space then runs at 1×). The "N cards waiting" alert counts only the cards that stop the clock. On autopilot nothing changes (the governor answers every card at once), and the headless tools never use any of it.
- **Speeds** (`src/ui/pacing.js` SPEEDS, keys 1–5 and the top bar): 0.5×, 1×, 2×, 3× and 5× for quiet stretches (an hour in 4 s, a day in about 1 min 36 s). At 3× and 5× the clock drops back to 1× by itself when a card arrives (pausing or tray) or a new red alert comes up, with a line on the map saying why (*Drop to 1× when something happens*, on by default). The loop still runs at most 12 hours a frame and drops a backlog, and the panels redraw once an hour; on a Huge map an hour takes about 1 ms on average to simulate (6 ms at worst in `tools/traytest.js` after a day; up to about 0.4 s with the machine busy), so 5× (one hour every 4 s) holds the frame rate; any hitch is the same as at 1×, only every 4 s instead of every 20 s.
- **Team orders:** when a team finishes a job it waits for orders: act on that tile, move to any tile (click or right-click), return home, or hold. Teams in radio range can be redirected while moving. Giving an order never changes the speed: an order given while the game is paused is armed and starts when you unpause (the pause banner counts the waiting orders, and the *Running orders* list and the team panel show "starts when unpaused"). When the order is done the team reports in with a "Done" card offering the next steps (an order that could not be done, or was stopped, reports in with a plain orders card saying why; a team cut off by water gets "Cannot go home").
- **Trip plans (`src/sim/plans.js`, planner section in `src/ui/plan.js`):** the planner's PLAN section builds the team's order queue before it leaves: the trip to the target with its mission, then steps (look around, scavenge here, scavenge the best sites nearby (up to N within R tiles, richest by what is known, picked when the step starts so the plan adapts), camp N hours, gather, fetch water (2 h on a tile next to a river or lake: every free container is filled, 1 kg a unit, and carried home; also a team panel order there), wait N hours, move to a known site, return home), reordered or removed freely (nothing after Return home). Presets: *Recon and return*, *Scavenge and return*, *Survey trip* (survey the area for the hours set, then home), *Explore* (recon, then the best 2 sites within 3 tiles, then home), *Camp N days, then return*, *Custom*. Missions that come back start with Return home after them. The planner shows the whole plan's time (every leg stepped hour by hour like the trip; searches count 6 hours, Look around 2, a recon's 2 hours are paid by the next move) and packs and checks food, water and fuel for it (plus the way home if the plan ends away), and a Heat look-ahead for camp and wait steps. *If … then come home*: anyone hurt below N health, food below N days, cargo full; checked every hour, the team turns home by itself and a "Coming home" card says why (continue home, resume the plan, or stop). While a plan runs the team raises a card, once each per trip, on a horde (15+) in sight, a rich site in sight (loot 55%+) that is not on the plan and not known to be rich (with *Search it next*), and food short of the way home; each offers continue, change the plan (team panel) or come home. The Teams tab and team panel show the plan, its steps left and its rule.
- **Radio rule:** a team in radio coverage (stations and linked relays) takes new orders at any time. Out of contact it follows its plan (the team panel and *Running orders* say "following its plan (no radio)") and you cannot give it orders, except through the cards it raises: while its orders or report card is open, and for the rest of the hour you answer one (its panel opens with orders then). A team holding out of contact checks in again with an orders card every 12 hours, an open-ended camp once a day. Your own base tiles count as contact.
- **Heat in the field (`src/sim/fieldheat.js`):** a team that stays on one tile (waiting, holding, camping, gathering, building a boat or the staging camp, looking around, crafting, repairing, treating) builds Heat from noise, smoke and smell, per hour: activity (waiting, looking around, treating 1; gathering, crafting, repairs 1.5; boat building 1.8; a camp, and anyone at the staging camp, by the camp danger model instead: its fire, people, vehicles, shooting lately and work, settling rather than climbing; see the first home above) × place (countryside 1, suburbs 1.4, city 1.8, × 1 + 0.6 × infection) × hive land 1.4 (2 on the ring next to a hive) × team size (0.7 + 0.1 a person) × night (a camp fire 1.3, otherwise 0.8) × stealthy 0.8, capped at 100. It falls 8 an hour on the move and is 0 within a tile of your bases. Each hour on the spot the chance of trouble is 12% × (Heat / 100)^1.5 × danger, where danger = 0.15 + 3 × the road's conflict chance there (groups, AI parties, infection, hordes, traffic) + 0.3 city / 0.15 suburbs + 0.3 hive land / 0.8 next to a hive + 0.2 a horde within 3 tiles (cap 2). Trouble is a road conflict card (the dead, a horde close by drawn in as a bigger surge, or people from the groups around; camp lookouts spot it a little more often) and halves the Heat; after it the team goes back to its camp or work. At most one conflict per 20 hours, as on the road. A quiet countryside camp for a night reaches Heat ~25 with about 1% risk; a night in an infected city is about a coin flip and three days there bring trouble every day or so. The team panel shows a Heat bar with the hourly chance and the reasons, the map a small bar under the team's sprite (hover the tile for the text). The autopilot gives up waiting at a site for another team's search past Heat 60.
- **Order queue and team panel:** click a team (map, Teams tab, or the *Running orders* list in the map's top-left corner, which shows each team's current order and time left) to open its panel: people (health, infection, skills, gear), packed supplies and found cargo against carry space, the vehicle's condition and fuel, and the queue. Actions: move, return home, scavenge, look around (2h), camp (8h / 24h / until broken; the hurt heal slowly, infected wounds need meds), roadside repair (1 part, 3h, better with Mechanics), treat a member with carried meds, field crafting, and boat crossings. A waiting team starts an action now; a team on the move (or with queue mode on) adds it to its queue, which runs by itself (e.g. move, then scavenge). A team stopped on the way with orders queued (after a road fight, say) puts the unfinished trip back at the front of the queue, so *Resume queued orders* carries on to the right tile; a queued job on the spot (a mission, look around, gathering) remembers the tile it was planned for and is dropped with a message if the team ends up elsewhere. A camp with no food or no water heals nobody. Arrival estimates (planner, panels, queue) step through the same hour-by-hour movement as the simulation.
- **Boat crossings:** water blocks travel and routes never cross it, but a team on a shore can build a one-use boat and cross one water tile to the land straight across (team panel, the orders card, or right-click the far tile). Cost: scrap = 4 + 2 per person + 1 per 40 kg of supplies and cargo (+14 scrap and 2 parts to raft a pickup, +9 and 1 for a sedan, +1 for bikes, or leave the vehicle on the shore, marked for recovery); build hours = 2 + 0.5 per person + 1 per 80 kg (+4 / +3 for a raft), cut 5% per Mechanics point (up to 50%), then 1h on the water (2h with a raft) with a small mishap risk (a crate lost, someone bruised, the raft vehicle damaged). Hours and materials are fixed when the order is given, so queue estimates hold. *Gather materials* (3h) turns up scrap, only on a shore with a crossing; a shore holds about 10 scrap and gets 1 back every 3 days. A boat order given before the team's vehicle changed (gained or lost one) is planned again when it starts. Delays on cards and the 2 hours of a recon are real hours at any pace.
- **Bridges, barricades and the cars on the road** (`src/sim/combat.js` layRoad / AREAS.roadcars, `src/sim/locks.js` "car", `src/sim/parties.js` bridgeClosedTo, `tools/bridgetest.js`): a road laid over a water tile is a bridge (`mapgen.js`, drawn as a railed deck on the State Map). On its battle and raid maps the river runs across the map, 12–24 cells wide (about half the map: 20 on the Large map a bridge raid or fight always gets), and the road crosses it on a concrete deck between railings (half cover you see over): the only way from one bank to the other, so fights funnel onto it; the ways in and out of a bridge raid are the two ends. Every road map's road is 8 cells wide (6 on a small field map) with the cars people fled in scattered along it in its lanes. **Cars are 2 × 3 cells (a sedan) or 2 × 4 (a van, a pickup, a truck)**, one object drawn across its cells in both views, with a free ring of cells round each (never against another car or a wall, at most against the bridge's railing), and the road is checked to still run end to end past them (cars come back out if not); cars parked around sites and in the suburbs are the same size (fewer of them). On a raid the road's cars are containers (search from next to any of their cells; the marker sits over the middle) (*Cars on the road*: fuel, a bag of food and bottles, now and then a pistol or binoculars); a bare road or a bridge is searched for them alone, a roadside site gets them outside it too. Some are **locked cars** (40% on the road, 20% in a wreck pile): no site key fits; pick the door (lockpicks), pry it with a crowbar (Heat +6, wakes the dead within 8) or **break the glass** without one (both actions, 90%: Heat +10 and the crash wakes every unaware dead and guard within 11 tiles). Locked cars hold more (×1.5, extras: fuel, food, water, rounds, a flashlight or a pistol). **Barricading a bridge:** an outpost may be founded on a bridge tile (*Barricade the bridge*; still not on open water) and is named "… Bridge Barricade". A base on a bridge, yours or a group's, closes the road over the river to everyone but its owner and their allies (your coalition): routes never pass through it, a team already on its way stops at the foot of the bridge and asks for orders, and groups' raids, convoys and caravans must go round. The barricade itself can still be a destination (raid it, storm it with *Assault their base*, trade at it), hordes walk up to it and attack it like any base, and the river can always be crossed by boat elsewhere. Groups barricade bridges too when they expand (RAIDS.BRIDGE_PICK, more for aggressive groups and for bridges near your bases); the log says so. **A barricade's battle map** (`deploy.js`, `conquest.js`): the wall stands across the deck only, the river is the rest of the line, and two staggered rows of **tank traps** stand on the deck in front of it (DEPLOY.TRAP_HP 25 / CONQUEST.TRAP_HP 30): people walk between them, no vehicle drives through, and they are battered down from next to them (click one next to your unit; Melee at a cell). **Hordes** are stopped by a barricaded bridge: their way never runs through one, and a horde whose way needs that bridge turns on the barricade (role attack) and attacks it when it arrives like any base. **Boats** cross plain water tiles only, never a bridged one.
- **The team's vehicle on the battle map** (`src/sim/combat.js` placeVehicle / driveTo, `raidloot.js` atVehicle, `siege.js` loadRam / unloadRam): the car or truck you bring is a 2 × 3 or 2 × 4 object in the entry zone of a raid (a bike stays one cell) and by the west edge of a field or assault battle. **Drive** (both actions, from next to it): click where it goes, up to 8 cells over open ground with nobody in the way (tank traps, railings, other cars and walls stop it); loud (Heat +5 in a raid, the unaware within 10 tiles hear the engine). The driver steps off beside it. The loot window and *Stash* work next to it wherever it stands, and extracting from a zone it stands at keeps it (drive it back; the raid header says where it is). On an assault, the **battering ram rides in the vehicle** instead of slowing three people: *Unload ram* next to it puts it on the ground for a crew to take up, *Load ram* puts a carried or dropped one back in. **Passengers** (*Get in* / *Get out*, 1 action, SEATS by model: sedan 4, SUV 5, Hummer 4, van 7, pickup 5): aboard you are off the map (not a target, not drawn, no shooting), ride along when it is driven, and *Extract* from inside takes everyone aboard out with it from a zone it stands at; everyone is put out on the ground when a battle ends. *Turn* (1 action, from inside or next to it) swings it a quarter at the same corner when the cells are clear. **Ramming** (RAMMING): driving through people hits anyone in the way, friend or foe (14–28, the dead 20–40) and shoves them aside (crushed for more if there is no room); light obstacles in the way are broken (fences, wire, sandbags, crates, hay, barrels, barricades, rocks, pumps, wells, spike pits, bear traps, tank traps) at a cost to the vehicle's condition (1 a person, 2 a zombie, 2–12 an obstacle; tank traps 12), settled on the vehicle when the battle ends. Walls, trees, other cars, water, railings and trenches still stop it.
- **Evac** (`src/sim/combat.js` evacTurn, the *Evac* button in the battle panel): everyone still standing heads for the way out with the actions they have, the extraction zones of a raid or the evac strip of a battle, or a free seat in the team's vehicle, whichever is nearer; arriving, they extract, slip away or get in, and upstairs they make for the stairs first. The order stands: each of your turns starts with the next leg until everyone is out or you press *Stop evac*. The downed are not carried (*Sound the retreat* carries them). **In the vehicle counts as out** (leaveAboard): when nobody of yours is left on foot, everyone aboard drives off with it; a raid ends with them extracted and the vehicle kept wherever it stands, a battle ends in a retreat with them safe (a won battle puts them back on the field). *Extract* from inside works anywhere on a raid map. A **bike or motorcycle** is a 2 × 1 object with one seat; it rides round people and obstacles instead of ramming them.
- **Hordes from 1 to 4 sides** (`src/sim/fort.js` HORDE_SIDES, `interior.js` interiorUnits): a horde attacking a base comes at it from up to 1 side (under 6 of the dead), 2 (6+), 3 (12+) or 4 (18+), how many by its dice: the side it came from on the State Map first with half of it, then the sides next to it, then the far one. On a base without walls the extra sides are the north and south edges of the yard and its back (never onto the way out). People (raiders) still come from one side, a quarter of them round the next.
- **Their vehicles** (`src/sim/aiscav.js`, `conflicts.js` takeTheirVehicle): a group's scavenging party drives out (half the time) when the group has 6+ fuel in stock, using 2: a pickup, a sedan, a van or an SUV, faster on the roads. Meet it in a fight (on the road or at a contested site) and its vehicle stands on the battle map behind them (cover; `combat.js` setup.enemy.vehicle). Win, on the map or by the numbers, and it is left where you fought with a little fuel and the wear of the fight: an abandoned vehicle marked on the State Map for any team to recover (*Recover vehicle*).
- **Base maps by tier** (`src/sim/battlesize.js` BASE_TIER, `interior.js` yardDims): the yard map is square and grows with the base: a shelter 25 × 25, an outpost 40 × 40 growing to 60 × 60 over its first 10 structures, the main hub 64 × 64 growing to 100 × 100 as it fills. A yard over 5,000 cells uses a sparse line-of-sight cache (combat.js SparseLos) instead of a (W·H)² typed array; a save makes a fresh one on load.
- **The fort map** (`src/sim/fort.js`): a walled base on land fights its whole battle on one map, whether it is yours under attack (`defences.js` "baseDefence") or a group's you storm (`conquest.js` "baseAssault"). The map is the yard with the base's buildings (the same generator as before) inside a walkway ring (▲ height 1) inside the wall ring, with a two-cell gate in the middle of each of the four walls, watchtowers at the corners when the base has one, ladders up to the walkway every 7 cells, and a margin of 8 cells of open ground all round with a little cover on it: the base can be attacked from the north, south, east or west. **Staging:** the AI's attackers come from the side they came from on the State Map (a quarter of them round onto the sides next to it) and you deploy your defenders anywhere inside the walls (walkway, towers, gates, yard, buildings) before the fight; storming a group's base, you stage your people on any side of the margin (the deploy step with a green margin), their defenders hold the gates, the towers and the walkway all round. The battle runs through its tiers on this one map: the wall until enough attackers are inside the ring (or any are and nobody holds the wall), then the yard, then (your own base) the last stand in the buildings; there is no fall back to another map. Floodlights light a third of the attack side's margin. A group's base gets a stand-in base for its layout (an outpost or a hub by the strength of its walls, buildings for its numbers, deterministic for the tile). A bridge barricade keeps the old maps (the deck's wall line, then the yard), as does a base without walls (the yard map); a battle's context `strip: true` forces the old maps (the tests use it). The wall map for a bridge keeps 18 columns and takes the yard's rows.
- **Weight and load** (`src/sim/load.js`, panels in `src/ui/loadview.js`): one system, weight in kg. Every item has a weight (`data.js` GEAR `weight`, last on each line; a pack also weighs what is in it) and so does every supply (`SUPPLIES.weight`: food 0.5, water 1, fuel 1, meds 0.1, ammo 0.02, scrap and parts 2, preserved food 0.35, jars 0.4). A survivor's load = what they wear, hold and keep in pockets and pack + their share of the team's packed supplies and found cargo; the team shares that out so everyone is equally burdened (the same felt load, each up to their max), so whoever carries best takes more. Max load 40 kg (Bad Back 28), + the pack's `carry` (improvised backpack +6, assault pack +10), + 8 with Packrat; a team carries at most its max loads together (plus a vehicle). Load bands, the same for everyone, by felt load (× 0.88 with an assault pack: its `ease`; × 1.2 with a Bad Back): **Light** ≤ 18 kg (full pace, normal rations), **Medium** ≤ 28 (−10% pace, +15% food and water), **Heavy** ≤ 38 (−25%, +35%), **Overloaded** above (−45%, +60%). Scouting takes 2.5% a point off the slow-down and the extra rations (half at 20). The team moves at its slowest member's pace; the extra rations count only on the move (resting, camping or waiting: one a day). The team is weighed when it sets off, each hour it waits, and when its people or vehicle change; on the move only what makes it heavier counts at once (eating does not speed it up until it stops), so the ETA, rations and fuel the planner shows are what the trip uses. Cars take the whole load off their riders; bikes take part of it (below). The planner shows each person's load, band, pace and rations and warns when anyone is Heavy or Overloaded; the team panel and the inventory screen show each person's load and band, item tiles and tooltips their weight. New packs only need `slots`, `carry` and `ease`.
- **Vehicles:** own fuel tanks and condition; worn vehicles break down more. Cars: payload truck 650 kg, sedan 400 kg, riders (80 kg each) and what they wear included; fuel per tile (road 0.6, off-road 1.5) × 1 up to 60% full, rising to × 1.3 at 100%, and a car can be loaded to 125% (overloaded: up to × 1.6 fuel, 15% slower, 1.5× wear and breakdowns); the planner shows the load % and the fuel multiplier, and its fuel estimate is the fuel the drive burns. **Bicycles** (`type: "bike"`, a stack of `count` bikes per base): one each for a team, no fuel, roads at 0.75 a tile (walking 1.2, cars 0.5), walking cost off-road and over mountains; each rack carries 15 kg of the team's load and riding takes 55% of the rest's slow-down and extra rations away; they break down less (a flat, a chain) and are patched with scrap; a boat takes them along for 1 scrap; left behind they are picked up again without fuel. Found as cargo (`GEAR.bicycle`, 12 kg, bulky: towns, apartments, motels, schools, campgrounds, police stations, auto shops, hardware stores, fire and ranger stations) or built in a workshop (8 scrap, 2 parts); a bicycle brought home joins the base's bikes. Trade routes, supply runs and caravans use cars only. A vehicle left behind (out of fuel, broken down, no road home, on a shore, at a raid site) is marked on the map and can be driven again when its tank plus the fuel the team carries reach 2. Found cargo the people cannot carry on foot stays inside it (logged, noted on its marker) and is back on board when it is recovered; a team that cannot restart it, or has a vehicle already, can still take what it can carry. Wrecked cars can be restored into new vehicles.
- **Army trucks, the abandoned convoy and found cars** (`src/sim/miltrucks.js`, `src/sim/foundcars.js`, maps in `combat.js` placeMilTrucks / AREAS.miltrucks, UI in `src/ui/carpanel.js`; test: `node tools/miltrucktest.js`): Army cargo trucks, Humvees and MRAPs stand on road tiles (about 1 road tile in 100, up to 45% next to a National Guard base), 3–5 in a base's motor pool, a Humvee at half the army helicopter wrecks, and 2–4 at an *abandoned Army convoy*, an event every 14–28 days (a card, a temporary site on a road that other groups race you for, stripped and burned out after 6–9 days). Each truck is big hard cover with a cargo bed to search (one loot table, `truckLoot`: rifles, ammunition by the crate, armour, grenades, rations, now and then night vision, a thermal sight or a radio; less in a wrecked or burned-out one, less in a base's motor pool), its turned crew of dead soldiers about it and more strapped in the cab who rise when the cargo is opened. Any car or truck on a battle map can be got into (locked: pick, pry or break the glass), its keys found or hotwired (Mechanics), inspected (engine, tyres, battery, fuel: most are dead, a few run, some can be fixed), siphoned and claimed; a claimed one joins the team or waits on the tile, a broken one is repaired in the field (parts, scrap, a Mechanics level, hours on the spot, a check that wastes half the parts when it fails), and a team with a vehicle can send someone home in the found one. A running Army cargo truck carries 2500 kg and seats 12, but is ~1.5× slower than a car (slower off-road), burns twice the fuel and its engine draws hordes.
- **Markers and map layers:** teams roll perception to spot caches, wrecked cars and abandoned camps, marked with how long ago they were seen. Toggle layers (locations, territory, teams, hordes, death sites, vehicles, finds, infection, weather, weather fronts, known area, coalition land) in the Layers box in the map corner: one line each with a color chip and a short hover description; its ? opens Help → Map layers (what each layer shows) and Map legend (every symbol on the map), and ▾ folds it away. **Known area** (off by default): teal hatching on the ground your people looked hard at (`state.searched`, `src/sim/discovery.js`: per tile the day it was last searched, a Uint16Array saved with the game; the spots a Survey covers hour by hour with the team's optics' reach, what a Look around takes in, a site a team searched), brighter with a faint wash for the last 30 days, a thin teal line round it; a team's looks reach the map with its report (radio contact or home), and the tile panel and tooltip say on which day a tile was searched. **Coalition land** (off by default): the land tiles the alliance victory counts (`alliance.js controlledLand`), each in the color of its nearest base's owner (you blue, each member its group color) with a border where another owner's land begins and a thin gold line round the coalition's land as a whole; the layer's line shows the share held against the 50% goal (other groups' land stays on the Territory layer). **Ruins:** a razed base (yours or a group's, `tile.ruin`) is drawn with the Locations as a burned-out compound (charred wall stubs, a lone chimney, rubble; a torn blue rag on yours) named "Ruins of …", on your map once you have seen it since it burned; the tile panel shows who burned it, the salvage left, the danger and the 5 scrap an outpost there needs, founding one replaces it, and the timelapse shows each day's ruins (`chronicle.js` frames). The overlays are drawn into the map's cached chunks and worked out again only when their data changes (a report of searched ground, a day passing, the coalition's bases or members), never per frame.
- **Tactical combat:** fights open an XCOM-style battle on a grid built from the tile (cover, two actions per unit, hit chances, flanking, overwatch, hunker, melee, meds, line of sight, retreat zone, ambush rules, enemy AI, zombies drawn by gunfire). Survivors and human enemies move 5 tiles an action; the dead move 3, the strong (power 5+, drawn as runners) 4 (review C-10's middle ground); the Wallbreaker and the Broodmother 2. *Critical hits* (`combat.js` `CRIT_MULT` = 2; the chances in `CRIT`) do double damage, on both sides: a shot at someone exposed 25%, at one of the dead in the open 10%, at anyone in cover 5% (+15% with Deadeye), a melee blow 8%, an overwatch reaction shot never; a critical also goes through a dead soldier's or riot cop's armour. Injuries and deaths carry back to the map. After a win the dead are bodies to loot (see Inventory). Every group fields its own people, humans only (raiders, fighters, guards, militia, soldiers, zealots); the dead fight beside humans only where both are found, as at a scavenge site. A sleeping enemy that is hit and lives wakes its group, whatever the weapon (silent kills stay silent). Makeshift gear wears when the battle ends: only on the units that took the field, and only the items they took it with. Battles that start in the same hour wait their turn; one whose team is gone by then is dropped.
- **Magazines and reloading** (`combat.js` magazines: `WEAPONS[k].cap`, `magCap`, `reload`; `tools/magtest.js`): every gun holds a magazine (9mm pistol 15, pump shotgun 6, hunting rifle 5, assault rifle 30 = 15 two-round bursts, pipe shotgun 1, .357 revolver 6, lever-action rifle 8, police carbine 30; × `gunMods(item).capMult` + `.cap` from a fitted magazine, `gunmods.js`) and starts a battle loaded. A shot takes `ammo` rounds from it; an empty gun cannot shoot, double tap or go on overwatch. **Reload** (I, 1 action) fills it, so reload-and-shoot fits one turn. Your side's rounds stay in the team's pools until fired (the magazine is a counter: `ammoUsed`, the raid's pools, auto-resolve and saves are unchanged), and a magazine never shows or fires more than the pools still hold; a reload fills it only as far as they go. The enemy's (and the third side's) guns have magazines too and endless spare ammo: the AI reloads an empty gun before acting, and tops up one under half full when nothing is in reach (your side's AI, the raid bot and auto-played fights the same). A gun swapped in mid-raid comes empty; the same kind of gun keeps its rounds. Bows, crossbows, melee and a spitter's acid have no magazine. Fights by the numbers ignore magazines.
- **Battle view (`src/ui/isoview.js`):** battles are drawn isometric (2:1 diamonds, pseudo-3D like the old X-COMs and Project Zomboid); the old flat top-down grid is no longer offered in play (kept only for the headless tests). Everything is procedural pixel art in the State Map's gritty palette, painted at 32 × 16 art pixels a tile and scaled up nearest-neighbour: ground textured by surface (grass, fields, dirt, gravel, asphalt with faded paint, floorboards, concrete, rubble, water, mud and blood round the hive's flesh), walls of brick, plaster, wooden siding or block with windows and doors, the base's log palisade and gate, fences, sandbags, barbed wire, barricades, crates, cars and trucks (along the road), trees, rocks, hay, pumps, shelves and lockers, the hive's flesh. Height is real elevation: the wall's walkway and flat roofs one level up, upper floors inside two-storey houses, watchtowers two (the near rail in front of whoever stands there), with ladders and stairs. Units are the battle sprites standing on their tile (shadow, a ring in their side's color, the kind's letter for a hive's special dead, ★ elite), drawn back to front with everything else (u + v, then height); health bars, action pips, status tags and, for every enemy in sight, the selected unit's hit chance float over them. Buildings have roofs (tar, rusted metal, shingles, concrete); a building with one of your people inside, an enemy you see or the mouse inside has its roof cut away and its near walls dropped to stubs (B or *Roofs* in the panel: every building that way), blocks between your people and the camera are faded, and anyone still hidden shows as a silhouette. Fog of war darkens and greys what nobody sees, with ghosts where enemies were last seen; at night the scene is darker and flashlights, fires, flares and floodlit ground light it. Move range, paths, the deploy cells, the evac strip, raid zones and the floodlit area lie on the ground (on a raised floor at its height); cover shields, the line of fire and the hit chance show at the cursor.
