Camera: drag, W A S D, the arrow keys or the screen edge pan, the wheel or + / − zoom (0.75–5 CSS px an art pixel), , and . turn it in 90° steps, V centers on the selected unit (picking one off screen with Tab or a number moves the camera there); a compass shows north. Mouse picking runs the screen point back along its view ray to the frontmost cell it meets (raised floors and blocks; roofs and building walls do not stop it, so pointing inside a building opens it) or the unit sprite under it. Speed: the whole map's ground is one small canvas (rebuilt only when a floor changes or the view turns), fog one more (rebuilt when what your people see changes), every block a cached sprite; a frame is two image draws, the overlays and one sprite per visible block and unit. Measured on the biggest maps (hive 32 × 22, raid 34 × 22, a fully built hub's yard 26 × 28) with a 4K-tall battle canvas (2830 × 2162 device pixels, Chromium on an RTX 3080): a frame takes 0.4–0.7 ms (median; 95th percentile 0.8–4.5 ms, worst 5.6 ms), well inside the 8.3 ms of 120 fps; headless Chrome at 3840 × 2160 draws 300–650 frames a second. The first frame of a battle or after turning the view builds the caches (40–90 ms, once); an action or an enemy step rebuilds only the fog and the cells' looks (a millisecond or two). Map sizes (`src/sim/battlesize.js` SIZES, one table; `registerBattleSize` for a layout's own): four classes, Small 24 × 18, Medium 32 × 24, Large 40 × 30, Extra-large 48 × 34 (a scale 0–3, in between in between). Raids by the site (a hospital, university, prison, military base, mall, factory, airport, the warehouse district or a city center Large; a supermarket, school, police or fire station, apartments, offices, a motel, church, farm, ranch, lodge or campground Medium; a gas station, diner, grocery, pharmacy, clinic, cabin, barn or small shop Small–Medium): the street map at that size, a building's own map (`buildingmaps.js`) its archetype's floor plan with the outside grown to at least the site's size. Fights in the open Medium, growing to Large with the people and the dead on the map (each of the dead counts 0.6), +0.2 on plains and by water, −0.15 in forest, hills and mountains, at a site at least its size less half a class. A hive assault Large (the mound at most 28 columns in, more of its land behind), a group's base Medium to Extra-large by the people living there (their wall at most 20 columns in, their grounds behind), your hub's yard map Large to Extra-large and an outpost's Medium to Large by their buildings (`interior.js` yardDims: the buildings by the yard and round the gate's rows as on the old 16-row map, the grounds beyond them grass, fields and a woodlot; the wall map 18 deep and as long as the yard less 3, the attack's front, breaches, towers and ladders within 6 rows of the gate: `deploy.js` FRONT). Content scales with the area: more buildings and blocks of houses, rooms (2–3 on Small to 6–9 on Extra-large) and their containers (one a 7 tiles of floor), the street raid's dead ×0.8–1.4 and its late waves the same, and every map topped up with props (`densify`: rocks and trees, hay, wrecks, crates and barrels by the ground; never closing a way through) until most open ground has cover within 3 tiles (82%, open plains 72%, a city 86%). The sides start 14–21 tiles apart whatever the size (`CONTACT`), so the first shots come on turn 1–2; the noise's reinforcements come in within 18 tiles of your people. Anti-slog: a raider who breaks and runs is gone once out of sight and 9 tiles off; when at most two enemies are left (a quarter of those at the start), none a boss, all unaware or running, 10+ tiles off and out of sight, the area is clear enough (the player is told, *Area clear: finish* or Shift+C ends it as a win; the battle AI ends it). The camera opens on your people and the ground toward the enemy (1.5–2.5 CSS px an art pixel, a raid 1.5–2; the whole map if it fits at 1.5), again when the map changes (the fall back to the yard); red arrows at the edge of the view point at every enemy seen (or last seen) off screen. Battle length (`tools/sizetest.js`, 10 battles each): Small 11 turns, Medium 11, Large 13, Extra-large 10, none past the 60-turn limit; combattest 0 timeouts in 1,080 (1 before). The battle AI: `distField` (every walker's way to its targets) is a binary heap and cached while the map is unchanged (the same field for every one of the dead in a turn), so the enemy turn takes 1–3 ms on average on every size (the worst 16 ms, a hub siege) and combattest runs 2× faster than on the old smaller maps.
  - *Sounding the retreat* (`combat.js` `soundRetreat`, `escapeOdds`, `retreatPlan`; any battle with an evac strip, on your turn): everyone still standing runs for the strip at once. On the strip with no enemy next to them, or anywhere with no enemy next to them, in sight or close: safe. Otherwise an escape check: 100 − 3 a tile of walking distance to the strip − 25 for each enemy next to them − 6 for each armed person who sees them and 4 for each of the dead (or a brawler) within two moves (those two at most 35) − 30 × the share of health lost − 2 a point of armor worn + 1 a point of Scouting + 10 at night (the hour the battle began), between 5 and 95%. Carrying a downed teammate who lies next to them: −10, and 4.5 a tile instead of 3 (each carries one; by default the one with the best odds carries, and the confirm can leave someone instead). Caught: one blow for each enemy next to them (one if none), 18–32 each before armor; at 0 health they go down and are left behind, otherwise they tear free and get out hurt; a caught carrier drops whoever they carried. A downed teammate carried out, or dragged onto the strip before the last one leaves (also with the per-unit Retreat), comes home like the downed after a win (stabilized: lives; else 50% + 2% a point of the best Medicine among those who got out). The outcome is "retreat" (a defeat if nobody got out) and goes to the battle's handler as before; the result screen lists who got out, who was caught and who was carried. The confirm (and the odds over each unit's head on the map) shows each one's chance, the chance of being lost when caught, the expected number out and who is left behind.
  - *The AI retreats* (`autoPlayerTurn`, `fightOutlook`, `RETREAT_AI`): at the start of its turn it weighs the two sides Lanchester-style (total firepower × total health ÷ armor; a gun counts its hit chance, −20 for cover against people, × damage; melee and the dead 40%) and sounds the retreat when the ratio falls below 0.45 (Cautious) / 0.3 (Balanced) / 0.18 (Aggressive), or when a third / half / two thirds of the team is down and the ratio is under 1. The style is the autopilot's (or `setup.aiStyle`, or the caller's); without one, Balanced. Defending a base on a break-in map that has a way out, every style uses the Aggressive numbers (giving up means abandoning the base). Human enemies already break and run when beaten badly (a third of them left and outnumbered 2 to 1; zealots never); the dead never flee.
  - *Retreat by the numbers* (`src/sim/breakoff.js`; `combat.js` `autoResolveCombat` hands every fight resolved by the numbers to `fightByNumbers`): the Auto-resolve button, headless tools, the autopilot and crews. The fight is decided as before (one roll on the odds the card shows; ammo, friends and rivals, fortified walls) and plays out in 3 rounds: a fight being won takes its wounds early, one being lost keeps taking them to the last round. After rounds 1 and 2 a team in the field (`breaksOff`: a team's fight with a way out, not a raid or a base defence) looks at its *Retreat if it goes badly* policy: **Never** (fight it out; the old resolution, the same odds and wounds), **When most are down** (more than half the team down or under 50 health), **If losing** (the fight turns: seen after round 1 in 50% + 40% × the odds against of the fights being lost, 95% by round 2; a false alarm in 25% / 10% × the odds against of the fights that would be won; or anyone is down), **At first blood** (anyone hurt). Breaking off: the blows of the fight land whole, so those still standing first take part of any blow still to come for them (that blow × (the share of the team down)^1.5, never to 0 health: about fresh after an early break-off, about half health when most are down, as on the map); then everyone still standing runs (`escapeChanceByNumbers`, the tactical check with the map's numbers estimated: 2–5 tiles to safety, +2 when they had the drop on you, −1 when you struck first; of the enemy left (at most 16 get at the runners), 0.35 of the dead per runner on them (people 0.05; at most 3), 0.35 of the people shooting, 0.5 of the dead chasing, the dead's share × 0.35 after round 1 and × 0.5 after round 2 as they close in; then −3 a tile, −25 a pursuer on them, −6 a shooter and −4 a chaser (at most 35), up to −30 for wounds, −2 a point of armor, +1 a point of Scouting, +10 at night, and by the numbers +10 with the car close by, +5 bikes, +10 a stealthy stance; 5–95%), each downed teammate carried by the free runner with the best odds (−10, 4.5 a tile); caught: the tactical blows (18–32 per pursuer on them, at least one, before armor), at 0 health dragged down and left behind (a carrier drops whoever they carried). The carried pull through at 50% + 2% a point of Medicine of the best medic who got out (home at 5–15 health); the left behind are lost to the dead, and to a group's people killed or taken prisoner half the time (`conflicts.js` `takeCaptive`). The enemy loses 10–50% (35–75% in a fight you would have won) × the share of rounds fought; nothing is looted (only a won fight is searched), the ammo of the rounds not fought is given back, and the result is "retreat" (a defeat if nobody got out) with `retreatOrder` (who got out, was caught, carried, dropped) and a summary line for the handlers' after-fight text. *The card*: every card with a fight choice (road conflicts, ambushes, a firefight, rescues, outsiders, a group-base assault, a job's rescue, the road's horde) shows the team's policy as four buttons (kept on the team, `p.retreatPolicy`; saved with it) and, under each fight choice, a forecast run on its own dice (300 fights, the game's dice untouched): "If losing: about 64% to win; if it turns, most get away (pulls out 30% of the time). About 0.1 deaths a fight (0.3 fought to the end)". A team without its own policy: a crew's role setting (Work tab, *Retreat*: by its style unless set), the autopilot's style (Cautious and Balanced If losing, Aggressive When most are down), else Never. A set policy is also the AI style of a battle a team plays headless on the map (a hive assault: At first blood Cautious, If losing Balanced, the others Aggressive). The road's horde fight breaks off between its wounds by the same policy: the horde thinned only for the rounds fought, then everyone runs from the dead. `breakOff(state, people, opts)` is the same escape for any other resolver (the downed carried, the caught hurt, the left behind killed or taken): for abandoning a base by the numbers.
- **Lights** (`src/sim/lights.js`, items in `data.js`, finds in `loot.js` LIGHT_FINDS, recipes in `crafting.js`): five, each heavier and brighter: headlamp 0.2 kg (hands free, works from anywhere on the person), flashlight 0.3, torch 0.7 (an open flame; made from firewood anywhere), lantern 1.2 (all around), spotlight 3.5 (a long beam); all but the headlamp in the off hand. At night in raids and base battles they set your people's sight (7 without; 8, 9, 9, 10, 12) and add to how far off the guards notice them (+1, +3, +4, +4, +6) (`stealth.js`); a rummage without one is louder (`search.js`); a survey's night hours keep ×0.42–0.7 of the odds by the best light carried (`survey.js`). Teams take a light each from the armory when they set out (the planner's *Lights* line, on by default; the autopilot's and crews' trip kit, `kit.js`), the lightest that works for each person, packed before the estimate so the pace counts the weight; a failed launch puts them back. Battles draw a beam as long as the light (a glow all around for a lantern or torch); on the State Map a team carrying lights glows at night. `node tools/lighttest.js`.
- **Travel times and road blockages** (`src/sim/data.js` TERRAIN / ROAD_COST, `parties.js` BIKE_ROAD, `src/sim/roadblocks.js`): on foot a tile takes an hour whatever the ground; a car 15 minutes a tile on a road or a bridge and 30 off-road (no mountains); bikes 30 minutes on a road, walking pace off it; stance, load and weather scale these. While the clock runs the top bar's minutes tick by and your teams glide between tiles on the map (`parties.js` glideAt; out of contact, the estimated position glides too). A car or bikes on a road meet a blockage on about 6% of road tiles (stalled cars, debris, a fallen tree, a pile-up, a washed-out dip): 15 minutes to 3 hours lost (bikes 40% of it), told in the log and shown as *Road blocked* over the team while it pays the delay off. Their own dice (hash01), so the rest of a run draws the same numbers; `state.noRoadblocks` is a test switch.
- **Travel events:** about 18 choice cards with skill checks (hazards, the dead, raiders, traders, discoveries, crew drama), plus forced horde encounters. One card per team per hour; a card is dropped when its team is gone or the person it is about has died or left; costs are checked against what the team carries when the choice is made.
- **Fog of war and recon:** geography is known; current conditions are not. Teams report only when they get home, unless they are in radio coverage (live position and sight).
- **Hidden sites** (`src/sim/discovery.js`; the rule is `known` at the end of each `data.js` LOCATIONS line): only what a normal person knew before the fall is on the map from day 1. *Public* types are on it wherever they stand: towns, cities and every shop and service in them (police, schools, hospitals, malls, gas stations…), truck stops, landmarks (dam, prison, university, factory, airport) and the National Guard bases (everyone knew where the base was). *Road* types (farmsteads, barns, ranches, cabins, hunting lodges, campgrounds, ranger stations, radio towers, quarries) are public when they stand on a road or next to one (within 1 tile) and hidden otherwise. *Secret* types (the old crash sites, and the wrecks and supply drops of crash events) are always hidden until found. A hidden site is not drawn (no icon, name or danger skulls), the tooltip and tile panel show bare ground, the right-click menu offers no scavenge, plans, presets ("nearby sites, richest first"), crews and the autopilot never pick it and text calls it by its ground ("forest (12,30)"), until your people have intel on its tile: seen from a base or a watchtower, seen by a team (radioed in when it is in coverage, else brought home; the team itself may use it at once), or reported (radio news, an explorer's or scout's map, intel bought or given as a job reward, a prisoner questioned, the coalition's reports, a group showing you where a retrieve job's pick-up is). Intel is never taken away, so a found site stays on the map and is saved with the game. Finding them on purpose: the **Survey** mission (below). Each find gets one log and diary line: *🧭 Move here team (Cole, June) found a hunting lodge deep in the woods: Pine Hollow Hunting lodge. It is on the map now.* (a team out of contact "brought word of" it when home; *Lookouts at …* from a base; *Word reached you of …* for reports). Hive names only use public sites. The hub knows the public sites everywhere plus what it sees on day 1. The timelapse replay is a god view and shows every site; Dev Reveal shows them too (display only). About 14–19% of the sites are hidden (Small 18 of 128, Normal 33 of 186, Large 54 of 282, Huge 82 of 419, about 5 within 12 tiles of the hub; `tools/maptest.js`).
- **Survey, rumours and optics** (`src/sim/survey.js`, `src/sim/optics.js`): the *Survey* mission (planner: a mission and the *Survey trip* preset with radius 2–4 and 6–48 hours and the odds as *good / fair / poor* with the reasons; right-click any tile: *Survey the area* from the hub, or *Survey here with team* for a team in contact; the team panel: 12, 24 or 48 hours; a *Survey the area* plan step) sweeps the land around a tile for hidden places and finds. The route is worked out when it starts (greedy: the next spot clears the most ground not yet looked at, never too far to walk back, no random numbers), so it ends back at the centre on its last hour and every estimate (hours, food, water, fuel) is the real one. A step takes 2 hours on foot (×1.4 in hills, forest and city, ×2 mountains, ×0.7 on a road), 1.5 on bikes, 1 by car; stealthy ×1.35, rushed ×0.8. Each hour it rolls for every hidden site within the radius + 2 (+ the optics' reach) that it does not know: 12% × Scouting (0.5 + 0.05 a point; Keen Eye and Spotter +0.15) × team (+8% a person past the first, at most ×1.3) × the site's ground (farmland ×1.35, suburbs ×1, city ×0.85, hills ×0.7, forest ×0.6, mountains ×0.55) × the distance from the team less the optics' reach (0–1 tiles ×1.6, 2 ×1, 3 ×0.45, 4 ×0.18, 5 ×0.06) × night ×0.35 (flashlight ×0.5, night vision ×0.75, thermal ×0.9) × weather (fog ×0.5, blizzard ×0.4, storm ×0.55, rain ×0.85, snow ×0.8) × car ×0.6 / bikes ×0.8 × stealthy ×1.2 / rushed ×0.75 × optics × a rumour ×3, at most 80% an hour; whatever it walks past within its sight radius it sees anyway, and caches, wrecked cars and camps get a look-around roll near the team every hour. It eats at the moving rate (load bands apply), Heat rises at half the rate of standing still (`fieldheat.js` ACTIVITY.sweep 0.5), every tile it walks into rolls the road's events and encounters (not the last step back to the centre; a card's delay comes out of the survey's own hours), a car burns fuel and wears by the tile, an hour on hive land is 3% (8% on the red-skull ring) for the dead to find it, and a car that runs dry is left and the rest is walked. Finds go into the team's report (on the map at once in radio range, else when it is home) with the discovery log line naming the *Survey team*; the map shows the survey's teal circle and, in contact, the path walked and to come, and the team panel *Surveying 3 tiles around …: 14 of 24 hours, 1 place found*. *Rumours*: radio chatter (a station: 5% a day), travellers and merchants at a gate (+15% a day each), travellers' news (half the time) and other groups' radio news (35%) speak of an unfound hidden place within 30 tiles: a dashed amber circle of 3 tiles that holds it (never on its centre) with the vague place ("a hunting lodge somewhere north of Dunmore") for 20 days (at most three at once); a survey centred in it has ×3 odds for that place, which is struck off once found. Rumours use their own dice (never the world's random numbers). *Crews* have a *Survey for hidden places* field job (the most ground nobody has looked at within range, or a rumour's circle, for as many hours as the crew's hours-out limit, at most a day) and take the hub's optics; the *autopilot* sends two scouts (on foot or bikes, its best at Scouting, not whoever is building or crafting) on a launch day when 2+ hands are still spare after the day's trip while at least 3 people and half the hub stay home whatever the style (the walls first: without that floor Aggressive lost its hub early), and a spot within 10 tiles has 8+ unknown tiles (a rumour's circle first): one survey team at a time, at most every 6 days (3 with a rumour), not counted among the style's teams out, with the hub's optics. Targets come only from what the hub knows (`tools/surveytest.js` checks they do not move when every unknown hidden site is removed). *Optics* (data.js GEAR `optics`, trade category *binoculars and scopes*: the military ×1.5, raiders ×1.3, explorers ×1.3): carried by anyone on a team the best counts and a second adds a quarter (look capped at ×1.75). Pocket monocular (pocket, 0.2 kg: look ×1.1, sees others first +4%), binoculars (pocket, 0.7 kg: ×1.25, a tile more reach, +1 tile of map sight by day, +8% seeing others first), spotting scope (pack, 2.2 kg: ×1.6 and two tiles more reach, only while the team stays put: surveys, Look around, camps), thermal monocular (off hand or pocket, 0.5 kg, military: ×1.2, a tile of reach, night and fog cost it almost nothing, +1 sight at night and in fog, +12% seeing others first at night, and in the off hand it sees in the dark in a battle like night vision goggles), telescope (bulky, 9 kg: in a base's armory +1 tile of the base's view, +2 with a watchtower). Found at lodges, gun stores, ranger stations, cabins, ranches, farms, police stations, the Guard, towns, malls and hardware stores (binoculars), the university (telescope), military bases and helicopter wrecks (thermal), raid rooms (gun counters, cabins, barracks, armories, houses, labs), traders, the military and merchants.
- **Radio network (`src/sim/radio.js`):** a radio station covers 12 tiles (`RADIO_RANGE`); a base holding a *Military encrypted radio* (the loot item) in its armory is a station reaching 24 (`MIL_RADIO_RANGE`), station building or not; groups' own sets add 6 (`GROUP_REACH`), so a group within coverage + 6 can be called. A chain of stations and relays runs through your own bases only. A *radio relay* (a utility plot at any base, the hub included: 15 scrap, 3 parts, 24 work-hours, a Mechanics 3+ survivor and the studied Radio Engineering Handbook to build) covers 6 tiles around its base. It is an unmanned repeater, so it needs no operator, but it uses 1 part every 10 days (from its base, else the hub) and goes off the air without one. A relay works only when linked: within range of a station or of another linked relay (the other node's range: a base within a station's coverage + 6 but beyond its 12 does not link), so relays chain coverage outward. None goes up where a radio station stands or is queued (the station's 12 tiles already cover the relay's 6: Construction greys it out and says so); one built before a station stands idle beside it, using no parts, and takes over again if the station is lost. A relay at a hub without a station is like any other: it works once a station elsewhere (an outpost's, or a Military encrypted radio) or a linked relay reaches it. **Radio reach and the coalition:** a group whose base lies within your coverage + 6 can be called and is in reach for the coalition's invitation (`alliance.js reachVia`: radio, else a team at their gates, a base of yours within 8 tiles, or a member's within 12). When a relay at one of your bases would bring a known group into reach (`radio.js relayReach / relaySpotFor`, chains it would complete included), the invitation's "out of radio reach" line, the group's radio panel and the Groups tab's radio header name that base. Coverage is the union of every station and linked relay; the map draws each circle (red: not linked or off the air) and the base screen says what a base has (station, relay linked / not linked / offline, or covered from another base). A *Hand-crank radio* (`data.js handradio`, loot and the hardcore prepper's kit; `radio.js listeners`) is a receiver only, and transmits nothing: `canRadioGroup`, `commandable` and the coverage ignore it. It needs no batteries; the *Pocket radio* is a thrown decoy.
- **Radio networks and listening in (`src/sim/radio.js` radioNetworks / networksAt, `src/sim/radiolisten.js`, `src/ui/radiolisten.js`; `node tools/radiolistentest.js`):** your stations and linked relays are one network; every group with a base has its own, around each of its bases by type (`GROUP_NET_RANGE`: military 24 tiles, traders and fortresses 12, communes, zealots and expansionists 8, raiders 6), so a base taken or lost changes it. A receiver (a station, a Military encrypted radio, a hand-crank radio) hears a network only while it stands inside that network's coverage: nothing past every network (no band beyond coverage). *Listen in* (1 hour: the team order "listen" in the team panel, or a base's set in the Groups tab's radio header) tunes a receiver to one network in range (several overlapping: you pick; the same action switches; remembered as `p.tuned` / `base.tuned`). It catches up on the channel's last 3 days at once, then, while tuned and in range, picks up the day's traffic: caravans and convoys setting out (route, destination, rough day), scavengers out, raids and war parties (target, rough day; a warning card with *Prepare defenses* when one is coming for your base), their war parties against the hives (which hive, how it went), their wars, their stores short and plentiful, rumours of places (survey.js), how they talk about you. Each line is caught or missed by the world's dice (`hash01`, per listener and day): base odds (raid on you 70%, hive war 60%, raids 55%, caravans 50%, wars 50%, stores 45%, talk 40%, scavengers 35%, rumours 30%) × (0.7 + 0.03 × the listener's best Scouting), at most 90%; a military channel is encrypted (× 0.6 without a Military encrypted radio); the catch-up counts × 1.25. Lines go to the log and the listener's "heard" list (team panel, Teams tab, Groups tab). A group notices now and then (1% a day while you are tuned in range: −5 goodwill). Your own channel gives what it always did. The map draws a tuned group channel's coverage in its colour. The governor tunes its hub weekly to the group that raided it last (else the nearest hostile group in range) and answers the warning cards with *Prepare defenses*.
- **Radio to other groups:** groups whose base is within coverage plus 4 tiles (they have radios too) can be called from the Groups tab (*Radio…*). *Scan the airwaves* (once a day) finds groups in reach you have not met ("heard on the radio", their base marked). Calls: greet (+2 goodwill, every 3 days), ask for news (every 5 days; a report of 2 / 3 / 4 tiles around their bases for neutral / friendly / allied groups, and every horde within 10 tiles of them), ask for a trade caravan (every 4 days; neutral or better, aggressive types at goodwill 0+; yes with a chance of 45% + goodwill%, +25% traders, +15% commune), make a deal ("we want 20 meds, paying 60 ammo": accepted when it is fair for them and they can spare it; the panel shows their lowest price), call for help (friendly or a pact, every 10 days: a raid on its way to you loses 30% of its strength, or 20–30 food if the hub has under 3 days; costs 4 goodwill), and pacts. Hostile groups refuse business. Groups call you too: offers (their surplus for something you have) and requests (something they need, paying with their surplus), as cards, at most one every 6 days per group (traders 15% a day, a commune 10%, others 6%).
- **Caravans (`src/sim/caravans.js`):** a group's caravan (their wagon or truck on the map) walks to your base along a land route (no route, no caravan) with 2–5 guards; hordes and raiders can catch it on the way like any group party. At your base the trade window opens with any radio deal filled in; the caravan stays a day (Bases tab: *Trade* reopens the window) and then goes home. A radio deal not done by then costs 3 goodwill. You can attack it (trade window, with a confirmation): a battle at your base against its guards, the goods are yours if you win, and either way the group turns hostile (goodwill −70, pact torn up), other groups you know that are not aggressive lose 5 goodwill, and for 60 days the group sends raids whatever its type.
- **Your trade caravans:** Groups tab → *New trade caravan…*: crew, vehicle or on foot, stance, goods to trade, items from the hub's armory, supplies to live on (auto-fill), and an ordered list of destinations (known groups' bases, your outposts) with the hours of each leg. It runs on the normal order queue (a move, then the caravan stop order). At each stop a card offers: trade there (the trade window), unload the goods (your outposts), stay a day, go on to the next destination, or return home, each with its ETA; *Other orders…* gives the usual orders card, which has a *Trade caravan* entry to get back.
- **Scavenging / raids:** an interactive raid on a generated 28×18 map built from the site (buildings and outdoor areas set by location type, e.g. a hospital has clinics, a pharmacy and offices). Search containers next to you (Shift+S) for loot drawn from the area's table, scaled by how picked over the site is and by Scavenging; what does not fit stays inside. Guards patrol and the dead wander, unaware until they spot you (Stealth) or hear gunfire. Heat rises each turn and with noise; each stage brings more dead. Leave from a green extraction zone (X); a vehicle you bring waits in its own zone, and extracting elsewhere leaves it at the site. A raid ends when everyone still standing has left: a downed teammate who was stabilized is carried out (at 5–15 health) if anyone extracts, one still bleeding has the same chance as the downed in a lost battle, and with nobody out everyone on the map is lost. Raid time passes on the map clock afterwards. Headless tools still use the older hour-by-hour abstraction.
- **Death sites:** the dead keep their gear where they fell (someone who dies at their own base leaves no death site: their things go to that base's armory); scavengers strip the most valuable items first; looted gear turns up with nearby groups; recovery missions take what fits in the cargo and say what was left. A site with nothing left on it is removed with its marker, and a team that finds nothing there (or finds an abandoned vehicle gone) clears the marker when it reports. One of yours who got up as one of the dead and walked off (`carriers.js`) leaves a site that stays and says so ("Dana's body is gone: they rose and walked"): their things walk with them (walking loot, below). A body carried home in the team's car leaves no site: it is buried (things to the armory) or burned on the base's Armory tab.
- **AI groups:** 7 types with starting attitudes, growth, expansion, raids on you and each other, convoys, attitude drift from your expansion, calls for help (instant by radio, late by runner), collapse and refugees. Only a hostile group (goodwill −40 or below) raids you (`factions.js` RAIDS): aggressive types roll their daily chance after an 8–16 day cooldown (raiders 10.5%, zealots 7.5%, expansionists 6%), peaceful groups you provoked into hostility rarely (military 4.5%, fortress 3%, traders and communes 1.5%; left alone, no base of yours within 5 tiles and no new offence for 30 days, they cool off by 0.1 goodwill a day); from their base nearest to yours, within 30 tiles. Your base's weight among their targets: 2.5 × greed (stock value / 1500, 0.4–3) × (1 + 0.2 per outpost you founded; bases you took from groups do not count) × (1 + 0.5 per point of grudge, at most 4: +1 for each raid of theirs you beat, fading 1% a day) × their size (strength / 35, 0.6–2) ÷ (1 + tiles / 12). The raid is sized to your base's prepared defence (0.6–1.0 ×, × escalation: +25% for each raid of theirs you beat, up to 2×, reset when one gets in), at most the group's whole strength. The 8-hour siege runs faster against a strong defence (each hour counts defence / strength / 1.2, 1–4); then held at 1.2×, a break-in at 0.8×, else overrun (what they take goes into their stores, and a raid that got in wins them 1–2 recruits). Tribute's +6 goodwill fades again at 0.5 a day from an aggressive group. A card after each raid says what was taken, who was hurt and what was smashed (`breakdowns.js` raid damage). Growth: aggressive groups recruit drifters (12% + 2% a base a day when the usual growth roll adds nobody, below 45 people, with food for two days a head) and expand at their rate + 2% (× people / 18) a day up to 2 + people / 10 bases (at most 6; others 4), within 7 tiles, toward rich sites and toward you (never within 3 tiles of your bases); a group whose raid or war raid breaks another takes over its bases, 2–4 of its people and half its stores (a group broken by hordes or by you: the strongest aggressive group within 15 tiles, 50%); a won raid between groups (and a war raid, 35% × the same wish) takes the base when the winner wants to keep it (`factions.js keepChance`: raiders 80%, expansionists 90%, zealots 70%, military 55%, fortress 50%, traders and communes 30%, × 1 within 7 tiles of their own bases falling to 0.4 at 25, × 0.8–1.4 by how rich it is, never beyond what they can hold) and carries home half of what the defenders lose; a group clearly outmatched (a raid 1.6× its defence) at a base that is not its main one gives it up half the time and falls back to the others (the raiders keep it by the same chance, else it stands empty); with fewer than 2 aggressive groups left hostile to you (from day 60) a new gang from beyond the border settles 12–30 tiles from your hub (2% a day, 12–18 people), at any time of the run; raider bands from outside that have looted enough also dig in (Outsiders below). A group is broken (`collapseFaction`) when it is down to 3 people (`GROUP_BREAK`): war raids grind a group down to 3 (they used to stop at 8) and a war ends at once when a side is down to 6 (was 12). A won raid between groups may also **raze** the base (`razeChance`, aggressive types only: raiders 22%, zealots 12%, expansionists 5%, × 1 + 0.25 a grudge point (at most 4), × 1–2 the farther it is from their own bases, at most 80%; rolled before keeping it): nobody holds it after, and the tile is a ruin. The raid warning gives the number of attackers and what *Prepare defenses* really does (non-guards count 1.3× instead of 0.8×); the alert ends when the raid is resolved, bought off or never arrives. *Defend in person* fields that many attackers (count × power = the raid's strength, at least one); a lost defence is the break-in itself, with no second raid by the numbers. Tribute is 30% of the food on hand when it is paid. A collapsed group's land is free at once and its raids and convoys vanish. Groups you have not met are not named in the log.
- **Sites run dry; other groups scavenge too** (`src/sim/aiscav.js`): site loot (`tile.loot`) is one finite stock for everyone and nothing grows back after the map is made (the only new loot is a crash site or supply drop, which burns out again; a boat crew's driftwood on a shore is separate and still washes up, 1 scrap every 72 hours). Your searches and raids lower it, and so do the groups: each group with 6+ people sends one scavenging party at a time, every 6–10 days when it is short of something (need = 1 − stock ÷ want; wants per head: food 10, ammo 20 × arms, scrap 6, fuel 2, meds 2, preserved 1, parts 3 + 0.2; it goes when a need reaches 0.25, else it looks again in 3 days). The party (strength pop × 0.12, at least 2, on foot; on the map as walkers in the group's look with packs, a crowbar and a stuffed sack) goes to the best site within 10 tiles of one of its bases by its needs × loot left ÷ (1 + distance ÷ 5): never a base, a hive, a crash site, a horde's tile, your land or another group's, or a site another party is working. It searches 8 hours, taking 0.03 loot an hour × its strength ÷ 3 (0.5–1: a bigger party crowds the site, strips it no faster and picks it cleaner, its haul × strength ÷ 3 up to 1.5) down to 3%, finds per point of loot what one of your teams would (the hour-by-hour search: 6.5 × loot left per 0.05, by the site's loot table) × 0.8, and walks home, where the haul goes into the group's stock. A horde on the way or at the site costs a quarter of its strength and of its haul (at the site they pull out); raiders near their base may ambush it on the way home (12% a tile) and take the haul; below strength 1 it is gone. Sites inside a group's own land lose 0.3% of their loot a day into its stock at half the yield. In exchange, groups make only 40% of the ammo, scrap, fuel and meds they used to make from nothing, and their fields feed them 2% of a head a day less. Your team on a site their party is working (not in the middle of its own search: then they wait) gets a *Contested site* conflict card (once per party and team; a friendly or allied group, or one with a pact, keeps to its side); win the fight and they leave, dropping their haul into your cargo as far as it fits. You only learn a site was stripped when you see it (in view, a team passing or reporting, recon, news over the radio): the map and the tile panel show what you last saw. Radio news: when a party took 0.12+ loot from a site (or dropped it a band) within 15 tiles of one of your bases and you have a radio station, the log says *On the radio: Red Hand has been stripping the Calder Mall.* (in view: *Red Hand scavengers are stripping …*), at most one a day and one a site every 10 days, and the loot you know there is updated (only the loot; the rest keeps its age). Over a year (five seeds, Balanced autopilot) about 68% of the site loot is left on day 120, 54% on day 240 and 48% on day 365 (93% without scavenging groups); the yearly group collapse rate stays the same within noise (0.40 → 0.42 a group a year; `tools/depletetest.js`).
- **Outsiders (`src/sim/visitors.js`, Groups tab section and alerts in `src/ui/visitors.js`):** Raider bands may settle at any time: a band of 8+ that carries goods worth 250+ (what it looted: enough to start a base) digs in at a site or a ruin within 6 tiles (3% a day; not within 6 tiles of your bases or 8 of a group\'s, not on a hive\'s ground) and becomes one of the map\'s groups, a hostile raider gang with its people and its loot (`settleBands`; "The Iron Jackals have dug in at the old quarry"). people from beyond the State of Calder who are not one of its groups (`state.visitors`). They enter on a land tile of the map's border (the border hordes' tiles, `hives.js borderTiles`: not within 6 of your bases; merchants on a road), travel the map and leave from a border tile (at the latest a few days after their stay is up). Spawns are rolled daily on their own dice (`state.visitorInfo.rng`). *Raiders:* the first band between days 20 and 26, then every clamp(30 − 0.06 × day, 10, 30) × 0.75–1.25 days (about 29 days early, 23 at day 120, 16 at day 240, 10 from day 334; at most 1 band on the map, 2 from day 120, 3 from day 240); clamp(3 + day / 30 + 0–2, 3, 14) people of power 2.6 + min(3.4, day / 80) (clubs and old pistols → assault rifles and plate). They pick what is weakly defended against what it holds: your bases (only when their strength is at least 0.6 × its defence), your teams (not within 2 tiles of a base; at 0.9 × the team), your convoys, a group's base (at 1.2 × 8 + 0.8 × its strength, one per band), groups' caravans on foot, merchant caravans and rich sites (loot 0.45+, not within 8 tiles of a group's base: their scavenging grounds; 10–18% taken). Your base: a warning card when they are 4 tiles out and seen or heard (prepare, pay them off: 4 × their strength in goods, talk them down: Speech 10% + 3.5% a point up to 75% for half the price, defend in person on the deploy map, hold); after 8 hours the numbers decide like a group's raid (held at 1.2, a break-in at 0.8 taking 20% of the stock (10% with a storehouse), else overrun: 50% (25%) and an outpost lost); what they take they carry, and beating them later gives it back. Your teams: a card when a band that thinks it can win is within a tile (fight, pay a toll of 35% of the load up to 3 × their strength, talk, run: 30% + 2.5% per Scouting, +20% a vehicle, +10% stealthy), or when you saw them first (slip away, ambush, lie low). A group's base: a 12-hour siege, then `factions.js resolveRaidOnFaction` (they keep 60% of what the group lost). Over the radio (Groups tab, their tile in coverage) you can pay them to leave the state (1.25 × the tribute, 2% less per point of Speech, at most 30%). They leave when they carry 10 × their strength, lost half the band, were paid or their 8–14 days are up. *Merchants:* the first on days 8–14, then every 10–16 days (1 at a time, 2 from day 150): 3–5 guards (+1 every 90 days, at most 7, power 3.5 + min(2.5, day / 120)) and 2 traders with a wagon train, carrying fuel 15–40, meds 8–22, preserved food 20–50, parts 3–9, ammo 60–160, a manual or blueprint (75%, a second 25%, unstudied ones first), 1–3 books and 2–4 pieces of good gear; they want food, water, scrap and jars (`trade.js` prices: their stock per head sets their need, with their own profile in `TRADE_PREFS` "merchants": food, water, scrap and jars wanted, valuables and collectibles prized for resale, fuel, meds and ammo a little cheaper, and a 10% margin on what they charge; travellers use "explorers"; they trade with the groups by demand on both sides). They visit up to 3 groups (never raider gangs or zealots), trading what each is short of for what they lack, and one of your bases when invited over the radio ("they can stop at your hub in about 30 hours if you want", when they come into coverage) or unannounced 55% of the time; there they stay 24 hours and the trade window opens (a team that meets them on the road can trade too). Rob them and merchant reputation drops 45 (new merchants come with goodwill 15 + reputation, hostile and no trade at −40, and skip your bases from −30; +0.25 a day back to 0), known traders lose 15 goodwill and communes and fortresses 8. *Explorers:* the first on days 5–10, then every 6–12 days (at most 2): scouts (40%, 2–3), refugees (35%, 2–5) or wanderers (25%, 1–2) walking between 2–3 ruins away from the groups' grounds (4–8 hours each, 3–6% of the loot) and, refugees 75% of the time (wanderers 45%, scouts 30%), to your hub. At the gate or on the road: news (another state's rumour, and the tiles along their way put on your map dated when they passed: the fog lifts only there), the scouts' maps (2 tiles either side, for 12 + 0.5 a tile visited in goods), trade (the trade window), who wants to stay (a recruit card, `recruits.js`: refugees 1–3, wanderers 0–1), shelter for 3 days (1 food and 1 water each a day, mood +3 at the base; then a gift half the time and someone asking to stay a third of the time). 8% are thieves who rob the base that shelters them (12% of its food, meds and ammo) or a team they can take. *Seen* only when your bases or teams in radio contact can see them (state.live) or *heard* in radio coverage (faded on the map); the Groups tab's **Outsiders** section lists those you know (what they are doing, attitude, last position) with Locate / Trade / At the gate / Invite to trade / Ask for news / Pay them to leave; alerts for raiders within 8 tiles of a base and merchants at one; log and diary lines; the map draws raiders from outside in dark red (banner, biker), merchants as a gold wagon train with oxen and travellers in grey; the timelapse records them and captions bands crossing the border and striking groups. The autopilot answers their cards by style (Cautious pays a band it may not hold, Aggressive defends in person), trades with merchants (a manual nobody has studied, then what the hub is short of), takes their news and maps and decides recruits by its bed and food rule.
- **Caravans on the road (`src/sim/escorts.js`, `src/sim/caravanraids.js`):** any caravan can be attacked and everything it carries taken. *Merchants from outside* carry goods by **speciality** (general goods, guns and ammunition, armour and military kit, fuel, parts and tools, medicine, food and water; plus the manuals, books, valuables and gun attachments they always brought), and what the wagons are worth (`escortValue`: supplies at base value, items at `itemValue`, guns, armour and military electronics 1.5×) buys the **escort**: under 550, 2–3 guards on foot with pistols and shotguns (power 3.0); 550–1000, 4–5 riflemen in a few vests with a pickup (+3 strength, power 3.8); 1000+, 6–8 hired guns in vests and plates with automatic rifles, a scout and a Humvee with a turret gunner (+8; an MRAP, +11, from 1500; power 4.6). +1 guard every 120 days (at most +2), +1 power over 150 days (at most +1.2). On the map the wagon has a guard ahead, a pickup behind or a Humvee / MRAP leading it; the tooltip says the escort in words. *You attacking:* a card when a team meets a group's caravan or convoy on a road (Attack / Let it pass; merchants keep their own road card with Rob them), or from the team's orders card: **Attack …** for a caravan in sight within a tile (you have the drop on them) or **Intercept …** for one in sight within 6 tiles (the team follows it for up to 48 hours and asks again when close). The battle is on the road map against its guards (`combat.js` kits `guards`, `escorts`, `contractors`, or the group's own fighters) with its vehicle parked behind them (drawn as a Humvee or MRAP for the armoured; not one of the map's found cars). Won: every supply and item, the guards' gear (by the numbers: their guns, half their armour and 6–17 rounds each; on the map you search the bodies), and the vehicle: 60% it runs (joins a team with no vehicle, else parked and marked), 30% it needs an engine (2 parts, 2 scrap, Mechanics 4, 5 for the Army ones: repair it on the tile), 10% wrecked (2–4 parts, 6–12 scrap). What the team cannot carry goes in the parked vehicle or stays on the tile as cargo left behind (Recover). *Witnesses:* consequences only when someone saw it: a guard or trader who got away (a lost or broken-off fight, one who fled the map, a merchant's trader slipping off 35% of the time when the guards are beaten by the numbers), another group's base within 3 tiles, or anyone else on the road within a tile. Seen: merchants -45 reputation and the usual goodwill losses; a group's caravan turns that group hostile (`caravans.js turnHostile`). Unseen: no consequence (the caravan never arrived). At your gates it is always seen. *The groups:* raider gangs rob merchant caravans and your convoys within 3 tiles of their bases (hourly 3%, up to 9% for a rich cargo, only with a war band of 0.35 × their strength at least 1.1 × the caravan; decided by 0.5 + 0.6 × (ratio − 1), 5–95%), and the groups' caravans and convoys within 2 tiles (`parties.js aiPartyHour`, now by the same odds, also by aggressive groups at war with the owner); won, all the supplies go into their stock and the items onto their shelf. Outsider raiders take everything from merchants and your convoys too, and a trade caravan of yours that loses a road fight loses its whole load. Dice: `hash01`, never the world's.
- **What things are worth to whom (`src/sim/valuation.js`, tables in `data.js` `CATEGORIES` / `TRADE_PREFS`):** every supply and item has a hidden base value the player never sees. The trade window, the radio deal and the caravan planner show only what a given group pays or charges, with short *why* lines (▲ wanted, ▼ little wanted, ✕ refused: "At war: rifles wanted", "Winter is coming: fuel wanted", "Zealots: they will not touch liquor", "They already have spare armor"); the window's header says what the group's kind prizes and shuns and its situation right now. Elsewhere (inventory and armory tooltips, crafting) an item shows only a worth tier: junk (< 4), cheap (< 12), decent (< 35), valuable (< 90), rare. A price = worth × kind × state × holdings × spread. **Kind** (`TRADE_PREFS`, by category: × on both what they pay and charge, 0 = refused): military rifles 1.35, automatics 1.5, ammo 1.3, armor 1.35, electronics 1.5, batteries 1.3, drugs 0.4, comforts 0.6; raiders guns 1.2–1.4, fuel 1.4, liquor 1.6, cigarettes 1.6, drugs 1.7, jewelry 1.5, books 0.45, farm goods 0.5; commune food 1.25, seeds and farm tools 1.6, meds 1.3, books 1.35, jars 1.4, coffee and sweets 1.35, batteries 1.3, gold 0.6, automatics 0.5; fortress scrap 1.5, parts 1.4, defences 1.6, armor 1.35, tools 1.3; expansionists fuel 1.5, vehicle parts 1.6, guns 1.15–1.35; traders everything 1.1 (valuables and collectibles up to 1.4: they resell) and a 10% margin on what they charge; zealots refuse liquor, drugs and cigarettes, gold 0.4 (vanity), books 0.35, comforts 0.5, meds 1.4, food 1.3; merchants from outside food 1.35, water 1.3, scrap 1.4, jars 1.5, collectibles 1.4, fuel / meds / ammo 0.85–0.9, margin 10%; travellers food, water, camp gear. **State** (live, no dice; × held to 0.35–2.6): at war weapons and armor ×1.3, ammo and meds ×1.35, fine things ×0.85; hordes (20+ within 5 tiles of a base, or a horde attack in 14 days) weapons ×1.2, ammo ×1.25, armor ×1.15, defences ×1.4; raided in 10 days weapons and armor ×1.15, defences ×1.3, fine things ×0.85; a base lost in 20 days weapons ×1.25, armor ×1.2, defences ×1.4, fine things ×0.75; sickness (8-day spells, 15% of 30-day cycles, 30% in winter) meds ×1.5; winter fuel and warm clothes ×1.4, food ×1.15; the last 10 days of autumn fuel ×1.25; harvest jars ×1.25, food ×0.9; summer water ×1.2, camp gear ×1.3; spring seeds ×1.4; starving (under 2 food a head) fine things ×0.6 ("they cannot eat gold"); rich (supplies worth 1.6× the normal a head, at peace) fine things ×1.2; poor ×0.8. **Holdings:** supplies by the stock curve below, and starving or thirsty groups pay up to 60% more again for food or water as their stock runs out; items by the spare ones of the same category on their shelf (books by title): 1.15 with none, −0.5 per comfortable amount (per head: rifles 0.25, armor 0.4, books 0.04…), down to 0.25. Every unit of a deal is priced halfway through it, so buying low from one group and selling high to another pays (that is what trade routes and caravans are for), but each sale fills the buyer up until it is a loss. **Valuables** (barter goods: jewelry, cigarettes, liquor, coffee and sweets, phones and cameras, collectibles, batteries and household goods) are good for nothing but trade and light (their own `weight` in carry space); five examples are in `data.js` (gold ring, cigarettes, whiskey, coffee, batteries) and no site drops them yet. **Tagging a new item:** give it `cat:` from `CATEGORIES` (an untagged item gets a guess from its slot and fields); a new kind of thing gets a `CATEGORIES` entry and, if groups care, numbers in `TRADE_PREFS`. The autopilot buys from and sells to the groups that pay best (a trade visit goes to the buyer that pays most for its load for the walk, pays with what the group wants most, radio deals pick the cheapest group, and at your gates it sells valuables and spare weapons to groups that prize them). Groups remember raids (`hitDay`, `hordeHitDay`) and lost bases (`lostBaseDay`) on the faction; old saves simply have none.
- **Where meds come from** (`src/sim/medfinds.js`, every number in `MEDS`; `tools/medstest.js` measures it): meds are semi-rare except at a **pharmacy, clinic, hospital or medevac wreck**. *An hour of searching* (`scavenge.js` `searchHour`; data.js `LOCATIONS` loot weights): at those four a meds find is a box (4–9 × what is left, about 4.5–5 meds an hour); anywhere else meds are a few % of the hours (weights 0.4–0.6 among a site's loot; a university, a fire station, a ranger station 1–1.5) and a find is a bottle from a bathroom cabinet: a quarter of the roll, 1–2 at a time (about 0.05–0.13 an hour; farms, gas stations, factories none). Groups' scavengers bring home the same share (`aiscav.js` `haulFor`: the meds weight of the site's table is cut to a quarter away from those four, and the share it loses goes to the site's other goods, so a haul finds as much, less of it meds). *A raid* (`combat.js` `AREAS`): pharmacy and clinic areas hold 2–6 / 2–5 in 85–90% of their containers and their drug cabinets 3–6; houses, stores, offices, cabins, chapels and locker rooms 5–7% of a container for 1; a school nurse's, a fire station's or a barracks' first-aid room (`nurse`) 30% for 1–2; a lab 30% for 1–2, a barracks 15%; a lockbox 15% for 1, a safe 25% for 1–2, a cargo case 45% for 1–3. A town's, a city centre's and a mall's street maps have no pharmacy area any more (their Pharmacy building is one). Measured (16 raids by the bot, 240 search hours at 70% loot): a pharmacy raid about 26 meds, a hospital 33, a medevac wreck 11, a clinic 8; an ordinary place 0.1–1.3 a raid (0.4 on average, was 1.9) and 0.06 an hour (was 0.49). *Bodies* (always 1): one of the dead 5%, a person 10% (`MEDS.BODY_HUMAN`), a dead soldier 8%. *Made and grown*: a med station (2 scrap + 1 food → 1 a day), boiled-rag dressings, medicinal herbs while foraging (1% a forager-hour, spring to autumn) and the Herbalist perk. *Bought*: base value 8 (was 6). Over a year (20 normal games, the balanced autopilot) the hub's meds stock runs about 18 / 26 / 31 at days 29 / 57 / 85 (was 23 / 42 / 48), the meds spent stay about 41 a year and nobody more dies of untreated wounds or illness (0 in 20 games; 2 before).
- **Trade and diplomacy (`src/sim/trade.js`, window in `src/ui/tradewindow.js`):** one price model for visits, caravans, radio deals and trade routes. Items: `itemValue` = base value × (0.2 + 0.8 × condition) × (makeshift gear: 0.3 + 0.7 × uses left / full uses); a pack adds its contents; × kind, state and holdings (above). Supplies: base value × the group's need (stock per head against a reference: 0.5 with plenty, up to 2.2 with none) × kind and state, each unit priced along the curve (a big sale pays less per unit), so selling and buying back always loses. Spread: they pay 75% (+0.8% per point of your best Speech, up to +12%; ± goodwill/1000, up to ±10%) and charge 130% (minus the same). Example: 10 meds (base value 8: semi-rare, see *Where meds come from*) to a group short of them (need 2) at Speech 5, goodwill 20 pays 10 × 8 × 2 × 0.81 ≈ 130; buying 10 from a group with plenty (need 0.5) costs 10 × 8 × 0.5 × 1.24 ≈ 50. A deal goes through when your side, at their buying price, covers theirs at their selling price; radio deals are honoured as agreed. Groups keep a reserve (food 2, water 1.5, ammo 3, meds 0.3, fuel 0.5 a head) and have water now (wells refill to 6 a head), so water is no longer top price. Goodwill from trading grows with the deal's size and what you overpay, at most 4 a day per group; gifts up to 15 a day. The trade window: two columns (you give / you get), every supply with −10 / − / amount / + / +10 / All, every item one by one (condition, uses left, price), running totals and a balance bar, *Balance with* ammo, scrap or food, the reason a deal is refused, gifts, pacts, and (their caravan) an attack button. Carry space: a team carries what it gets, their caravan what it takes. Intel: buy a report of the area around them (3 / 6 / 10 tiles, 0.4 a tile at their price; only those tiles lose their fog: owner, loot left, infection, hordes, hives), horde and hive sightings within 12 tiles, or sell your own maps (0.15 a tile you know well near them, up to 40, once every 10 days). A team carries a report home, or radios it in; at your base it goes on the map at once. One trade screen at a time: a second team or caravan waits its turn. A group that turns hostile tears up its pact (log line).
- **Trade routes:** recurring convoys with Light / Standard / Heavy escorts following the design doc's risk table. Terms follow the group's barter prices (`routeProblem` / `createRoute` in `trade.js` refuse a route that asks for more than the load is worth and say why), and each convoy is paid what its load is worth that day, up to the agreed amount and what they have. A route with no escort left pauses itself.
- **Supply runs:** convoy jobs between your own bases (hub ↔ outpost, outpost ↔ outpost), set up in a base's *Supply runs* tab: crew (with their current gear), optional vehicle, goods to deliver, goods to bring back, stance, and once or every X days. At the destination the convoy comes back, holds there (a parked convoy: the crew do not work that base but eat and drink from its stock; send it home or disband it later), or disbands (crew join that base, vehicle parks there, the job stays as a template you can recreate; recreating from the far end runs the other way with the lists swapped). Runs travel like trade convoys (escort table by crew size, stance, fuel, wear, deaths). Editing a job changes the next run, not the convoy already on the road. If the vehicle is missing, broken or short of fuel the convoy goes on foot when the load fits, otherwise it waits; every blocked case is logged. Outposts hold back the stock their runs need instead of shipping it to the hub.
- **Medical:** everyone at a base heals slowly (faster with Medicine). Build a hospital and upgrade it step by step: Makeshift infirmary (2 beds) → Field hospital (4) → Clinic (6) → Trauma hospital (10). Each tier needs people on the Medic role (1/1/2/3); with too few it runs as the highest tier its medics can staff (a Clinic with one medic works as a Field hospital, with none as no hospital), and an upgrade that finishes understaffed logs a warning. Beds go to people below the base's admit line (Medical tab, "Admit below N health", 30–100 in steps of 10, default 70) and to anyone with a severe (below 30) or infected wound or too ill to work (work factor below 35%), worst off first; medics are never patients. Patients in beds heal faster, get +3 mood a day and do not work while admitted, and meds work better; everyone else keeps working and heals slowly. **Treatment limits:** one wound treatment per patient per day (at a base or in the field; illnesses one per condition a day), and every treatment takes a medic's time: each Medic-role survivor at a base gives 3 + Medicine/4 treatments a day (fewer while ill), with nobody on the role the best at Medicine gives 2 a day, and surgery takes 3 of one medic's (most of a day; with no medic that free the patient gets a plain treatment). **Automatic care** (one rule for every automatic dose, each night): worst off first, it treats wounds below 70 health or infected and illnesses that matter, never takes the base's meds below its reserve ("Keep N meds in reserve", default 0; your own Treat buttons can use the reserve) and never beyond the medics' time; it can be switched off per base. The Medical tab shows medic time used today, what tonight's care will do, each patient's bed status and why, when they can next be treated (and why a Treat button is disabled), and a health-per-day figure that is the expected change over the next day (passive healing hour by hour, tonight's treatment, illness). Severe wounds (below 30 health) heal at 40% speed without a bed and at the tier's speed in one; from the Clinic up, surgery makes treatments of severe wounds 50% bigger and med stations make +1 meds a day (staffed only, like everything a hospital does). A kit in the treater's off hand adds to their Medicine for every treatment, at a base, in the field or in battle: bandage and splint kit +2 (each treatment uses it up a little), surgical kit +4 (full at Medicine 10, less below, nothing below 3). The base's Medical tab lists the injured with a Treat button (uses base meds). People out on a team are treated with the team's carried meds (weaker than base care): a 1-hour team order from the team panel or the People tab, and only while the team is in contact. A team also patches up its own on its own once a day (`medical.js` `fieldCareDaily`, `medfinds.js` `FIELD_CARE`): anyone below 40 health or with an infected wound gets a field treatment from the team's meds (1, +1 to clean an infection), as long as it carries them; illnesses on the road take the team's meds the same way (severity 45+ or pneumonia). Meds are only ever spent on need: a treatment of someone hurt or sick (at a base from its stock, in the field from the team's), a fighter downed or below half health in battle; never a flat daily drain (`tools/medstest.js` checks a base where everyone is well spends none). Without contact the People tab does not show a team's health either. Wounds below 40 health can get infected (half healing at a base, worse on the road); a field hospital bed prevents it, and from the Clinic up a treatment cleans it for sure (night care treats infected wounds first).
- **Illness and injuries** (`src/sim/health.js`, every number in `SICK`; the worm is a separate condition with its own stages and cures, below): named conditions with a severity (0–100) that drift each day (better with rest, a camp fire, a hospital bed and Medicine; worse with hunger or thirst, on the march, and for chest colds, flu and pneumonia in the cold without heat), cost health while they last (pneumonia kills the weak without meds), cut how much the person can work, cost mood (up to 1.5 a day) and aim in battle (up to −15), and can spread. Deep cut (work, wounds: an untreated cut can become an infected wound), sprain, chest cold (🤧 colds going round and the cold; catching; can go to the lungs), fever (heat, hunger, filth on a site; can turn into pneumonia), flu (cold, crowding; catching; pneumonia in the cold), dysentery (dirty water, spoiled food, raw food at a fireless camp, thirst; catching; drains water), pneumonia (kills without meds). **Where they come from** (a chance per person a day, at most one new case a day; `illnessRisk` lists the parts): ordinary life (0.6% a chest cold), the weather (`exposureRisk`: in full on the road, a lit camp fire keeps most of the cold off; at a base 60% for outdoor roles and a quarter of half indoors, unless the base is cold — no heating fuel, `needs.js` `heatDaily`, or a shelter with its fire out — then indoors is as cold as out), hunger or thirst gauges below 25 (up to 5–6% a day), dirty water (0.4% without a water filter, 0.1% with one, ×3 while thirsty, ×2 with nothing boiled), spoiled food (food stored outside in the warm), crowding (more people than beds) and contagion (each contagious case: more when crowded, less in quarantine, more in a team's close quarters, a third once treated; medics catch it more often), work at a base and searching a site (cuts, sprains, fevers), a hit of 10+ that does not kill (25% a deep cut). **Frailty** multiplies the risk: hunger or thirst below 50 (up to ×2 each), below 50 health ×1.3, hard work at a base (building, scavenging ×1.25), a long march under a load (needs.js counts the hours; up to ×2.5), sleeping rough on a team ×1.25; full gauges ×0.85, and a staffed hospital lowers everything but the weather and work (×(1 − 0.12 × tier)). **Treatment is a course** of 1–3 doses by severity (below 35 one, below 65 two, else three; pneumonia at least two), 1 meds a dose, one a day: each dose takes severity off and stops it worsening; after the last the illness is *on the mend* (no longer worsens or turns into pneumonia, barely spreads, half the health cost, fades 6 a day). At a base the doses come from the base's meds and a medic's time (automatic care each night, see Medical, or the Treat buttons); on a team or at a camp from the team's meds (packed, then found), given by the member with the best Medicine (+ kit): automatically each night for anything serious (severity 40+, pneumonia, 5+ health a day) or by hand (Treat on the team panel or People tab while in contact). No meds where the patient is: the dose is not given, a log line each night (*No meds at …*, *No meds on the team …*) and a 💊 alert. Three or more contagious cases bring an *Outbreak* card (quarantine, a dose for everyone now, or carry on). **On screen:** each condition is a chip with its icon, name and days left (or *worsening*), the dose count, and a Treat button (People tab, survivor screen, team panel, Medical tab; the roster shows the chips too); the Bases tab and a base's Overview have an *Illness* line (who is ill, meds spent on illness this week, today's risk a person, red with no meds); alerts: 🤒 *fell ill* (a day), 🦠 *illness spreading* (two or more contagious and someone caught it lately), 💊 *no meds*, ✚ *untreated for two days*. The autopilot doses the sick, wants meds hard while the sick need more doses than it has, and crafts a water filter for any base without one. The census `node tools/sickcensus.js [days=112] [runs] [normal|hardcore]` counts illnesses by kind, cause and season, meds spent on them, deaths and meds held.
- **Purified and un-purified water** (`src/sim/water.js` helpers, `worm.js` purifyDaily / drinkHour, `economy.js` baseDaily; `node tools/watertest.js`): two supplies, `water` (*Purified water*: boiled, filtered, treated, or bottled water found in shops, houses and crates) and `rawwater` (*Un-purified water*, value 0.5, 1 kg: from the land). Everything a base draws from the land goes in un-purified (its well or spring, Wells, a stream, rain catchers, claimed wells, prisoners hauling it), and so does what a team fetches (Fetch water, the staging camp's river). Each day before drinking a lit Water boiler purifies the day's drinking for everyone fed there (it burns its firewood only on days the purified water in store and the filters do not cover it), each water filter `WORM.PURIFY_FILTER` 10 a day (on top of its +3 purified water), a shelter's lit fire like the boiler. People drink purified water first, then un-purified water (the worm's chance on the share drunk un-purified, × the eggs of the base's ground water); crops take un-purified water first; tanks, cisterns, thirst and the outlook count both. **Purification tablets are for the road**: a base uses its armory's only in an emergency, when its boiler is cold with nothing to burn (no firewood, no junk it may burn, no fuel; or a shelter's fire has nothing to burn) and it has no water filter (one per 10 water still to be drunk; "Out of firewood at …: purification tablets used for the water (N left)"). A team packs purified water first (and a tube of tablets for a water haul or a trip of more than two days: the autopilot and the planner's checkbox); it drinks purified water first, and its un-purified water is safe, in this order, through a water filter carried, at a lit camp fire that can be kept going (firewood carried or deadfall on the land; it also boils 6 an hour, 12 with a stove, into purified water), or with tablets (one per 10: on the move, or at a camp whose fire is out or has nothing left to burn); otherwise it is drunk as it is. Any un-purified water can carry the worm (`WORM.RAW_BASE` 0.35% for a full day's drinking, on top of the eggs), so a base's boiler works whenever un-purified water would be drunk. The top bar shows *Water N +M raw*; the base card's water row shows both, the purification a day and its sources; the team panel says how it drinks its un-purified water ("dropping a tablet in the river water"). Trade: un-purified water is worth about half; other groups keep one water pool (they take un-purified water into it and sell only purified water). Loot: bottled water stays purified; farms and campgrounds (and the raid's farmhouse and campsite areas) have a little rain-barrel water. Old saves keep their water as purified water (a team's water marked unboiled becomes un-purified).
- **The worm** (`src/sim/worm.js`, numbers in `WORM` and `HERD`; lore in Help → *The dead and the worm*): nobody turns from a bite; people swallow the worm's eggs, and a tile's Infection is how thick they lie (below 10% the land is clean enough; eggs = (infection − 0.1) / 0.9). *Catching it* (per person): any un-purified water, `RAW_BASE` 0.35% for a full day's drinking (clean ground and rain too: about one case per 10 people per 30 days on nothing else), plus 2% a day × eggs × the share of its water from wells, springs, streams and prisoners hauling it, × the share of the day's drinking that was un-purified; residents, prisoners, guests and a holding convoy's crew at your base, teams on the road, and your people held by a group (half a base's chance there) (nothing with a boiler, filters or a fed fire; nothing with a **Water boiler** burning (10 scrap to build; it burns any burnable: `WORM.BOILER_WOOD_HEAD` 0.2 firewood's worth a day for everyone fed there, in every season but only on days un-purified water would be drunk and no filter covers it, **firewood first, then the armory's burnable gear the base allows, then fuel** (0.04 a head): items marked *Boiler may burn it* in the inventory (any value, weapons included) and, while the base's *Boiler may burn spare wooden items and paper* is on (the boiler's card; on by default), cheap junk (`woodcutting.js` `isJunk`: a pack item with a GEAR `burn` value worth 5 or less: novels, comics, magazines, crossword books, rag boots, toilet paper, candles; never weapons, tools, armour or bags, nor anything marked *Keep from the fire* or worn), the cheapest firewood first, whole items (what they make beyond the day stays as firewood), with a log line ("The water boiler at … burned two paperback novels and a stack of magazines (1 firewood's worth)"); a day with nothing to burn it stays cold (filters, then the emergency tablets, else unsafe water); the water row, the boiler's card and the Manage screen's upkeep line say what it burns and whether it is lit; its firewood is in the winter's wood plan (`woodcutting.js` woodNeed: 14 days of it kept, the stocking season always on while it burns) so the autopilot's woodcutters cover it, an outpost's comes from the hub over its supply line, and where the land gives too little wood the autopilot crafts water filters instead), a water filter there, or a shelter's fire lit); water on the road (packed at a base whose water is unsafe, fetched from an infected river, drunk from it at the staging camp: the team tracks how much of what it carries is unboiled; a camp fire boils 6 an hour, 12 with a smokeless stove; a water filter carried cleans what is fetched and drunk); raw meat, 4% a ration eaten (game, fish and snares taken on land at 25%+ infection carry eggs: a **Cook fire** or a preserving kitchen cooks everything a base eats, drying racks and a hunting cabin's smoke house take that meat first, otherwise it waits in the stores and is eaten last, raw, when nothing else is left; a team cooks it at a camp fire and eats it raw only as the last resort); camping on land at 50%+ infection, 3% × infection a day (half with the fire lit); searching a site on infected ground (a site search, a raid, a city crawl: hands that handled the dead), `WORM.SEARCH` 0.4% an hour × eggs for each searcher, kept on the team's hands for the day and rolled when the day turns or the team gets home, skipped `SEARCH_WASH` 75% of the time when the team washes (camped with its fire lit that night, or home the same day to a base whose water is safe; the planner's notes and the tile panel warn for sites at 30%+ eggs: "infected ground: the worm, N% a day a person searching"; no gloves exist in GEAR yet, so nothing halves it); crops and forage from land at `CROP_MIN` 40%+ infection (fresh harvests `CROP` 1.5% a ration eaten raw, forage from the base job or a team's Forage order `FORAGE` 2%: kept like the meat with eggs (`base.wormCrop`, `base.wormForage`, `cargo.wormForage`), eaten after the clean food and the preserved and before the raw meat, cooked by a cook fire or a kitchen (boiled greens) or a team's camp fire, dried by the racks after the meat; dry grain and beans are safe; the Farming tab's Ground row says when a base's land is that bad). Un-purified water and uncooked food also give an **Upset stomach** (`health.js` stomach, 🥴; `WORM.SICK_X` 3× the worm's chance for the same drink or meal, at most 25% a roll): no health, no thirst, almost no work lost, −1.5 mood a day for two to four days, then it passes; treating it is optional and decisive: *Treat (1 meds)* on the Medical tab (a medic's treatment) or a team's panel (the team's meds) cures it at once; automatic care, a team's night care and the autopilot leave it alone. It replaces the old dirty-water and cold-camp dysentery; spoiled food is still dysentery. *Its course* (`DAY_HP`, `DAY_MOOD`): it shows from the day after it is caught; −3, −6, −10, −15, −20, −24, −30 health on days 1–7 (then −40 a day) and −2 … −8 mood a day (relations.js addMood); every healing heals at half the rate while it is in them (`WORM.HEAL`: rest and hospital beds, treatments and field treatment, camp rest, a holding convoy, a prisoner's rations, a battle's patch-up), and the ramp outpaces it: someone at full health dies on day 7 with no rest, day 8 resting at a base, day 9 in a Clinic's or a Trauma hospital's bed with a medic (the default admit line, Medicine 6), at best day 10–11 (Medicine 10, admitted at once); hurt, sooner. Days 1–4 the gut (an extra ration a day, works at 85%), from day 5 the brain (fits and staggering, works at 30%). Old saves keep the stage and move onto the new count in proportion. *Spotting*: the damage shows from day 1: at a base each day 50% + 5% per point of the best Medicine there (×1.5 with someone on the Medic role); a team from day 1, 35% + 5% per point (the card *The worm*: a dewormer if carried, purge it with 2 meds and a Medicine check, end it, or hope it passes, 25%); the Medical tab and the survivor screen show *Infected (day N of about 7): −X health today, −Y mood* and the cure open now; the brain stage shows itself (always; a card at a base: surgery, lock them away, end it; on a team the card again with the purge disabled). *Cures* (Medical tab buttons and the night's automatic care, which uses a dewormer first and may dip into the meds reserve: the worm kills for sure): a **Dewormer** (new item: pharmacies, clinics, hospitals, farms and ranches; made at a med station, Medicine 5, 3 meds + 1 food) cures the gut stage for sure; a purge (2 meds and a medic's treatment, 50% + 4% per Medicine point + 8% per hospital tier, at most 95%; −25 health, or −10 and the worm holds on); surgery in the brain stage needs a staffed Clinic or better (4 meds, three of a medic's treatments, 35% + 4% per point, at most 90%; otherwise they die on the table). *Prisoners and guests* (`wormOthersAt`, `otherTreatInfo`, `applyOtherTreat`): your prisoners and guests sheltering at your base catch it from the same water, are spotted the same way (a log line) and take the same three cures: the Medical tab lists them under *Prisoners* and *Guests*, the Prisoners tab and the guests' card in the Groups tab have the buttons. A guest cured: their group's goodwill +8 (a failed purge or a death on the table +3: an honest attempt); a prisoner cured: opinion of you +12 (+5 for a failed purge) and their group's goodwill +3 more when you release them or trade them back. The night's care treats them only with the base's *Treat prisoners and guests too* (Medical tab, off by default; the autopilot turns it on while guests are sheltering there). *Death and rising* (`wormRise`, from carriers.js riseOnMap: anyone who dies with the worm in them): at your base (residents, a holding crew, prisoners, guests) they go for the nearest people (one hurt by 10–35, a second half the time, each killed 12% of the time, half that with a guard on duty; everyone −8 mood), the body put down and its things in the armory; locked away (the brain card, a prisoner idle in a cell) nobody is hurt; on the road the card *<name> rose* (put them down: the team against one of the dead by the numbers, 6–60% someone is hurt, their things kept; leave them: a death site with their things; crush the skull before they rise when someone saw the last breath, 20% + 6% per point of the best Medicine or Scouting; the autopilot picks by `ai` weights); alone they walk off with their things; in a city crawl's block card the encounter *One of you turned* (the Alert +6), inside a building the unit dies and one of the dead stands on its cell and floor (a battle hour costs an infected unit a 24th of the day's damage); at another group's base the group puts the body down (15% it loses one of its own; a visiting team costs −5 goodwill, a prisoner nothing); on a personal errand a death site where they fell. *Infested animals*: game on land at 60%+ infection breeds herds of infested deer and hogs (at most 2 per 1,000 tiles, one born 8% of the days; 3–8 animals; 60–120 days) that do not attack but wander (70% of days a tile, to the cleanest land next to them 60% of the time: out of the dead land, toward farms and bases) and seed the ground they stand on (its own infection level +0.006 × size / 6 a day, up to 0.35). A share of the game on infected land is infested ((infection − 0.35) / 0.5, at most 80%, +50% with a herd there): each such kill gives a quarter of its food, all of it with eggs, and one fewer in the herd (hunted out, it is gone). Hunt estimates count them; the tile panel shows the infested share and a herd you can see; the State Map draws herds in sight (the Hordes layer). The water row (Farming and Medical tabs) says whether the water is safe; the alerts list everyone known to carry it; the team panel says how much of its water and game is unboiled or uncooked. The autopilot builds a water boiler when a base drinks un-purified water (filters first where the land around gives less firewood than the boiler burns), keeps enough woodcutters to feed it, builds a cook fire once game, crops or forage with eggs come in (or farmers, foragers, hunters work infected land), keeps dewormers on hand (crafted at a med station: two at a hub, one at an outpost, while meds allow), puts a queued water boiler (or cook fire) first in the queue with a builder while the base drinks un-purified water (eats food with eggs uncooked), sends the sick from a base that cannot cure them (no dewormer, not the meds and someone to purge) to the nearest one that can on a one-way supply run when they get there before the brain stage, or else meds from the hub for two purges, picks the dewormer, surgery or the purge on the cards (lock them away or end it in the brain stage), and moves a team off badly infested land before it camps. *The other groups* (`WORM.GROUPS`, `groupsDaily`): a group base on infected ground loses people at the rate a player base of its size would catch it (heads × eggs × (water 2% + crops 1.5% on land at 40%+) × `UNCURED` 0.5), × the type (military and fortress hubs boil their water ×0.3, traders ×0.6, communes farm the infected land ×1.2, raiders, zealots and expansionists ×1), and after a base's first loss it boils its water too (×0.3, the `wormBoilers` flag per base); never below `MIN` 4 people, so the worm alone never breaks a group; their parties on the move and hardcore's bands lose one now and then on a herd's land or ground at 50%+ infection (`ROAD` 3% × eggs a day). Losses are radio news (the World affairs screen, logged when heard), chronicle notes and a line on the group's card in the Groups tab.
- **Newcomers ask first** (`src/sim/recruits.js`): everyone who would join is a card: a stranger at the gate, refugees after a group collapses (1–3), a loner on the road, survivors saved or freed after a road fight (rescued from the dead, a robbery's victims, prisoners bought or freed), a lost teenager (hub event) and a deserter coming back. The card window shows each candidate (portrait, health, best skills, traits, what they carry) and the hub's free beds, days of food and water, now and with them (and on the road the team's food and water against the way home). At a base: *Take them in* (5 food each on arrival), *Take only X in* (several at once), *Send them to* an outpost (they walk: about 1 day per 8 tiles, 97% − 1.2% per tile to make it, at least 60%; free beds there shown), *Give them supplies and part ways* (3 food and 3 water each: +2 mood for everyone there, +2 goodwill with known groups that are not hostile within 12 tiles), *Turn them away*. On the road: *Take them along* (only when the team's food and water cover everyone on the way home), *Send them to* the nearest base or the hub on foot, part ways (2 food and 2 water each from the team, +2 team mood, the same goodwill), turn away. Walkers arrive (or never do, logged) after their days on the road.
- **Morale, crowding and desertion** (`src/sim/morale.js`): beds: +1 mood a day when everyone has one; over the beds, −(2 + 8 × over) a day up to −16, where over = (people − beds) / beds (11 in 10 beds −2.8, 15 in 10 −6, 20 in 10 −10).
