Every 120–180 days a site for a new hive is picked on open ground 4–9 tiles from the nearest hive, away from people (one at a time; on a bigger map more of them, and the days between sites and between border hordes divided by its area: a Huge map picks a site every 49–73 days, up to two at once, and gets a border horde every 2–4 days); hives within 10 tiles of it are *supporters* and send up to two hordes each until enough of the dead are gathered or on the way, the rest are *defenders* that send hordes out to roam near home (1 + half their cluster size at a time, and only while the state holds fewer free dead than 1,000 + 250 per hive and their surroundings fewer than 600; medium ones only before day 60, then large ones too) and, more often in the first two months (20% of new hordes at first, 40% on day 30, 65% from about day 70, always with a Wallbreaker), to attack a base: yours by your numbers and distance (weighed 2.5× a group's, from up to 45 tiles), or a group's by its size (within 22). While the hives near a build site feed it, they send out fewer hordes: a lull you can use. Hordes reaching a site gather into one mass; once the hordes delivered add up to a **score of 30** the mass starts turning into a hive (a quarter of it, at least 25 and at most 40, a day), and the hive stands when **1,000 of the dead** have gone into it (up to 150 left over stay as its guard): about four weeks from the first gathering, the window to strike the hives that feed it or thin the mass. Wandering hordes within 6 tiles of a site that still needs the dead are drawn to it. Every 5–9 days a horde of random size (half small, a third medium, the rest large, a little bigger as the year goes on) comes in from off the map on a border land tile at least 6 tiles from your bases, so destroying every hive makes the state safer but never safe. A hive holds the land within 4 tiles: sites there show at least 3 danger skulls, red on the ring 1 tile around the hive, where every one of the dead met (encounters, raids on its sites, culls, horde cards) is much tougher (+3 power; +1 elsewhere in its land). It radiates infection (1 on its tile, 0.1 less per tile out to 6); tiles move toward that or their own level by 0.02 a day up and 0.01 down, and when a hive is destroyed the land within 7 tiles settles toward a low level over the following weeks (the overlay and the hotzone counts follow). **Destroy hive** (mission on a hive tile, also right-click): a card with the hive's guard, then a tactical battle against its brood (10–18 of the dead at power 5+ on its red-skull ground) and the **Broodmother** (300 health), around the hive's flesh mound; win and the hive is destroyed. Or set fires around it (6 fuel) to burn part of its guard and stores. **Destroy every hive with none being built and you win** (from year 8, once the outbreak beyond the border has burned out and the state has stayed clear for a season: see *Eras and winning*): a victory screen with *Watch the timelapse*, *Keep playing* and *New campaign* (`world.js checkGameOver`).
- **Hive birth you can see (`src/sim/hivebirth.js`, every number in `BIRTH`):** a new hive is no longer born out of sight. The day the hives pick a build site you may hear a rumour (log and diary: "Travellers talk of the dead drifting toward …") when the site is inside your radio coverage, within 12 tiles of a base of yours or within 6 of a team in the field. Once the gathering is known by the usual rules (in view of a base or a team in radio contact, or in a report that reached the hub) a card **"The dead are massing near X"**: *Break it up* (the planner opens aimed at the gathering with the Cull horde mission; also marks it), *Mark it and watch it* (the default: an alert that tracks the days left, red under a week, and a dashed red ring on the map) or *Ignore it*. The build-site mound on the map grows with the dead massed there and has a line under it, the days until the hive stands as far as you know (from what is in view, or the last report less the days since, with a "?" once it is over 3 days old); the tile panel says the same. A Cull horde team at the gathering gets **Break up the gathering**: a tactical battle against 6 of the dead at the edge of the mass (+1 per 80 massed, at most 12) at power 2 (+0.6 per point of the ground's toughness), with no Broodmother: far smaller than storming a hive (10–18 of the brood at power 5+ and the Broodmother). Win and the gathering scatters: 30% of it falls and the rest run 5 tiles off for 6 days, and the hordes still on their way are called off. Before the mound is begun (a score under 30 delivered) the site is given up and no new site is picked for at least 30 days; once it is under way the mound loses 40% of the dead gone into it (at least 150), has to gather a score of 30 again, and the hives stop feeding it for 10 days. Fall back and what the team cut down is gone from the mass. If a hive you knew of rises anyway, a card says so (plan an attack with the planner aimed at it, or not now). The autopilot marks every gathering and sends a Cull horde squad of 4 (at least 3) when a known gathering is within its range (×1.15, Aggressive ×1.5) and its best fighters have the odds (its hive odds ×1.2, Aggressive ×0.9), one such trip at a time. The hive pace itself (sites every 120–180 days, the 30-score / 1,000-dead rule) is unchanged.
- **Hive tech tree (`src/sim/hivetech.js`, the special dead in `zombietypes.js`; every number in `hiveconst.js` `TECH`, `GROWTHS`, `ZTYPES`):** a hive spends resources on **growths**, structures it grows on land within 2 tiles of itself (not water or a bridge, a site, a ruin, a base, a hive or build site, another growth), one at a time, at least 12 days apart, up to 10, each taking days to grow:

  | Growth | Tier | Cost | Days | Gives its hive |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Biomass pit (up to 2) | 0 | 12 | 10 | +1 resource a day |
  | Brood chamber | 0 | 20 | 15 | guard kept at +40; from tier 2 one more horde out |
  | Worm cyst | 0 | 18 | 15 | Carriers in its hordes (the first special dead: a young hive grows its first pit, then this) |
  | Feeding grounds | 1 | 25 | 20 | income ×1.5 |
  | Runner warren | 1 | 25 | 20 | runners in its hordes |
  | Brute pit | 2 | 35 | 30 | brutes |
  | Spitter sac | 2 | 35 | 30 | spitters |
  | Screamer spire | 3 | 40 | 40 | screamers; attacks bases 10 tiles farther; calls wandering dead within 6 into its guard |
  | Crawler burrow | 3 | 40 | 40 | crawlers |
  | Wallbreaker den | 3 | 50 | 45 | Wallbreakers (with `HIVE.BOSS_DEN` they come from nowhere else) |
  | Mutation vat | 4 | 60 | 60 | its special dead are elite (×1.3 hp, ×1.2 damage) |

  **Tiers** open by the calendar and the hive's age (`TECH.TIER_DAY` 0 / 120 / 450 / 1100 / 1500, each up to 35% later per hive; `TIER_AGE` 0 / 20 / 90 / 200 / 400 days old; in the game's 112-day years, see *Eras and winning*): Carriers from the first months (the worm cyst is tier 0; a young hive grows it after its first pit, so about the second month), runners from year 2 (the early game), brutes and spitters from year 5–6 (the mid game), screamers, crawlers and Wallbreakers from year 10–13 (the late game; no Wallbreakers before), elite dead from year 14, so the threat grows over a 10–15 year game; a hive that rises late starts again at tier 0. **The hive's mind** (`growthWish`, daily after its income): its guard first; income while young or poor; a brood chamber when it was attacked within 60 days (fires, a storm, a growth burned); a kind of dead its cluster-mates do not breed yet over one they do; the den and the vat high once open; a growth it cannot afford it saves for (no new roaming hordes) for up to 20 days. **The special dead** (`ZTYPES`): a hive that breeds a kind puts about its share into every horde it buys (runners 15%, brutes and spitters 8%, Carriers and crawlers 6%, screamers 3%, × 0.6–1.4, together at most 45%: `h.mix`, kept through merges by numbers and through splits); border hordes carry the era's kinds at half that from day 900 (year 9). In battle (`combat.js` hooks: `applyZType`, `zombieTurn`, `zTick`, `zRevealed`, the `acid` weapon) each is a unit with its own sprite, ring color and letter: **runner** (6 tiles an action, 0.85× hp; counter: walls, overwatch), **brute** (2.4× hp, takes ¾ of every hit, 1.5× damage, slow; smashes the barricade, wall section, fence or crates in its way when it cannot reach anyone; counter: armor, heavy guns, pits and trenches), **spitter** (acid at 4–6 tiles every other turn, cover a step less against it, keeps its distance in between; when it dies, by any hand in any battle, its sac bursts (`acid.js`, `ACID`): its tile and the 8 round it (open floor at its height, not through walls) pool with acid for 3 rounds, everyone living there is splashed for 10–16 at once, and the living who step in (once a round) or start their side's turn in it take 6–10, armour stopping only half as much; the dead walk through it unharmed, people's AI paths round it when it can (dearer, never closed), the move preview turns green and the tile hint warns; worth ×1.6 in a fight by the numbers; `tools/acidtest.js`; counter: smoke, shoot it first, kill it away from your people), **screamer** (hangs back ~5 tiles and shrieks every other turn, calling 1–2 more of the dead from the enemy's edge, 6 a battle; counter: kill it first: the AI's shooters do), **crawler** (unseen and unshootable until within 2.5 tiles of one of yours, lit or it has struck; its first bite ×1.6; counter: floodlights, flares, staying together). Fights resolved by the numbers count each by its weight (`typeStrength`). On the State Map: the walls hold a horde as its size × the mix's `wall` weight (brutes 2.5, spitters 1.4, screamers 1.2, runners 0.9, crawlers 0.5); every 12 brutes smash a defence like a Wallbreaker (2 at most); crawlers slip past held walls (60% of them, half less with a watchtower, half with lit floodlights, a quarter if manned) and 4+ are a small ambush battle inside (lost: 15% of the food and meds); a horde with 10%+ runners moves a quarter faster; screamers in a horde pull wandering hordes within 3 tiles along; warnings, cull and horde cards and the tile panel name the special dead. **The player:** growths show on the map beside a hive you know of (live or last reported: faint while growing, grey husks after the hive), in the tile panel (what it does, its flesh) and the hive's row (its tier and growths); one finished in sight is logged, one out of sight at a hive you know of reaches you as a rumour 40% of the time and goes on the map; a team that comes upon a hive sees them too. **Burn the growth** (mission on its tile, right-click): fight its guard (6 + 1 per 40 in the hive's guard, at most 12, power 2 + 0.6 × the ground's toughness, the hive's special dead plus a quarter of the growth's own kind; the "growth" battle layout), or burn it: 4 fuel burns 60 of its 100 flesh, 2 Molotovs 100; it regrows 5 a day. Gone, the hive loses what it gave and marks itself attacked. Destroying the hive withers its growths (no effect; gone after 40 days). **The autopilot** burns or fights the most dangerous growth it knows of (a spawner, the den or the vat, never pits or brood chambers; den, burrow, vat first; near its bases ahead) when no hive can be stormed yet, with a crew of 4 and fuel for two fires (`growthCandidate`, `growthChoice`). The hives' choices (what and where to grow, a horde's mix, the spire, rumours) roll their own dice (`hivetech.js roll`: a hash of the seed, the hour and what is rolled for), so they never shift the world's random numbers. Levers for the long-game balance: `TECH.TIER_DAY` / `TIER_AGE` / `JITTER` (the pace), `TECH.MIX_MAX` and each `ZTYPES[k].share` (how many special dead), `ZTYPES[k].wall` / `weight` (their worth at the walls and by the numbers), the battle stats in `ZTYPES` (`hp`, `dmg`, `mob`, `armor`, `cooldown`, `callMax`, `reveal`, `ambush`, `slip`, `smashEvery`), `GROWTHS[k].cost` / `days` / `income` / `incomeMult` / `guard` / `out` / `outTier`, `HIVE.BOSS_DEN`, `TECH.GAP` / `SAVE_DAYS` / `THREAT_DAYS`, `TECH.BORDER_DAY` / `BORDER_SHARE`, the growth fight and fires (`GUARD_*`, `FIRE_*`, `BOMB*`, `REGROW`), `SPIRE_*`, `ELITE_*`.
- **The armoured dead (`src/sim/deadsoldiers.js`, every number in `MILDEAD`; their stats as special dead in `hiveconst.js` `ZTYPES.soldier` / `ZTYPES.riot`):** soldiers and riot police who turned in their gear. No hive breeds them (no growth unlocks them, `share` 0); they are where they fell. **Dead soldier** (`soldier`): 1.3× an ordinary zombie's health, the same bite (a walker's damage, hit chance and critical chance exactly: no bonus for being armoured; the 1.7 they weigh in a fight by the numbers is how hard they are to put down, not how hard they hit), never faster than a walker (the kit weighs them down), a plate carrier and a combat helmet: each hit is × the armour for the weapon that struck (`MILDEAD.ARMOR.soldier`: pistol 0.5, revolver 0.6, shotgun 0.6, bows 0.45–0.55, lever-action 0.7, carbine, assault rifle and hunting rifle 0.75 (no rifle a full counter); knife 0.4, spear 0.45, machetes 0.5, fire axe 0.75, baton 0.75, bat 0.85, nail and spiked bats 0.8, crowbar 0.85, sledgehammer 1; a gun butt 0.8). Through it: a **critical hit** (a round under the helmet's rim, a blade in a gap: the armour is ignored, so Deadeye and the Assassin perk shine), a **blade from behind** or on one that has not noticed you (knife, machetes, pipe spear, fire axe: a knife to the base of the skull, `stabsGap`), **fire** (Molotovs and burning ground ×1) and **pipe bombs** (×0.85); spike pits and bear traps ×0.8. **Dead riot cop** (`riot`): 1.25× health, hard-shell riot gear the other way round: blows and blades glance off (bat 0.6, baton 0.5, knife 0.45, sledge 0.8), pistols 0.7, buckshot 0.75, but rifle rounds go straight through (×1). By the numbers they weigh 1.7 and 1.5 (`weight`), at the walls 1.1. `combat.js damage(b, t, raw, by, nonLethal, hit)` asks `armorVs` with the hit (`{ how: shot | melee | bash | fire | blast, crit, sneak }`); the AI's shot choice and the battle tooltip (how much of the selected unit's weapon gets through) use it too. **Where** (`siteDeadMix`, deterministic: no world dice): the share of them among a site's dead, as a horde-style mix placed by share (`zombietypes.js battleTypes` / `mixList`) in raids on the street map (`createRaid`), inside buildings (`buildingmaps.js placeInterior`), in road encounters (`conflicts.js enemyGroup`) and among the dead drawn in by noise later (`nextOfMix`, `b.zmix`): National Guard bases 45%, army helicopter wrecks 50%, the tiles next to a military site (the old cordon) 15%, within 2 on a road (the checkpoints) 8%, a fallen military base in hardcore 60% (30% next to it; read from `state.hardcore.groups.military`); police stations 30% riot police, prisons 20%, city centres 5%; anywhere else a lone soldier 3% of the time (a site, a week). The city crawl (`citycrawl.js`): a police station among a block's places has its riot police (30%), a barricaded checkpoint's street map half dead soldiers (`MILDEAD.CHECKPOINT`), and the street hordes the city's share; the hardcore military arc's horde of the fallen (`HC_MILITARY.FALLEN_SOLDIER`) and the strike's dead merged into a hive's guard come in as dead soldiers. A horde crossing a military site nobody holds picks some up (+6% a pass, up to 15%: `hordeMilitaryPickup`), and they come with it to your walls; `makeHorde(state, { ..., mix: { soldier: 0.6 } })` is the horde of the fallen. The hour-by-hour search at such a site is harder (threat + 4 × the mix's extra weight) and a won fight there may leave one to strip (share × 1.2). **Their bodies** (`bodyLoot`; `inventory.js enemyBody` after a battle, `deadBodyFinds` in a raid, and by the numbers a body or two after a road fight): a soldier a plate carrier (15%) or a Kevlar vest (30%) worn to 30–60%, a **combat helmet** (`milhelmet`, new: head armour 1.4; 35%), 3–10 rounds (60%) and a rifle magazine's 10–20 (15%), now and then an assault rifle, a carbine, a military radio, a smoke grenade, a flare, a bandage kit; a riot cop the riot helmet (35%), arm and leg guards, a baton, cuffs, a few rounds. Levers: `MILDEAD.ARMOR`, `ZTYPES.soldier.hp` / `weight`, `MILDEAD.SITE` / `NEAR` / `ROAD` / `FALLEN` / `RARE`, `HORDE_ADD` / `HORDE_MAX`, `SEARCH_THREAT` / `SEARCH_BODY`, `MILDEAD.BODY`. Sprites: standing `sprites.js paintArmoredDead(kind, seed, look)` (`armoredCanvas`): rotting skin in the zombie palette, camo with a sleeve torn off, a plate carrier with pouches, a helmet with its strap hanging; the riot cop in black with a hard shell, shoulder pads, guards and a cracked visor pushed up. Tested by `tools/milzedtest.js` (the balance tables too).
- **The Carrier, the worm swarm and the human dead (`src/sim/carriers.js`, every number in `hiveconst.js` `CARRIER`; their stats as special dead in `ZTYPES.carrier` / `ZTYPES.swarm`; `tools/carriertest.js`):** the dead carry the worm, and a **Carrier** (`Ca`, a pale ring) is one of them so full of it that it is swollen: 1.5× an ordinary zombie's health, 0.8× its bite, slow (2.5 tiles an action), shot first by the AI. The **worm cyst** (tier 0, 18 resources, 15 days) is the first special dead a hive breeds: a young hive grows its first biomass pit, then the cyst; its hordes carry about 6% Carriers (× 0.6–1.4), and border hordes some from day 900. **Raising the dead, at once:** a human body (anyone killed: your people, raiders, soldiers, neutrals; never one of the dead, never someone only down or knocked out) within 2 tiles of a Carrier (diagonals count as 1) gets up with no warning: on the Carrier's own turn as it ends its move (and acts at once), or at the start of the enemy turn for a body killed near one during yours, as an ordinary zombie ("Raider 3 (turned)", or for your own people and the third side the first name: "Dana (turned)") with 1 action, going for every living thing. The map marks a body in danger with a pale "!" while a Carrier is within 8 tiles or the worm is in it (red: within 2, it gets up at the end of this turn); the hover text names what can be done. An aware Carrier with none of the living next to it walks to the nearest body within 8 tiles. **Answering a body** (any human body, any time, Carrier or no Carrier): **Crush skull** (U, 1 action, silent, from next to it; the loot stays; the battle AI and the raid bot do it to a body in danger next to them), **Double tap** (J, 1 action, 1 round, the noise of a shot, line of sight: the same from range), **fire** on its tile (as it would rise and at each turn's change: the body and everything on it are gone), or **Pick up body** (P, 1 action: half the move distance and no shooting while carrying, a carried body cannot get up; P again puts it down in front of the carrier, Z lays it in the team's car at a raid's vehicle zone or on a field battle's evac strip when the team came by car, if the car has room: a body weighs `CARRIER.BODY_KG` 70 kg plus its things in the team's load and capacity (`parties.js bodyLoad`) until it is buried or burned). A body in the car cannot rise and comes home with the team (`p.cargo.bodies`, `base.bodies`): the base's Armory tab lists it with **Bury** (its things to the armory) and **Burn** (nothing kept); one with the worm in it left unburied gets up in the night after a day, hurts someone and is put down. **The risen keep what the person wore:** worn armour takes its share of every hit (your people: what they have on, not the Tough trait; others: their vest or plate), no guns. **Their things are never duplicated or lost:** put down and the battle won, one of yours is their own body to loot under "Dana (turned)", anyone else one body with the person's kit (their weapon, armour, pockets); in a raid the body's container goes with the risen and lies where they are put down. **Walking loot:** a risen still walking when a battle ends (any side, any outcome) takes its things off the death site (or out of the armory) into `state.walkingLoot` with its tile and the person's name; the death site stays and says the body walked. Each day an entry drifts a tile, half the days (toward the nearest hive 70% of the time, else at random), so the dead seem to wander. A battle or raid made within 8 tiles of an entry (60% on the tile, less with distance, at most 2 a battle) has one of its ordinary dead marked as that person ("Dana (turned)", in their armour), the entry leaves the pool, and the Diary notes the meeting; put down and the battle won its body has the things on it (a raid: a body container with them in it); still walking at the end it goes back in the pool; put down in a lost fight the things lie at a death site on the tile. **The worm swarm** (`W`; a Carrier that dies bursts, a Sleeper splits, a Flinger throws one): 0.7× health, 0.6× bite but +15 to hit, quick (4 tiles); a single bullet or bolt (pistols, rifles, carbines, bows, crossbows) does 15% of its damage (`CARRIER.SHOT`), a shotgun's spread all of it (`SHOTGUN` 1, by the weapon's `data.js` category), blades 0.9, blows 1.25, blasts 1.5, fire 2.5× as hot. **Its bite infests:** each hit on someone living leaves the worm in them 35% of the time (`INFEST`; a kill always does): an enemy so bitten gets up when killed; one of yours who lives through the battle has caught it (`worm.js catchWorm`, cause "swarm": the normal incubation, but spotted at once since everyone saw the bite), and whoever has the worm and dies, anywhere and any time later, gets up: in a battle where they fell; on the State Map or at a base they tear at someone before they are put down, and alone on the road they walk off with their things into the walking loot (`riseOnMap`, from `killSurvivor`; the worm module's own turning and a person put down by their own people clear the worm first, so nobody rises twice). With no one to go to the swarm sinks into the ground after 6 enemy turns and leaves no body. By the numbers a Carrier weighs 1.5 and presses the walls at 1.1. Battles without Carriers, swarms or walking loot nearby play as before (no dice are drawn for them).
- **The mid game's special dead and the Sleepers (`src/sim/middead.js`, `sleepers.js`; every number in `hiveconst.js` `MIDDEAD`, `SLEEPER`; `tools/specialtest.js`):** five tier-2 growths (year 5 on) each breed a kind: the **Ossuary** Bonebacks (`Bb`: plates in front, a hit from the half it faces does 30%, from the side or behind all of it; hit, it turns on its attacker), the **Sinew scaffold** Climbers (`Cl`: over walls, fences, barricades, a closed gate, cars and crates and up onto walkways without a ladder, never stopping on one; at a held wall half of them get over, fewer with a watchtower and manned defences, and fight inside), the **Worm pit** Flingers (`Fl`: wind up a turn at someone within 7 tiles, then throw a worm swarm next to them; a hit of 10+ while winding drops it at its own feet; 3 throws a battle), the **Egg cistern** Sowers (`Se`: every tile their horde crosses on the State Map gets Infection, 1.5 points a Sower, at most 20 a pass, fading as usual; in battle the infection rises while one stands) and the **Dread hollow** Nightmares (`Nm`: a scream every 3 turns once it sees you: Heat +15 in a raid, noise, the dead within 12 tiles wake, and your people within 6 who fail their nerve at 30% (brave +30, cowardly −20, a friend beside +10) spend their next turn running for the way out or away from the dead). **Sleepers** (`Sl`, from day 1): bodies lie about every fight in the open and every raid map, some of them Sleepers (15% + 25% × Infection) that split into a worm swarm, with a free bite, when anyone living steps next to them; Scouting spots one within 3 tiles (then it can be shot from afar), fire cooks it. **Where the special dead come from:** a hive's new horde rolls for each kind it breeds by its size, the hive's tier and a brood chamber (`HORDEMIX`), a hive assault fields at least one of every kind, and away from the hordes they turn up exploring: more in towns and cities every year, more on a hive's land, the hive's own kinds on its red-skull ring (`AMBIENT`, `hivetech.js siteSpecials`).
- **The fallen (`src/ui/downsprites.js`):** every unit that is down or dead lies on the battle map as its own picture, not its standing sprite turned on its side: a body seen from above on a 26 × 14 grid, built from the same look as the standing sprite (the same seed's colours and clothes, the person's appearance, armour and weapon). The dead (`paintDownedZombie(variant, seed, look)` for walker / runner / bloated / brute: sprawled in a pool of blood, torn clothes and wounds, the runner mid-stride, the bloated one burst open, the brute a slab; `paintDownedZType(kind, seed, look)` for spitter (its acid sac burst, an acid puddle), screamer (mouth still wide, arms over its head), crawler (no legs, arms reaching on), shepherd (its shroud spread out, the bone crook beside it), soldier (camo, plate carrier, helmet), riot (black shell, visored helmet, shin guards); `paintDownedBoss(kind)` for the Wallbreaker (its concrete slab beside it) and the Broodmother (the mound collapsed, egg sacs burst, the crown of bone)). People (`paintDownedSurvivor(app, weapon, armour, pose, { ko, searched })`, `paintDownedEnemy(kit, colour, weapon, armour, seed, pose, opts)`): pose `"down"` (wounded or knocked out: curled on their side, an arm over the stomach, no pool; eyes shut) or `"dead"` (sprawled on their back in a pool, the weapon dropped by the hand). Anyone drawn by `paintSurvivor` (your people, or a unit carrying an appearance `t.app`) or `paintEnemyHuman` (any hostile kit, whatever its side) gets them through `unitDownCanvas(state, b, t, salt)`. A searched body (its raid container searched) has its pockets turned out and its weapon gone. Drawn flat by `drawDowned(ctx, cv, X, Y, ax, ay, { facing, scale })`: top-down with the tile's axes, isometric laid onto the projected floor axes so it turns with the view, in the painter's order with everything else; the tied-up rope still goes over a knocked-out prisoner. **Every body is searchable in a raid**: the dead too (combat.js `damage`: a body container with `zombie`, `ztype` and the unit's id): what the person had on them (`ZBODY`: a phone, lighters, smokes, a snack, a bandage kit, a ring or a watch 30%; a few rounds 8%; food 12%; a pistol with 3–8 rounds 2%; a bag 3%; a key to a nearby place 4%, `locks.js`), a dead soldier's or riot cop's kit as above; a spitter's acid still burns the hands that search it (4–8). One action like any container (two in the dark); the raid bot walks over only for the armoured dead and searches the others when it passes. Battles with a loot step (the bodies after a won fight on the map) already list the dead with their pockets; a dead soldier's body there holds his kit.
- **Jobs for other groups (`src/sim/jobs.js`, Groups tab and the Diplomacy window in `src/ui/diplomacy.js`):** known groups that are not hostile post jobs over the radio (in reach: 6% a day, traders and communes 8%) or at their gates (your team there: 60%, once every 3 days per group); at most one open offer and two jobs under way per group, six in all. An offer is a card (Accept / Maybe later / Decline) and stays on the Jobs list for 4 days (declining or leaving it to expire costs 1 goodwill). Types, each checked by the sim every hour: *clear a horde* within 6 tiles of them (gone or under 40% with your team having come within 1 tile; 12 days), *escort their caravan* to another group (it sets out when your team is at their base, raiders leave it alone while your team is within 2 tiles, done if your team was within 2 tiles for half its road hours; 10 days), *deliver* N of what they want (meds after sickness, food, ammo at war): a team at their base hands it over, or *Ask them to fetch it* and their caravan takes it from your trade base (10 days), *rescue their people* held by a war enemy or raiders (a card at the holders' gates: storm 60% of their guards, talk them out, or pay 40 a head; 14 days), *storm a hive* within 8 tiles of them with their fighters beside you (20 days), *scout a region* (70% of the tiles within 2 of a spot reported after the job; 12 days), *retrieve a package* from a site, a crash site if there is one (picked up by a team on the site, carried to their base or brought home for their caravan; a team lost with it drops it; 14 days). Rewards: 6 + 4 × size goodwill and one or two of supplies from their surplus (about 40 × size), gear you cannot craft, a manual or blueprint you lack (`blueprints.js blueprintReward`), 1–2 recruits (a card), intel (their region, hives and hordes near them) or, for a big job from a group with two or more bases, one of their outposts with two of their people as staff. Missing a deadline: −2 goodwill (small jobs) or −4; a job that can no longer be done through nobody's fault is called off at no cost. Accepted jobs show on the map (a gold *Job* tag, also in the tile tooltip) and in the planner (job targets first in *Move to a site*, a note when the trip's target is a job; the Jobs list's *Plan a trip…* opens the planner on it).
- **Groups and each other (`src/sim/diplomacy.js`, Diplomacy window → World affairs):** a relations matrix between the groups (−100..100: from their types and how close their bases are, drifting 2% a day back toward that; raids outside a war −12; a shared enemy +0.4 a day). Pacts at 28+ (5% a day, broken below 10): pact partners never raid each other and may join each other's wars. Wars at −42 or worse between groups within 18 tiles (1% a day × temper: raiders 1.5 … traders 0.1; three wars at most; 90 days of quiet after a peace) over land, resources or hive ground. Each side raids the other (6% a day, aggressive 9%): sieges as usual (you can answer their call for help), odds att / (att + defence), a win kills 5–10% (never below 3), loots 10%, may take a secondary base (35%) and a captive. Peace when a side is worn out, down to 6 people or after a long war: a lead of 5+ cedes a base (land), 2+ pays 25% tribute, else a white peace; captives go home. News comes over the radio (📻 in the log; the Affairs tab keeps it). A group at war pays 35% more for ammo and meds and 30% more for weapons and armor (both ways) and keeps twice its reserve of food, ammo, meds and fuel; arming one side costs 1 goodwill a deal with the other (3 a day at most). You can stay out, *broker a ceasefire* (both in radio reach, neither hostile, once a week: 20% + 3.5% a Speech point + their goodwill / 400 + weariness / 200; success +8 with both and a thank-you, failure −3), or *join a war* (from the Affairs tab or the card a friend sends when its war starts): the enemy turns hostile (−60) and its war raids come for your bases too, your side +10 and 25% stronger; if your side wins you get half the tribute, or the land it cedes is left free for your outpost for 60 days; afterwards the enemy settles at −35. *Leave the war* costs 8 goodwill with your side.
- **The Calder Coalition (`src/sim/alliance.js`, Diplomacy window → Coalition & goals):** invite a group (Groups tab, radio panel or the Coalition tab) when it is in radio reach, your team is at its gates, one of your bases is within 8 tiles of one of its bases or a member has a base within 12 tiles of it, its goodwill is 50+ (more for a peaceful group once you have conquered others: 50 + infamy / 2), it has a pact with you and you did a job for it (or the council votes it in: members with relations 10+ toward it, a majority). Qualifying groups also ask to join (a card). Members share every horde within 12 tiles and every hive and hive site within 15 of their bases (and the land within 3) each day, send fighters against raids on your bases within 20 tiles (strength × 0.15 each, up to half your own defence), harry a horde heading for your base once (15% each, 30% at most), and send fighters to your hive assaults within 15 tiles (1 per 10 people, 1–3 each; each keeps one of the brood off your squad, 40% at most); they never raid you or each other and do not go to war with each other. A member leaves below goodwill 20, without a pact, or when it attacks another member. The council meets at the start of every season (days 15, 43, 71, …) and asks for supplies for a member (send: +6 / +3 to the rest; refuse: −4 with all), a job (refuse: −3 with all) or a ruling between two members (side with one: +6 / −8 and they fall out; or a compromise, a Speech check).
- **Alliance victory and goals:** the coalition is its own way to win, next to destroying every hive: it wins when it has at least one member and holds more than half the land (hives standing or not), and keeps that up for a year (the Calder Accord, see *Eras and winning*). Groups outside the coalition no longer block it: conquest counts (break them, claim the land up to their gates, or leave them be); the Goals panel still lists them with what to do about them. Land: your claims (below) plus land tiles within 9 of your hub and of every base you took from a group, 7 of an outpost (8 with walls standing) and of a member's base (`alliance.js controlRadius`; × the square root of the world's area on other sizes: Small 7 / 5, Large 11 / 9, Huge 14 / 11, so it takes about as many bases on any map). On Normal at the start your hub holds about 10% of the land; every group's bases at 7 tiles plus the hub come to about 50–60%, so a coalition of everyone left plus two or three outposts or captured bases gets there. A member's radio carries an invitation: a group within 12 tiles of a member's base is in reach. The first victory ends the run with its screen; after *Keep playing* the other one can still come, once, with its own (both at once: the coalition's is named). The Goals card at the top of the Groups tab (and in full on the Coalition tab) shows both: hives destroyed and left (and being built); land held against 50%, members of groups still standing, and every group outside with what to do about it (invite them now, win them over and why not yet, or defeat them: hostile, their bases and the fewest defenders at one), your infamy, and the bases of yours other groups hold (to retake). The timelapse captions the coalition's victory.
- **Land claims and wells (`src/sim/claims.js`, `orders.js`, `economy.js`, `alliance.js`, `ui/render.js`, `ui/expedition.js`, `main.js`; or `node tools/claimstest.js [seed]`, a few seconds):** a team on a land tile bordering land you hold (a base, the ground right around it or another claim) gets the order **Claim this tile** (team panel, right-click menu, orders card): 2 hours on the spot and 3 scrap carried for the marker post. The tile counts as land the coalition holds (`controlledLand`), toward the 50% the alliance victory needs, so claims chain outward from your bases to the contested ground; the Territory layer draws them in a lighter blue and the hover text says *Claimed by you (day N)*. Not on water, a base, a hive or the ground a group's base holds; another group's claim bordering yours can be claimed over. A claim is lost when a group founds or takes a base on or within 2 tiles of it (the group gets the claim), a hive rises there, or a hostile team or band (a hostile group's party, raiders from outside, a hardcore band) stands on it at the day tick (`claimsDaily`). **Dig a well** (24 hours, 15 scrap, 1 part carried) on a claim of yours next to one of your bases: beside a river or lake 10 water a day, farmland 6, forest 4, hills 2, suburbs and city 2, mountains none; it holds 1,500–4,000 (the dice and the ground), gives half under 20% left and runs dry at 0 (a log line). The base's people haul it into `waterProduction.hauled` (at most one well per 4 people at the base, the best first); the base card's water line lists the wells. `state.claims = { [tile]: { owner, since, well? } }`, plain data in a save; older saves have none.
- **Eras and winning (`src/sim/eras.js`, every number in `ERAS`; `outbreak.js`):** years are the game's calendar years (four seasons of 28 days = 112 days; year 1 is days 1–98, year 5 starts on day 435, year 6 on 547, year 10 on 995, year 15 on 1555). *Early game* (years 1–2, the first winters): nothing below grows yet. *Mid game* (years 3–7, centred on year 5, the long war): from year 3 the threats grow every year to a cap: new roaming, attacking and border hordes +3% a year (at most +30%; the hives multiplying and their growths are the main growth: about 6 standing in year 1, 9 in year 5, 11 from year 10), the aggressive groups' strength +2% a year (better arms; raids, wars, the bases they defend; at most +20%), they recruit up to 45 + 1 a year (at most 55), new gangs from beyond the border arrive with 12–18 + 1 a year people (at most +10), outsider raider bands go past their first-year ceiling of 14 people and power 6.0 by 1 person every four years and +0.05 power a year (at most +3 / +0.5). *Late game* (year 8 on, the reckoning): the growth nears its caps, new gangs keep coming whenever fewer than 2 hostile aggressive groups are left (`ERAS.LATE_MIN_AGG` can raise that), and every coalition member sends two more fighters to your hive and base assaults (the coalition has grown into an army); a coalition that holds over half the land wins the peaceful groups over with time (+0.25 goodwill a day, up to 60: `ERAS.BANDWAGON`) and closes the state to new gangs and raider bands digging in. **Fewer, bigger threats** (`ERAS.RAIDS`, `ERAS.HORDES`): raids on your bases (a group's raid, a war raid, a grudge raid, an outsider band coming for a base, together) come at a pace of about 3 a game year in years 1–2, 5 from year 3, 7 from year 5, 10 from year 8 and 14 from year 10 (not a cap: the next one may start 112 / pace × 0.75–1.25 days after the last began, and only if someone is ready then; in practice about half of that); a group raids a base only if its whole strength reaches 0.8 × the base's defence with everyone at a post (0.95 from year 3, 1.05 from year 5, 1.15 from year 8, 1.25 from year 10) and sizes the raid to 0.6–1.0 × that defence early, up to 0.85–1.15 × from year 10; an outsider band goes for a base only at 0.6–1.05 × its defence by the same years. Hordes a hive sends at your bases come at a pace of 6 a year early, 10 from year 3, 14 from year 5, 18 from year 8, 22 from year 10, 26 from year 13 (in between the hives' hordes go for the groups or roam; wandering and border hordes that stumble on a base are not paced: empty outposts in hive land are overrun often). **The hives late** (`ERAS.BROOD`, `HIVE_CAP`): the brood inside a hive fights 3.5% harder a year from year 4 (at most +30%: `hives.js broodForce`), and from year 8 no new build site is picked while 11 hives stand or are being built. **New gangs and raider bands do not dig in on the coalition's land** (`alliance.js coalitionMask`): holding the land keeps the gangs out. **The Balanced autopilot pushes to win in the late game** (`PERSONALITIES.balanced.lateConquer`): from year 8 it storms any hostile group's base, and any group that will not join (goodwill below zero), at 70% odds. **The outbreak beyond the border** (`outbreak.js`): until it burns out in a year between 9 and 12 (fixed per world from its seed) the state never runs out of hives: when fewer than 2 stand or are being built (1 from year 8), a mass of about 1,100 of the dead gathers on open ground near the border 20–40 days later (8+ tiles from any base) and starts building a hive at once: about four weeks to strike it, as any build site. **Winning:** no victory before year 8 (day 771). The hive victory needs every hive destroyed and none being built after the outbreak beyond the border has burned out, and then the state kept clear for 28 days (a hive or a started site sets it back to zero). The coalition victory (the Calder Accord) needs over half the land with every group standing a member kept for 112 days (each day it holds counts one, a day it fails takes two back; a gang that has just come over the border or a raider band that dug in does not count against it for its first 56 days: break it or win it over), with a coalition at least 12 seasons old. Coalition members also fight beside you when you storm a group's base: each member with a base within 15 tiles that is not on good terms with the defenders keeps 1 per 10 of its people (1–3, +2 in the late game) of the defenders busy, at most 40% of them (`alliance.js assaultAllies`, `conquest.js assaultForce`). The Goals card shows the year and era, both clocks and how far the coalition's age has got; Help → *Eras and winning* says the same in the game. `tools/eratest.js` checks it; `tools/balancesim.js` with a long run (over 730 days, e.g. `node tools/balancesim.js 1666 --runs 50`) adds a year-by-year table.
- **Fights by the numbers, calibrated on the battle map (`combat.js winOdds` / `ODDS`, `breakoff.js BREAKOFF`, `tools/calibrate.js`):** a fight nobody plays on the map (the Auto-resolve button, the autopilot, crews, the headless tools) is as likely to be won and as deadly as the same fight played out by the AI on the tactical map. The odds are a logistic of the power ratio (squad over enemy, × 1.4 for striking first) with the numbers on each side for people (3 against 5 weak raiders is a coin flip), the dead about half as strong per point of power as people, a mixed crowd in between and a little for a hive's brood (the cards, the planner, conflicts, assaults and the autopilot all use it). The blood: each member of a team that loses a fight fought out goes down 80% of the time (92% against the dead), in a won fight 5% + 42% × the chance it had of going the other way (whoever falls in a won fight falls in its last round: nobody breaks off a fight they are winning because of it), and of the rest about half are wounded. Over 1,100 grid fights (`tools/calibrate.js`): the map wins 70% with 1.44 dead a fight, by the numbers 66% with 1.58 (before, 52% and 0.10). The retreat policies still save most of those lives (`tools/retreattest.js`). The autopilot's fight choices count the blood a won fight costs.
- **Conquest (`src/sim/conquest.js`, mission *Assault their base*, right-click a group's base):** a tactical battle against the base's defenders behind their walls (the "baseAssault" map: their wall line with a gate and breaches, barricades behind it; headless and on the autopilot the usual statistical resolver with the walls as a multiplier). Defenders: the group's people shared over its bases (the main base counts double), 60% of them fighting (3–16), each as tough as a rescue's guards (2.6 + arms × 1.6), × (1 + 0.05 a wall point: a fortress or military base 7, a group's main base 4, any other 1). The planner and the card warn hard against a group that is not hostile: attacking costs 30 goodwill at once, ends any pact, and a coalition member you attack leaves (−5 with every other member). Win: the base is your outpost (battered walls, their bunkhouse, half of that base's share of their stores; it reaches 9 tiles of land); one of the team (the weakest fighter, if the team has 3+; the autopilot keeps its squad together) stays to hold it, the rest wait for orders; a base with nobody in it is land you hold: raiders loot it and the dead wreck it, but it is not lost; of the defenders who live 40% get away to their other bases, of those who give up a quarter ask to join you (a recruit card, at most 3) and a fifth are taken prisoner (at most 3), your people held there walk free. Their main base: the leaders fall with it and half of those left scatter. A group down to 3 people breaks up. A group with no base left (or 2 people) is broken (`factions.js collapseFaction`). Fallout: pact partners of the victim −8 goodwill (−15 when you finish it), its friends (relations 28+) −4 (−8), its enemies +2 (+4). Infamy: +2 a base taken from a hostile raider-type group (breaking bandits worries nobody much), +15 from anyone else (+10 more for finishing it); −0.1 a day; above 20 peaceful groups you know lose (infamy − 20) / 200 goodwill a day and want goodwill 50 + infamy / 2 (at most 90) before they join the coalition. A failed assault: the defenders lose 60% of those you killed, a beaten team may lose one to capture.
- **Losing bases, retreat and the hub:** a raid that overruns a base of yours may **raze** it (aggressive gangs, `razeChance` above; the warning card says "<gang> burn what they cannot hold" when it is 15%+): every building and parked vehicle goes, the stock is carried off or burned, the people walk to your nearest base, and the tile is a ruin (a little to scavenge; a new outpost there needs only 5 scrap). Your hub burns 0.4× as often, a base with nobody in it 0.5×. The hub razed is the hub lost (the nearest base becomes the hub, else the exiles). Otherwise a raid that overruns a base of yours (or breaks into an outpost, a third as likely) may keep it (`keepChance` above; the hub × 0.35): it goes on their bases list with its land, half of what is left in it goes into their stores, the raid damage stays on its buildings, and a garrison of 60% of the raid's head count (at least 2) holds it. Your people there walk to your nearest other base as a team, carrying what they can (water and food for 4 days, 15 scrap, ammo, meds; one vehicle; the others are left on the tile, marked). The raid warning card offers *Retreat to <base>* (or *Abandon <base> and take to the road* from your last one): everyone leaves before the raid arrives and the raiders take the base without a fight. A base battle (raiders, outsiders, a horde breaking in, a rescue raid) has a way out on the side away from the attack: pulling out of a group's raid abandons the base to them as above; outsiders loot it as an overrun and move on; the dead tear through it (half the food, water and meds spoiled, the walls battered) and move on, and everyone who got out comes back; a rescue raid frees its people. Raiders from outside the state never keep a base (they are passing through): an overrun is looted and wrecked but stays yours. Lose the hub with another base left and the nearest one becomes the hub (the buildings stay where they are); with no base left your people are one team on the road (exiles) that founds a new base (*Establish base*: a staging camp as hardcore's first hub, a little cheaper: **10 scrap, 1 part, 8 food, 8 water** and 40 person-hours of work, about a day for three; it becomes a shelter, built up into the hub later like hardcore's) or takes one back (a camp still standing then is struck, its stores left as a cache); the run ends only when nobody is left. The holders patch a base up (each broken building 5% a day, wear −1 a day; the garrison grows back 0.2 a day), so it is easiest to take back right after it fell; the tile panel says who holds it since when, what is still broken and the garrison, a card and the Goals card name it to retake, and *Retake the base* on it is the same assault: won, it is yours again with its buildings as they stand and the stock they left. If its holders are broken and nobody takes the tile, it comes back to you empty.
- **Wants and surplus, the market board (`src/sim/trade.js` marketLists, Diplomacy window → Market board):** every group's lists are made again every 7 days from its real stock, kind and state (`demand`: a group at war wants ammo, a sick one meds): *wants* (demand 1.25+: the quantity back to comfortable, at least half a usual lot, and what they pay per unit for a usual lot, before Speech) and *surplus* (demand 0.9 or less with 5+ to spare: the quantity and what they charge; never what their kind refuses). Shown on each group's card in the Groups tab and in its radio panel (with arrows against last week). The market board lists every group you know in radio reach (not hostile) for a good you pick, best to sell to or best to buy from first, with arrows, what they want and have spare, and a shortcut to their radio panel. Shortages hit at random (1% a day per group: a lost harvest, sickness, a fuel fire, a lost ammo store; news on the radio) and the lists are made again at once. Their radio offers and requests and the caravans you ask for use these lists.
- **Autopilot and diplomacy:** the governor accepts jobs it can do (deliveries the hub can spare, fetched by their caravan; scouting and packages within 12 tiles with 4+ people at home, sent as recon trips; Aggressive also culls a small horde), keeps in touch with every group it knows in radio reach (a greeting whenever one is due, a scan of the airwaves once a week) and signs the pact each will sign (non-aggression, then trade), builds a radio station from a dozen people (once it has the parts) and a radio relay at any base where one would link and bring a known group (not hostile) into reach (saved up for when radio reach is what keeps that group out of the coalition; a supply run carries the parts an outpost lacks for it), sends envoys to groups out of reach (two people with the hub's surplus as gifts while goodwill is short, a pact, and the invitation at their gates once they qualify; one per group every 20 days), welcomes groups that ask to join and invites every group that qualifies, stays out of wars on Cautious (Balanced and Aggressive join only beside a much stronger friend against a group that is no friend of yours), and on Aggressive sells ammo over the radio to groups at war (once every 8 days).
- **Hordes (`src/sim/hordes.js`):** each has a job (guard, build, mass, roam, attack, border) and a **score** from its size (size^0.8 / 3.4: about 3 small, 8 medium, 16 large, 33 at 400) that sets how hard it is to hide from and how a fight with it goes (horde cards, culls) and what it adds to a new hive. A horde moves at most **2 tiles a day** (12 hours a tile, slower in deep cold and snow: weather.js `hordeSpeedFactor`; a quarter faster with 10%+ runners, `hivetech.js`), along a land path (rivers at bridges). Hordes on the same errand that meet merge (up to 600; the rest stays a horde of its own), very big wandering ones split now and then, and a split always leaves at least 10 in each part. Wandering hordes rot slowly (1.2% a day) and drift toward people (more often as the year goes on). You see hordes only near your bases (further with a watchtower), with teams in radio range, through a team's report (recon, encounters) or a trader's sightings; where you saw one last stays as a faded "N?" ghost. Cull or lure them away; a horde cut below 5 is gone from the map.
- **Defences against hordes (`src/sim/defences.js`):** a base holds back a horde up to its **hold limit**: 15 + 18 × wall strength (hub 69 / 123 / 177 / 231 for wall tiers 0–3, outposts 33 / 87 / 141 / 195), plus 2 per point of combat power on guard duty (everyone at 0.8 for two days after *Man the defences*), times the defence buildings: **barricades** (8 scrap, 10 h, up to 3: +12% each, full cover on the battle map; one is wrecked in each break-in fight), **spike pits** (6 scrap, 8 h, up to 2, one use: the next horde loses 7% + 4 per pit before the walls, and the pits hurt the dead on the battle map), **trench and stakes** (12 scrap, 30 h: +25%, and the dead that reach it lose their turn), **reinforced gate** (14 scrap + 1 part, 20 h: +20% and two more barricades in battle), **floodlights** (8 scrap + 2 parts, 12 h: burns 2 fuel per attack for +10%, and defenders aim +10 at night, +4 by day). A **Wallbreaker** cuts the limit to 60% (two: 35%) and smashes a defence each attack (a barricade, then the gate, else a wall section 40% of the time). A horde at or under the limit is beaten off at the walls (it falls back 4 tiles and is not drawn back to that base for 10 days); a bigger one **breaks in**: the walls still hold back half the limit and the rest (up to 20 on the map, power 1.5 + their number / (5 × units), up to 6, tougher on a hive's land, plus the Wallbreaker) is fought on the base's battle map by the best 8 there, with the barricades, pits and trench on it. In the browser that is the tactical battle; headless and on autopilot the same battle is played out by the AI on both sides, so the odds match. Lose it and the base is **overrun**: the downed have a better chance at home (40%), everyone else there has a 40% chance of a 20–60 wound, a third of the food, water and meds is spoiled, an outpost is lost; a hub with nobody left alive ends the game. The base panel shows "Holds a horde of up to ~N (M with a Wallbreaker)". When lookouts see a horde heading for a base within 6 tiles you get a **warning card** with the same figure: *Man the defences*, *Burn a decoy fire* (6 fuel; 25–80% by its size, 15% with a Wallbreaker) or hold.
- **One shared clock** for the map and every scavenging run.
- **Seasons and weather** (`src/sim/weather.js`): a year is 4 seasons of 28 days (112 days) and a run starts on day 15 of spring. Every day has one regional weather record (clear, overcast, rain, storm, fog, heat wave, dry spell, snow, blizzard, hard frost) drawn by season from the seed on its own random stream, with warm and cold spells that last a few days, plus ground that carries over: mud after rain and thaw, snow cover that builds up and melts, soil moisture. Averages: spring 13 / 3 °C, summer 26 / 15 °C, autumn 13 / 4 °C, winter 1 / -8 °C. *Regional weather (fronts):* the season, the temperatures and the ground are statewide, but a day's storms, blizzards and fog come as **weather fronts** (`state.weather.fronts`, `FRONT_KINDS`): thunderstorms (radius 5–8 tiles on a Normal map, 0.9–1.5 tiles an hour), rain belts (7–12, 0.7–1.2), blizzards (7–11, 0.6–1.0) and snow squalls (6–10, 0.7–1.2) come in at a map edge (mostly the west; more from the north in winter, the south in summer), head for a point inside the map and cross it over hours to a couple of days (72 hours at most, growing over 3 hours and fading over the last 6); fog banks (6–10) form inland before dawn and burn off in 8–14 hours. A stormy, blizzard or foggy day brings 1–2 of its fronts (45% a second) and the background sky between them is rain, snow or overcast; each season adds its own (spring: rain belts 30% a day, thunderstorms 6%; summer: thunderstorms 14%, rain 12%; autumn: rain 30%, storms 5%, fog 10%; winter: snow squalls 30%, blizzards 4%, fog 5%), rain falling as snow below freezing. Bigger maps have bigger fronts (up to ×1.4). Fronts are rolled a day at a time from their own seeded stream. `weather(state)` / `wxNow` is the weather at the hub (`weatherAt(state, tile)`, `wxAt(state, x, y)` for any tile): a team's pace, sight, ambushes, road injuries, travel events (the Storm card only comes under rain or snow, mostly under a storm or blizzard front), boat crossings, hunting, fishing, foraging, woodcutting and surveys go by the weather where it is, a horde's pace by the weather where it is; the hub's crops, rain water, wells, thirst, exposure and heating by the hub's own day (a front that sat over it 3 hours for a storm or blizzard, 6 for the rest). The top bar shows the hub's weather (and ⛈️ in Nh when a storm or blizzard front will reach it within 12 hours); its tooltip lists the fronts on the map with distance, direction and when they reach the hub ("Thunderstorm 6 tiles west, here by evening (5 h)"). Alerts: a storm or blizzard heading for the hub within 12 hours. *Frozen rivers:* a freezing index (degree-days below 0 °C, minus 3× the warmth above, at most 45) on each day freezes the rivers' northern reaches at 30 and the far south at 45, so they freeze from the north down in deep winter (about 3 weeks a year up north, 2 down south, none in the south in a mild winter) and thaw the other way. Frozen river tiles (drawn as pale ice) can be walked, 1.75 hours a tile, never driven (bikes neither): by your teams, other groups and hordes, so a river-protected base is open until the thaw (a log line and an alert: "River by X frozen: the dead can cross"). Within 5 of the freezing point the ice is thin: a team on foot goes through on 6% of the tiles it enters (3–8 damage, 2 hours lost). When the ice melts, anyone on it gets ashore and every way over it is planned again (`src/sim/rivers.js`). *Travel:* every step's cost is multiplied by the weather of the hour it is taken in: mud slows off-road travel (vehicles about twice as much as walkers), snow cover slows everything (off-road most), storms and blizzards slow everyone a lot (up to ×3) and can hurt someone on the road (1.5% / 2.5% an hour on foot, half in a vehicle), fog and blizzards cut sight by a tile and make it easier to be ambushed, storms bring more road events, and boat mishaps are 1.5–2.5× as likely in wind and storms. The whole year's weather is fixed ahead (rolled day by day from the seed as far as anything asks), and estimates (the planner, panels, queue, the map's estimated position) step through the same hours with the ground and the fronts already on the map when the estimate is made (they keep their course and speed): a front that forms later can still slow the team. The planner's ETA says how many hours the weather adds. *Forecast:* hover the season and weather next to the clock for the effects and the next 3 days (4 with a watchtower, 5 with a radio station), less sure the further out. *Farming hooks:* growth factor (0–1.3) per crop and day, death chances (frost, hard freeze, blizzard, drought, heat, storm) and rot (wet or snow), growing seasons per crop, rain water per collector and a well factor; also cold / heat exposure risk, a horde speed factor in deep cold and a food-need factor and the thirst factor (`thirstFactor`: water a person drinks that day by season and heat; used by the farming, sickness, horde and water systems). The *Weather* map layer washes the land white with snow, dark with rain and warm in heat (one fill, in the cached map) and draws the fronts as faint drifting clouds (a few cached puff images a front, on the moving layer; they glide while the clock runs, and with Map animations off move only when the map draws anyway); the *Weather fronts* layer (off by default; Shift+W, rebindable) shows them strongly with their edge, name and a 6-hour heading arrow, and a tile's tooltip names the front over it and frozen river. With Reduce motion the fronts move an hour at a time; their colours (blue-grey, white, grey) carry no red/green meaning, so the colour-blind palette leaves them as they are. The minimap shows frozen river as pale ice; the sound follows the weather where the camera is (ui/audio.js weatherAt); the Codex has a Weather page for each kind of front and the frozen river, opened the first time one is on the map. The timelapse shows the season by the date and the statewide wash (not the fronts). Season changes and stormy days in the region are logged. The autopilot launches no trip while a storm or blizzard is over the hub, and no trip longer than a day with one forecast in the region in the next two.
- **Autopilot (dev tool):** the dev panel's *Autopilot* checkbox (or P) hands the holdout to an AI governor (`src/sim/autopilot.js`) so you can watch it play itself; turn it off and you are back in control with everything as it stands. It runs from the hourly tick (`autopilotHour`, daily planning at 07:00 in `autopilotDaily`) and only uses the same calls as the UI and what you know of the map (knowledge, markers, known groups). While it is on, battles resolve by the numbers (auto-combat; restored when you switch off, and a battle already open is played out by the AI), scavenging uses the hour-by-hour search, and cards, searches and trades never stop the clock. Every move is in the log with 🤖 (role changes as one line a day). What it does:
  - *Roles* every hour, per base: patients and the worn-out rest (up to a third), farmers for the plots in use (more when food is short of the food planner's target; the plots get their tenders first while the winter stores fall short, but one hand stays free for a queued build; extra plots only while there are hands to tend them), an operator for the preserving kitchen when fresh food would otherwise spoil (stored outside, or the summer and autumn surplus), whoever is left farms too while food is short (base scavenging is a last resort), guards against a spotted raid or a horde at the door (and a standing watch against known raiders or hordes in sight), medics while anyone is hurt, builders while something is queued, operators for stations worth running (ammo, meds, parts, by stock), one crafter while the craft queue has work, the rest scavenge.
  - *Buildings* through `canBuild` / `queueBuild`, saving scrap for what matters: rain catchers and wells when water runs short (cisterns when it overflows), beds when they run short (`nextHousing`: a bunkhouse while building plots are plenty, a barracks once a quarter of them or fewer are free or the bunkhouses are at their max, while a part is left after paying for it, else a bunkhouse; with no plot free a bunkhouse expanded into a barracks), farm plots, food storage before it is needed (root cellars, then a cold store or a storehouse, as soon as the fresh food plus what the fields still bring would fill the room; urgent once food is stored outside), a drying rack and a preserving kitchen for the summer surplus, a greenhouse before winter (saved for when the winter stores fall short), walls when threatened (and when a horde in sight would break in), then barricades, spike pits, a trench, the reinforced gate and floodlights once hordes are a known danger, the hospital tier by tier, watchtower, med station, storehouse, machine shop, radio, garage; outposts get a farm plot, beds and walls (and a root cellar once four or more live there and the food fills most of the pantry). *Growing outwards* (`hubCramped`): when the hub has outgrown its plots (no building plot free, nothing more it can build or expand for beds, and its people within two of its beds) it founds one outpost more than its style would once no outpost it founded has a bed free, its outposts build their beds ahead (three free) with the scrap the supply runs bring, and up to three people at a time (farmers first, the medics, builders and station hands last) move to an outpost it founded (not a base taken from a group: those stand out in the wild) with beds free, summer water for them and a supply line of strength 0.5 or better while the hub has 10 days of food a head (`movers`; a supply run that disbands there with 4 food a head). *Crafting*: weapons for anyone without a usable one (crossbow, saw-blade machete, spiked bat, pipe spear), armor, dressings when meds are short, a water filter, a crowbar for scavengers, backpacks, pads; armory gear goes to whoever it helps most. *Medicine*: infected and severe wounds treated with base meds right away, others below 50 when stocks allow.
  - *Food and the winter* (the food planner): the governor plans the year with `winterOutlook` (farming.js): day by day from today to the first spring harvest after the coming winter (`hungryGap`: winter starts on day 71 of each year, the first spring harvest is the quickest crop sown on the first day of spring, about four weeks in), everyone at the hub, the outposts and the teams out eats (the usual food need for the time of year, more in the cold), fresh food first, spoiling by the storage there is at the usual temperature; the crops in the ground come in when they should ripen (times the chance they survive) and every plot then gets the crops the farmers would sow on it (`plannedCrop`, at 85%); seed for the spring and a 15% margin for bad luck are kept back. From mid-summer to the first spring harvest its *food target* (days of food the hub should hold) is that need less what the fields and outposts bring, so a short winter plan means more farmers, a higher food need for trips, trades and radio deals, storage and preserving built in time, and **no newcomers** (strangers, refugees, lone survivors on the road) while the plan with them would fall short. Every week in autumn and on the first day of winter it logs a 🤖 *Winter stores* line: food in store (fresh, preserved, cellar room, outside), what the fields should still bring, what the people need to reach the first spring harvest (eating, spoilage, seed, margin) and the verdict.
  - *Trips* (one a day at most, style sets how many teams, how often, how far, how many stay home and how healthy the crew must be): scavenge the best known site for what the hub needs (loot left as it last knew it, danger, infection, hostile land, distance; when fewer than 3 sites within its range are believed to hold 30%+ loot it looks 8 tiles farther out and allows trips 1.6× as long, logged as "the sites nearer home are picked over"), check spotted caches, camps and wrecked cars, recover the dead's gear or a lost vehicle (only with the fuel to start it), a trade visit (sells surplus, buys what is short, proposes pacts; it asks only for what the team can carry home, and every amount is priced as a whole, the way the trade window totals it), a rescue for captives, or found an outpost when the population allows. Teams take the truck when it can make the round trip; searches stop at the style's Heat or hour limit, when someone is hurt, the cargo is full or the site is dry. Done cards: treat the badly hurt with carried meds before a long way home, sometimes search one more site nearby, else go home. Only one search runs at a time: a second team arriving holds at the site until it is free.
  - *Supply runs* to outposts that are short of hands (a volunteer disbands there), to take people from a hub that has outgrown its plots (see *Buildings*), or of scrap for a building (crew sized to the load).
  - *Cards*: conflicts are scored from the odds they show (talk, toll, pact, run, hide, fight, ambush, slip away, steal; style weighs fights and raids), travel events by fixed rules (detour, shelter, spare parts, mark hives on the map, take in loners if the food lasts), raid warnings judged against defense on alert (prepare, pay tribute, or fight in person), horde warnings (a decoy fire when the defences would not hold and no Wallbreaker leads it, else man the defences), calls for help, newcomers (every recruit card: strangers, refugees, loners, people met on the road, a lost teenager, a returning deserter) only with food and beds for them (built, queued or the next bunkhouse or barracks the hub can still get; Aggressive allows some crowding; at an outpost, or sent to one, its own beds and water and the hub's food also do), else only the best one, else sent to a base with free beds, else supplies and a handshake when the stores are full, else turned away; hub events and warnings before desertion by a score on each choice (`ai`); the Overcrowded card (moveout.js) *Leave it for now* (its own outposts and bunkhouses deal with crowding; it raises none itself), the *Enter …?* card yes for a team sent there to move in or when the base has a bed for each of them, else no; a new leader by Leadership; anything else falls back to simtest's rule ("Return home", else the first choice).
  - *Reserves* (Cautious; `reserveStatus(state, base)` → per supply `{ have, target, days, short, low, why }`): a target for every supply that the hub should not drop below, from what you can see. Food is the food planner's (fresh + preserved: `foodTarget` and the winter outlook); water 7 days of drinking (10 in summer, at most 90% of the tanks; from three weeks before summer the summer plan's heat-wave stock if that is more; short while the wells fall behind on a dry day or under 15 days are left); meds a treatment for everyone sick now (+1 per infected wound) plus a third of the people + 2 (a quarter more in autumn and winter); ammo 2–4 break-in battles (about 6 a defender, up to 8 defenders, plus the 3 a head every horde attack burns; one battle more for each threat: a horde in sight, one the defences would not hold, a horde warning, a hive or hive build site known within 12 tiles, two or more attacks so far); fuel a decoy fire, the floodlights and the truck's next trip; scrap 20 + 1.5 a head + 10 per threat level (up to 70) for repairs and defences; parts 3 (+2 with a vehicle). Below target Cautious acts at once, and safely: a trip every 2 days instead of 4, up to twice as far (trade visits too) with a crew of three and a quarter more food and water, aimed at sites whose loot has what is short (low danger first, never risk above 0.8), two more search hours under the same Heat limit, and a recon first when no known site has it (a look around, then the search if it is worth it); a radio station once there are parts for it, a daily scan of the airwaves and one radio deal a day for the supply furthest below target, paid with what is well above its own target (parts, surplus food, ammo); trade visits that also sell surplus parts and food; the ammo bench and the med station staffed before the plots take every hand (from the scrap there is, down to a day's worth when the stock is low), a med station as soon as meds run short, dressings and a water filter; nothing optional built or crafted from scrap, meds, parts or fuel below target, and trips leave the ammo reserve at home. Risk it cannot afford: no trip while a horde in sight would break in, 5 people stay home while one is in sight or the defences are manned, standing guards before extra farmers, no trip of more than half a day with a storm or blizzard forecast while it is out, a second wall tier once hordes are a known danger. The dev panel's 🤖 line says what is short; hover it for the whole table with the reason for each target.
  - *Styles*: **Cautious** (one stealthy team close to home, short searches, more guards, pays tribute, avoids fights; keeps a reserve of every supply and goes for more as soon as one runs short: see *Reserves*), **Balanced** (default), **Aggressive** (more and longer trips, loud searches, raids hostile land, ambushes camps and robbers, takes in more newcomers, expands fast).
  - *The war on the hives* (every style, from day 30 Aggressive / 90 Balanced / 150 Cautious): a squad of six of the best fighters (health 80+, 12 ammo each) goes for a known hive when it can beat the brood it expects there: the squad's storm value (per fighter: hit chance × damage of the gun they can use, as the battle plays it, more with armor, less when hurt; about 2 for a shotgun and a vest at Shooting 6, 1 for a pistol at Shooting 4) over the brood (`hives.js broodForce` from the guard last seen at the mound, 100 if never seen up close, less one for every fighter coalition members and job givers send: `alliance.js hiveAllies`) must reach the style's margin (1.05 / 1.2 / 1.35: at 1.3 a storm costs about one fighter in three, at 1.1 about one, at 0.9 two or three). Fires around the mound (6 fuel each, up to 3, never the fuel for the drive home) thin the guard first when that is what it takes; the team storms on its next orders. Targets: hives near your bases, then those a group asked you to storm (the governor takes a hive job when it would storm that hive anyway), then the nearest. In the endgame (a coalition member, at most 3 hives left, the land short) it founds up to 3 more outposts where they add the most uncovered land.
  - *Conquest* (`autopilot.js conquestCandidate`): a squad of five or six of the best fighters (health 75+) storms a base when its combat power against the defenders behind their walls gives the style's odds (Aggressive 65%, Balanced 75%, Cautious 80%, the odds the card shows), at most one assault or hive squad out and 12 days between assaults: every style takes back a base of yours a group holds (10% lower odds accepted); Aggressive goes for any hostile group from day 60; Balanced for hostile groups that raided it in the last 120 days, and for any hostile group once every other group still standing is in the coalition or the coalition holds 35% of the land (from day 150); Cautious only then (from day 240). It sticks with one group until it breaks (its main base first, then the group it went for last, the one with the fewest bases). The old hub in their hands: whoever is fit (health 50+, at least 3, two stay home) goes at once at 35%+ odds. With no base left it takes one back when the odds are fair (45%+, 60%+ once a staging camp is 30% built; any odds from 20% while it lacks the camp's scrap), else it runs the staging camp as hardcore's road does (roadAct; exiles keep what they carried out of their base). On the raid warning it retreats from an outpost that would be overrun (defence below 0.9× the raid for Cautious, 0.8× Balanced, 0.6× Aggressive) when the tribute is not worth it, from the hub only below 0.25× with another base to go to, never from its last base.
  - *Water through the summer* (`waterPlan`): from three weeks before summer (and all year for newcomers) the governor plans by the summer outlook for the people at the base, the teams out and two more: wells, rain catchers and cisterns go up before summer (saved for), water filters (ahead of anything else in the craft queue, kept up as they clog; a clogged one is re-packed for 1 scrap before a new one is made for 4) close the heat-wave gap or today's dry-day gap (up to 18), and a summer stock (a heat-wave stretch, at most 90% of the tanks) becomes the water reserve and a trip need. Scrap and parts are held back for water: a broken well or the hub's pump is repaired before any other repair (`breakdowns.js`: water fixtures first, nothing else takes the materials while one waits), and builds and crafts leave the scrap for it and for two filters. No newcomers the summer water could not carry (an average summer day and a heat-wave stretch that fits the tanks, filters at three quarters); water carried to an outpost about to run dry or short of its summer stock; water runs while the hub's water is tight and a river or lake shore is within 16 hours on foot (two to four people, Fetch water, at most a Heavy load each, one run at a time); while water is tight (under 8 days left, or summer near and under half the summer stock) no water goes into the still or the preserving kitchen and no guests are sheltered.
- **Saving and loading** (`src/sim/save.js`, storage in `src/ui/saves.js`, the menu in `src/ui/settings.js`): ⚙ Menu in the top bar (or Esc with nothing open, or ⚙ Menu in a battle's panel). A save is the whole world, written as a graph: plain objects and lists as JSON; Maps, Sets, typed arrays (the timelapse frames, base64), the seeded generators (their exact position) and the hex grid as tagged objects; an object reachable from two places (an item worn by a survivor and listed at a death site, the chronicle's shared frame lists, a battle's supply pools that are the team's own supplies, the battle's rng) is written once and referenced after, so the links survive; the chronicle's WeakMaps keep the entries for the hordes and teams still in the world; undefined, NaN, Infinity and −0 keep their values; the id counter (`world.js` `newId`) is saved, so a loaded world hands out the same ids and plays on exactly as the original (`tools/savetest.js`). Battles, raids, the deploy step, searches and trade windows are saved as they are, mid-turn included. **An open event card cannot be saved** (its choices are code, closures over the moment the card was made): Save is disabled with the card's name until it is answered, and the autosave waits for that too. Storage: up to 12 named saves plus the autosave in the browser's storage (`localStorage`, one key a slot plus an index with the day, hour, hub, survivors, seed and a small picture of the map), gzip-compressed through the browser's `CompressionStream` and packed 15 bits a character (a 365-day Normal world: about 3 MB of JSON, about 490 KB stored (250,000 characters; the diary and the timelapse recording are the biggest parts)); without `CompressionStream` the JSON is stored as it is. *Export* and *Save to file* download a `.bsave` file (the save's JSON); *Load from file* adds one to the list. The autosave runs at dawn every day (or every 2, 3, 5 or 7 days), when a hive assault starts (the battle is saved before the first shot) and when the run ends (that save leaves any open card out); at dev fast-forward speeds at most once a minute. *Save and exit* saves to the game's slot and goes back to the new-run screen; loading pauses the game. Saves carry a format version (`SAVE_VERSION`): a save from a newer version, or with no version, is refused with a reason; older versions are upgraded by the migrations in `save.js` (none yet: this is format 1). **Rotating autosaves** (`src/ui/saves.js` writeAutosave): each campaign keeps its last 3 autosaves (`auto-<campaign>-1…3`, the oldest written over), each named by a one-line summary (`src/sim/savesummary.js`: "Day 143 · 31 people · winter · at war with Iron Jackals": the day, people alive, the season, then the run's end, a war you joined, a hostile group or a Hardcore band on the road); manual saves show the same line under their name. A campaign is a run's own id (given as it begins); saves from before that are grouped by their seed. The menu's save list is grouped by campaign (`src/ui/savelist.js`: a head line with the hub, mode, seed and count; the game being played first) and warns when the saves fill 70% of the browser's storage; a full storage makes the autosave clear the slot it writes over, then the campaign's older autosaves (its newest always stays). Sizes (seed 42, Balanced autopilot): a Normal map's save at day 365 is about 3 MB of JSON and 250,000 stored characters (gzip, 15 bits a character), at day 730 about 5.2 MB and 430,000; a Huge map's at day 365 about 4.5 MB and 360,000 (so about 650,000 at two years). Browser storage holds about 5 million characters a site, so three two-year autosaves take a quarter of it on a Normal map and two fifths on a Huge one. **The error screen** (`src/ui/crash.js`): an exception nobody caught (the frame loop: the clock, the autopilot, drawing; a click, key or timer; a promise) stops the clock and writes the world to the crash save (its own slot, `crash`, open cards left out; never a manual save, the autosaves or an Ironman slot), then a screen says what happened: *Copy report* (the error and its stack, the time, day, seed, map, mode, Ironman, autopilot, speed, the build's commit (`build.json`: the dev server and the desktop app run from source answer it from git, `npm run dist` writes it), the browser, the crash save and the last 20 log lines), *Try to keep playing* (paused), *Load the crash save*, *Back to title*. While it is up no frames run and further errors are only counted; a crash within 5 s of Try to keep playing shows it again with a warning and keeps the first crash save. Ironman: the crash copy sits beside the game's slot (which keeps its last save, and is not written as the page closes while the screen is up) and is removed once the game saves itself again. `window.game.crashTest()` opens it from the console.
- **Timelapse:** the world is recorded from day 1 (`src/sim/chronicle.js`): one frame a day (every 6 hours for the first 3 days, plus a frame a few hours after a death or a base gained or lost) with tile owners and infection (stored as changes against the previous frame, a full keyframe every 60 frames), your bases, the groups' bases, hordes (with their Wallbreakers), hives and hive build sites, parties, your population and hub stocks, and captions: your log's deaths, raids, horde attacks, bases founded and lost, plus what the fog hid (groups taking or losing sites, sieges and how they ended, hordes striking a group, big hordes, hives rising and destroyed, collapses). About 0.25 ms per frame and roughly 300 KB for a year. When every survivor is dead (or every hive is destroyed) the game-over or victory screen offers *Watch the timelapse*; the dev panel has *Timelapse so far* (the game pauses, Close returns to it).
